# Feature Implementation Plan: sbx restricted mode, mcp-sandbox (stdio script runner), mcp-skill (read-only skill loader/runner), mcp-proxy per-child `relist`

> **SHIPPED, AND NOT MAINTAINED. Read this as a record, not as instructions.**
> The plan was executed in `7b603d2` (2026-10-11); the decisions are recorded in
> [[0031-sbx-restricted-mode]] and [[0032-a-runner-behind-a-proxy]], and the living WHAT/HOW
> is the sandbox-run SKILL.md, the [[mcp-sandbox]], [[mcp-skill]] and [[mcp-proxy]] component
> pages and the module docstrings. Where the implementation deviated from this text (the four
> security-review rounds added refusals and caps this plan does not list), the code and the two
> ADRs are right and this page is not. Its path:line anchors are left as they were written.

> spelling, proxy `--max-body-bytes` anchor, the deferred list). Every risk-first ordering rule holds:
> each milestone opens with the escape rows it must close, run RED before the implementation.

## 0. User decisions (final)

These are final and are not re-opened by any step below.

1. **Runner** `Scripts/mcp-sandbox.py` = stdio fleet server, tool `sandbox_call` (`run_script`, `capabilities`); HTTP/token/sessions come from a dedicated `mcp-proxy --http` instance; global concurrency cap.
2. **Skill server** `Scripts/mcp-skill.py` = stdio, tool `skill_call` (`load`, `run`, `list`, `validate`), read-only, the runner's only client via a stdlib Streamable HTTP client.
3. **Skill dir contract with ai-soul**: `--household-root` / `--private-root`; `skills.json` per root (atomic, the only enumeration source); name `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]{0,63}$`; dir one component, real dir; `SKILL.md` frontmatter exactly `---` / `name:` / `description:` raw remainder / `---` (not YAML), description one line <= 120 code points; `scripts/` and `refs/` leaves; `O_NOFOLLOW` per component, refuse `nlink > 1`; runnable = index-listed scripts only, interpreter from the index; every entry has `"scripts"` (maybe `[]`).
4. **Caller identity from deployment**: one proxy per ai-soul uid, the skill child gets that user's `--private-root`; household shared read-only; same OS uid; isolation by configuration (household threat model = accidents).
5. **Private shadows household** with a visible `[private]` mark; household scripts may run; private only with `--allow-private-scripts`.
6. **Index rendered into `skill_call`'s description** at `tools/list` time, names as an enum; ai-soul enforces a per-tier budget of 1800 UTF-16 code units at approve time; the loader's single ceiling = the whole rendered description <= 4096 UTF-16 code units (ai-soul's chat cuts at JS `.length` = `4 * 1024`, `CHAT_TOOL_DESCRIPTION_MAX_CHARS`, `/Users/zoltan.ponekker/Code/ai-soul/src/adapters/chat/tool-broker.ts:69`); measure `len(s.encode("utf-16-le")) // 2`; over = refused, never cut.
7. **Runner returns output raw**; mcp-skill sanitizes (bidi, zero-width, tags, C0/C1 except `\n\t`) and frames bodies as quoted reference material.
8. **mcp-proxy opt-in per-child `"relist": true`**; frozen tool-name set; ADR 0027 addendum.
9. **sbx gets new flags** (restricted reads + `--read`, clean env + `--env`, wall-clock timeout with group kill, rlimits); the gate auto-allows them when narrowing; `--net` always prompts.
10. **macOS read set computed precisely at runner startup** by a stdlib Mach-O reader, passed as `--read`; parse failure = refuse to start.
11. **NPROC = the uid's current process count + 64**; a fork bomb is bounded by timeout + group kill (declared starvation limit); a dedicated sandbox uid -> roadmap.
12. **Limits**: timeout 30 s (max 120 s), CPU = timeout + 1, AS budget 1 GiB (macOS: measured baseline VSZ + budget because XNU refuses `setrlimit` below the current VSZ — measured 2026-10-10: python3 ~32.5 GiB, node ~33.2 GiB, enforced cleanly once settable; Linux absolute), FSIZE 16 MiB, NOFILE 256, CORE 0, stdout/stderr 64 KiB each, files <= 64 / 4 MiB, outFiles <= 16 / 1 MiB, stdin <= 1 MiB, concurrency 2 (busy immediately), runner proxy body cap 8 MiB; a refused requested `setrlimit` = fail closed.
13. **Linux branch** (target 192.168.2.245 = Ubuntu 22.04 LTS) built now, verified offline; no probing of that host; `liveVerified: false`.

## 1. Requirements Summary

### Functional Requirements

**sbx (`ClaudeCode/skills/sandbox-run/scripts/sbx`)**
- [FR-1] `--restrict-reads`: replace the read-anywhere baseline (`(allow file-read* (subpath "/"))` sbx:201; `--ro-bind / /` sbx:327) with: `/` file-read-data literal; file-read-metadata LITERALS on `/` and every proper ancestor of every allowed path (never global metadata); `--read PATH` (repeatable) entries; reads on the writable scopes; `/dev/null` read+write literal. Linux: ro-bind `/usr`, `/lib`, `/lib64`, `/lib32`, `/bin`, `/sbin` (`--symlink` when a usr-merge symlink), selected `/etc` files, plus `--read` paths. In restricted mode the `~/.claude` carve-outs (`carveout_paths` sbx:663) are NOT emitted (they are read grants). On Linux the `shadow_write_denies` re-binds (`--ro-bind path path`, sbx:396-397; targets from `shadow_write_denies()`, body sbx:750-769) are ALSO not emitted in restricted mode for any target outside every write scope: on an empty root such a `--ro-bind` is a READ grant of the repo/plugin trees (C-H4 = S-H1); a target inside a write scope keeps its re-bind (write protection, no new read). The Seatbelt shadow rules stay (they are write DENIES and grant nothing). The restricted Seatbelt branch emits the `/dev/null` read+write literal ITSELF (bash background jobs and node's `uv_loop_init` fail without it, `item-3a-g.md` 3c, `item-3h-j.md` 3h) and never relies on the legacy `/dev/null` line (sbx:215, `--ro` fix `4d3640c`) being inherited (M0:5). It also denies `file-write-create` beyond 100 directory levels under every write scope (Seatbelt only; M0:9, see Step 3).
- [FR-2] `--read PATH` keeps the path AS SPELLED: absolute, `os.path.normpath`, injection floor, must `lexists`; NEVER `realpath`'d. A spelled symlink becomes a `(literal ...)` rule (bwrap: `--symlink <readlink> <spelling>`), a directory a `(subpath ...)`, a file a `(literal ...)`. Each symlink hop of a chain is therefore its own rule; the caller (mcp-sandbox) passes every hop and the final target explicitly.
- [FR-3] `--exec PATH` (repeatable, restricted mode only): `process-exec` is filtered to the target `argv[0]` spelling plus the `--exec` entries (literal for a file, subpath for a directory). Restricted mode never emits the unfiltered `(allow process-exec)`. **Measured (M0:3, `item-3a-g.md` 3g)**: the filter matches the REALPATH of what is exec'd; a symlink spelling literal is neither needed nor sufficient (spelling only -> `sandbox-exec` exit 71). sbx stays dumb (KD-14: it never realpaths), so the argv0 spelling literal it emits is harmless but inert; the CALLER passes every realpath it needs as `--exec`.
- [FR-4] `--clean-env` + `--env KEY=VALUE` (repeatable): the target's environment is EXACTLY the given set; nothing inherited; `LD_*`/`DYLD_*` keys refused; duplicate keys refused; target `argv[0]` must be absolute (and, on darwin, contain no `=`, because it follows the `/usr/bin/env -i K=V ...` trampoline, KD-5). The unsandboxed engine itself always gets an EMPTY environment on both platforms.
- [FR-5] `--timeout SECONDS`: supervised fork mode; the child `setsid()`s and execs the engine; the parent kills the whole process group on expiry (macOS) / kills the bwrap pid so the PID namespace is torn down (Linux, `--die-with-parent`); exit 124 + one stderr marker line.
- [FR-6] `--status-fd FD` (requires `--timeout`): JSON-lines status protocol — one `spawned` event carrying the target `pid` AND `pgid` (so a supervisor above sbx can kill the group even if sbx itself dies), then exactly one terminal `exit` event with `outcome` in `exited|signaled|timeout|refused`; the fd is never inherited by the target.
- [FR-7] `--rlimit-as BYTES`, `--rlimit-cpu SECONDS`, `--rlimit-nproc N|+N`, `--rlimit-fsize BYTES`, `--rlimit-nofile N`, `--rlimit-core BYTES`: set soft AND hard; STRICT: a refused setrlimit for a REQUESTED limit is exit 3, nothing runs. `+N` = the real uid's current process count + N, counted by sbx immediately before the fork. Unrequested limits keep today's best-effort defaults (`_apply_rlimits` sbx:1138).
- [FR-8] `--dry-run` prints every new plan field (env KEYS only, never values); module stays import-side-effect-free.
- [FR-9] `sbx-gate.py` recognises every new flag (space and equals forms); auto-allows a new flag only when it NARROWS relative to today's defaults; an rlimit value ABOVE sbx's current default prompts; `--net` still always prompts; `--status-fd` always prompts (R2:S-L1: it makes the unsandboxed sbx parent write JSON into an arbitrary fd inherited from the Bash tool — not narrowing; mcp-sandbox spawns sbx directly, never through the gate); a parity row pins the gate's flag table to sbx's parser.

**mcp-sandbox (`Scripts/mcp-sandbox.py`, new)**
- [FR-10] Stdio fleet MCP server, one tool `sandbox_call`, functions `run_script` and `capabilities` (decision 1). No HTTP code.
- [FR-11] `run_script` input exactly the spec's (`files`, `entry`, `interpreter`, `args`, `stdin`, `timeoutMs`), strict, unknown members refused; output exactly the spec's JSON object as one text block AND `structuredContent`.
- [FR-12] Every run goes through `sbx --restrict-reads --clean-env --timeout --status-fd --rlimit-*` with a fresh 0700 workdir as the only writable scope, deleted after every outcome (a workdir the remover cannot delete within its bounds is moved to `<work_root>/quarantine/` and retried later; the runner keeps serving until more than 16 are quarantined, KD-18, R2:S-H1).
- [FR-13] Startup computes the per-interpreter read set on macOS with a stdlib Mach-O reader (decision 10), measures the per-interpreter baseline VSZ for RLIMIT_AS (decision 12), self-tests every interpreter under production limits; any failure = refuse to start (exit 1).
- [FR-14] Interpreter plans SELF-REFRESH: each plan carries a signature (`st_ino`, `st_mtime_ns`) of every file in its closure (every symlink hop, every Mach-O image, every keg root); each run re-checks it and on change recomputes the plan (closure, VSZ, self-test) under a lock. A failed refresh makes that interpreter refuse runs (`sandbox_unavailable`, fail closed) and `capabilities` reports it. No manual step after `brew upgrade`.
- [FR-15] Global concurrency cap (default 2); excess refused immediately with error code `busy`; no queue. The slot is acquired (non-blocking) BEFORE the plan signature check, so a plan refresh (VSZ + version + self-test spawns) runs inside, and counts against, the cap (C-M6).
- [FR-15a] The work root is single-owner: an exclusive `fcntl.flock` on `<work_root>/.lock` is taken at startup and held for the process lifetime; a held lock refuses start (C-M1).
- [FR-16] `capabilities` reports interpreters (path, realpath, version, plan state), effective limits (incl. per-interpreter AS limit and effective budget), backend (platform, engine, seccomp, `liveVerified`), and the computed read set.

**mcp-proxy (`Scripts/mcp-proxy.py`)**
- [FR-17] Opt-in per-child config key `"relist": true`: on every upstream `tools/list` that child's tools are re-listed (coalesced, min-interval bounded); a changed tool NAME SET disables that child (as the restart path does, mcp-proxy.py:1478-1485); only descriptions/schemas refresh. `ChildSlot.stop()` (mcp-proxy.py:1523-1547) cancels and awaits an in-flight relist task (C-L7).

**mcp-skill (`Scripts/mcp-skill.py`, new)**
- [FR-18] Stdio fleet MCP server, one tool `skill_call`, functions `load`, `run`, `list`, `validate`; READ-ONLY.
- [FR-19] Tiers from `--household-root` / `--private-root`; each root's `skills.json` is the only enumeration source (never lists the root); strict schema; private shadows household; `[private]` marker.
- [FR-20] The index is rendered into `skill_call`'s description at every `tools/list` (stat-keyed cache), skill names as an enum; whole description <= `--max-description` (default 4096, range 512..4096 — never above ai-soul's 4096 cut, decision 6; R2:M5) UTF-16 code units, preamble <= 300 code points; over the ceiling = a clear error naming both tier sums in UTF-16 units (R2:M4), never cut; `list`/`validate` stay usable.
- [FR-21] File hygiene: component-by-component `O_NOFOLLOW`, refuse `st_nlink > 1`, non-regular, `..`, absolute; script/ref files are leaves directly under `scripts/` or `refs/` matching `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9._-]{0,63}$`.
- [FR-22] `run`: only index-listed scripts, interpreter from the index, private scripts only with `--allow-private-scripts`; sends the `scripts/` + `refs/` leaves (<= 64 files, <= 4 MiB) to the runner through a stdlib Streamable HTTP MCP client; refuses BEFORE sending when the serialized request exceeds `--runner-max-body` (default 8388608, the runner proxy's body cap); DELETEs its runner session on shutdown (best effort); sanitizes stdout/stderr/outFiles before the model sees them.
- [FR-23] Bodies and outputs are framed as quoted reference material (nonce-delimited) and sanitized with a declared hand copy of `_web_clean` (keep `\t\n`).

### Non-Functional Requirements
- [NFR-1] Fail closed everywhere: backend missing, profile build failure, Mach-O parse failure, plan refresh failure, setrlimit refusal of a requested limit, workdir/root permission anomalies, sbx refusal => refuse; never run unconfined, never "best effort" on a requested limit.
- [NFR-2] Pure Python 3.9 + stdlib only (ADR 0024); sbx stays import-side-effect-free (sbx:23-28).
- [NFR-3] Server memory stays flat under hostile output (capped while reading; discard after cap).
- [NFR-4] Logging metadata only by default (spec "Logging"); wire debug logging structure-only (ADR 0011).
- [NFR-5] A handler failure reaches the caller with `isError` set (ADR 0010); alias/param collisions refused (ADR 0015); near-miss pointer on unknown function/param (ADR 0030).
- [NFR-6] Concurrency per ADR 0008: one task per request, reader thread nothing can take; `capabilities` answers while two runs are in flight.
- [NFR-7] Linux branch built now and verified offline (golden argv); Linux-only live rows INFO on macOS; `liveVerified: false` for Linux until run on target (decision 13).
- [NFR-8] Every shared-looking piece has exactly one declared home (ADR 0014/0025): generated, declared hand copy in `HAND_COPY_REASONS`, or host-local with a stated reason.
- [NFR-9] No manual upkeep on Homebrew drift: no version string typed anywhere; read sets, kegs and VSZ baselines are measured at runtime and refreshed on signature change.

### Success Criteria
- [SC-1] The spec's acceptance escape suite passes live on macOS (each case contained, reported as a normal result or a clean refusal): `~/.ssh` + store-root reads, write outside, TCP, unix socket, fork bomb, infinite loop (group killed), 10 GiB stdout (capped, server memory flat), huge allocation, `..`/absolute paths in `files`, symlink in `out/`, env canary, workdir deleted after every outcome, concurrent-limit refusal — plus the risk rows E19-E34 (R3:L3).
- [SC-2] All fleet gates green: `forge_call test` over the whole `tests/run.py` fleet incl. the new suites with their typed counts, the 7 fleet tables, the smoke harness.
- [SC-3] `python3 Scripts/amalgamate.py --check` clean (both new hosts carry generated regions); the hand-copy census shows mcp-skill's two sanitizer names as DECLARED, not `[UNDECLARED]`.
- [SC-4] Every new test group was observed RED before its implementation (red counts recorded in the ADRs).
- [SC-5] Proxy relist rows (group M) green; the existing 112 proxy cases unchanged; `sbx_gate` stays at 105.

### Assumptions
- A1. Two new ADRs: 0031 "sbx restricted mode" (amends 0005), 0032 "a runner behind a proxy, a skill server as its only client" (declares: runner behind a proxy, an MCP HTTP client inside a server, a third sanitizer copy as a declared hand copy). ADRs 0027 (relist) and 0005 (amended-by 0031) each get one dated addendum; ADR 0008 gets TWO, one per new server, each written in the step that adds that server's `tests/test_read_loop.py` FLEET row (Steps 7 and 16, R3:I2); all written only by `addendum.py` (accepted ADRs are append-only, P25). The last ADR today is `docs/adr/0030-point-at-the-near-miss-never-resolve-it.md`.
- A2. Error shape: refusals are `isError: true` tool results whose `structuredContent` is `{"error": {"code": <symbolic>, "message": ...}}` (ADR 0010), NOT JSON-RPC errors (see "Contract with ai-soul").
- A3. `run` lists files only inside ONE skill's `scripts/` and `refs/` (fd-relative, bounded); decision 3's "never lists dirs" applies to the tier ROOT.
- A4. The runner's proxy reads its token file once at start (`_http_token_file` mcp-proxy.py:709); rotation = rewrite + proxy restart. mcp-skill re-reads the runner token file at every `run`.
- A5. Same OS uid for ai-soul, the proxies, the runner and the skill server (decision 4).
- A6. Default work root on macOS: `<realpath(tempfile.gettempdir())>/mcp-sandbox-<uid>`; Linux: `~/.cache/mcp-sandbox/work` (NOT `/tmp`: avoids the sbx socket-dir re-bind path and shared-`/tmp` races).

### Out of Scope
- The skill-proposal Claude Code skill (ai-soul's `skill_propose` schema now received, `.claude/tmp/r0020/skill-propose-schema.md`, R4:CONTRACT) -> roadmap.
- Deploying to `~/mcp`, editing the live proxy configs, generating live tokens (Zoltán does/approves).
- Touching 192.168.2.245 in any way (no probing).
- Network access for scripts, package installation, persistent state, `--restrict-reads` combined with `--net` (refused in v1).
- A canonical source for the sanitizer or for the MCP HTTP client; a dedicated sandbox OS user; a disk quota; an RSS watchdog; a macOS orphan sweep -> roadmap (see §5 "Deferred to roadmap").
- The uncommitted user hunks in the tree (bitbucket skill, `.claude/settings.json`, `ClaudeCode/CLAUDE.md`, `hooks/post-edit-lint.sh`; the sbx `/dev/null` hunk is no longer pending — committed as `4d3640c`, M0:15). See Preconditions.

## 2. Architecture Analysis

### Affected Subsystems
- **sbx helper** — new flags, new `Scope` fields, two new builder branches, a supervised fork path — `ClaudeCode/skills/sandbox-run/scripts/sbx` (`Scope` :56, `_seatbelt_argv` :138, `_bwrap_argv` :269, `_build_parser` :524, `_format_plan` :818, `_apply_rlimits` :1138, `main` :1199).
- **sbx PreToolUse gate** — new flag grammar — `ClaudeCode/hooks/sbx-gate.py` (`is_clean_sbx` :110, flag loop :139-191).
- **MCP fleet** — two new single-file stdio servers with generated regions — `Scripts/mcp-sandbox.py`, `Scripts/mcp-skill.py` (new); shape from `Scripts/MCP_SKELETON.md`; subprocess template `Scripts/mcp-inspect.py` (`_Reclaim` :384, `_signal_group` :432, `_run_child` :458).
- **Generation machinery** — two `HAND_COPY_REASONS` rows, no new source — `Scripts/amalgamate.py:152-185`.
- **mcp-proxy** — one new child key, a relist path in `ChildSlot` and `tools/list` — `Scripts/mcp-proxy.py`.
- **Test fleet** — new suites `sbx_restrict`, `mcp_sandbox`, `mcp_skill`; new group M in `mcp_proxy`; the 7 fleet tables — `tests/`.
- **Wiki** — 2 ADRs + 4 addenda (ADR 0005, ADR 0008 twice (R3:I2), ADR 0027, via `addendum.py` only, R2:M6), 2 component pages, subsystem rows, roadmap items — `docs/`.
- **Forge config** — 3 new test targets, 7 stale descriptions, the `all` timeout — `project-forge.yaml` (R2:M7).

### Integration Points
```
ai-soul daemon / Claude Code (HTTP, bearer per uid)
   -> mcp-proxy --http (per uid, port P_u)                         [relist: true on the skill child]
        -> mcp-skill.py (stdio child)  --runner-url http://127.0.0.1:P_r/mcp --runner-token-file F_r
              -> HTTP POST (Streamable HTTP, SSE answer with ": keepalive" every 15 s)
                 -> mcp-proxy --http --port P_r --token-file F_r --max-body-bytes 8388608 --session-idle 900 (dedicated)
                      -> mcp-sandbox.py (stdio child, one process => global concurrency cap)
                           -> [sys.executable, -I, -B, sbx, --restrict-reads, --clean-env, ..., --, <interp>, <entry>, args...]
                                -> sandbox-exec -p <SBPL> <interp> ...            (macOS)
                                -> bwrap ... --clearenv --setenv ... -- <interp> ... (Linux)
```
- Data direction: files/args/stdin flow down as JSON; stdout/stderr/outFiles flow up RAW from the runner, sanitized only in mcp-skill (decision 7).
- The runner proxy passes its own environment to the runner (`build_child_env`, mcp-proxy.py:681-690: full `os.environ` minus `MCP_PROXY_TOKEN`). The runner therefore NEVER passes its environment to sbx: sbx is spawned with a fixed 2-key env and `--clean-env` sets the target env exactly. The env-canary row proves the double barrier.
- The proxy body cap for the runner instance is the existing flag `--max-body-bytes` (mcp-proxy.py:2919, in the HTTP argparse group :2904-2930); no proxy code change for it.
- The skill server's HTTP client treats SSE `:` comment lines as liveness and uses a per-recv socket timeout of 30 s (> the proxy's 15 s keepalive).

### Contract with ai-soul
These are the items ai-soul must be told and confirm. Item 4 was confirmed by ai-soul on 2026-10-11 (M0:12); items 7-8 were added in round 2 (R2:L7).
1. **Refusals are `isError` tool results with `error.code`** — never JSON-RPC errors (ADR 0010 fleet invariant). Codes: `busy` (concurrency cap reached; carries `retryAfterMs`; ai-soul maps it to 503), `sandbox_unavailable` (backend/plan/cleanup/disk problem or an sbx refusal), `invalid_params` (any input refusal, incl. unknown function or member, with a did-you-mean pointer), `internal` (handler crash; message = exception type only). A non-object `params` stays a protocol-level `-32602` (mcp-search precedent). A failing script (non-zero exit, signal, timeout) is a NORMAL result.
2. **`run` lists files only inside one skill's `scripts/` and `refs/`**, never the tier root (which is enumerated only through `skills.json`).
3. **The runner proxy reads its token file at start**: rotation = rewrite the file + restart that proxy. mcp-skill re-reads the runner token on every `run`. Per-request re-read in the proxy is a roadmap item.
4. **Leaf-name grammar** for `scripts/` and `refs/` files: `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9._-]{0,63}$`; `.` and `..` refused (the leading-character class already excludes them; stated explicitly); no nesting; **<= 64 files and <= 4 MiB decoded per run**. CONFIRMED by ai-soul (2026-10-11, M0:12). ai-soul's own bounds are narrower — <= 16 files and <= 262144 bytes per skill version, base64 only under `refs/` — and a run bundles exactly ONE skill, so mcp-skill's 64 files / 4 MiB is a runner-side backstop, never the binding limit for an approved skill.
5. **Result field shapes (runner, `sandbox_call`)**: `signal` is a signal NAME string (`"SIGKILL"`, from `signal.Signals(n).name`) or `null`, never a number; on a timeout `exitCode` is `null`, `timedOut` is `true` and `signal` carries the kill signal's name. An engine-stage failure after the target was spawned (a `sandbox-exec` profile error, a `bwrap` die) is a NORMAL result with a non-zero `exitCode` and the engine's own message in `stderr` — a declared limit, see KD-16.
6. **mcp-skill's `run` answer is NOT the spec's raw result shape**: mcp-skill returns sanitized, nonce-framed TEXT (a header line with exit/signal/timedOut/durationMs plus framed `stdout`/`stderr`/`outfile` blocks, decision 7); the raw JSON object is visible only on the runner's own endpoint.
7. **Group/world-writable skill files and directories are refused** (R2:L7a): every `SKILL.md`, `scripts/` / `refs/` leaf and the `scripts/` / `refs/` directories themselves with `mode & 0o022 != 0` are refused (`writable-by-others`); ai-soul must write them 0644/0755 or tighter. The tier root itself is checked at startup (`_open_root`).
8. **No `notifications/tools/list_changed`** (R2:L7b): neither mcp-skill nor the skill proxy emits it (the proxy drops a child's `list_changed`, mcp-proxy.py:1037-1038); a client sees a new or changed skill only by calling `tools/list` again, which triggers the relist (FR-17).

**Settled (no longer pending):**
- **M8 — description unit = UTF-16 code units.** FINAL, agreed with ai-soul: both the per-tier approve-time budget (1800) and the loader ceiling (4096) are counted in UTF-16 code units, matching ai-soul's JS `.length` cut (`CHAT_TOOL_DESCRIPTION_MAX_CHARS = 4 * 1024`, `/Users/zoltan.ponekker/Code/ai-soul/src/adapters/chat/tool-broker.ts:69`); measured as `len(s.encode("utf-16-le")) // 2` (decision 6).
- **R4:CONTRACT — approve-time text cleanliness is a superset of load-time cleanliness.** FINAL, confirmed by ai-soul (peer `ai-soul-08`, 2026-10-10): every skill text predicate ai-soul applies at approve time — `description`, body, files, `reason` — refuses `UNSAFE_TEXT_RANGES` ∪ `/[\p{Cc}\p{Cf}\p{Cs}]/u` (with `\t` and `\n` allowed only in body and files). That set is a superset of what `_web_clean` drops (P11), so an approved text is already load-time clean: approve-time ⊆ load-time-clean, and mcp-skill's `description == _web_clean(description, "")` check (Step 16, reason `description-not-clean`) should never fire on an approved skill. Declared residual (ADR 0032 limits, Step 20): ai-soul classifies with Node's ICU, mcp-skill with Python's `unicodedata`; when their Unicode versions differ, a newly assigned code point can fall in a general category on one side and stay unassigned (`Cn`, kept) or differently classified on the other. If mcp-skill reports `description-not-clean` on an APPROVED skill, check that version skew first. <!-- UNVERIFIED: the ai-soul-side predicate (UNSAFE_TEXT_RANGES and the regex) is a peer confirmation, not read in this repo -->
- **`skill_propose` schema received** (for deferred item 8, the skill-proposal Claude Code skill): it lives at `.claude/tmp/r0020/skill-propose-schema.md` (temp copy; the roadmap item names it, the skill is still out of scope for v1).

### Constraints
- Seatbelt evaluation is last-match-wins (sbx:142-161); restricted mode keeps every DENY rule after every ALLOW rule.
- Seatbelt regex bounded quantifiers `{n}`, `{n,}`, `{m,n}` COMPILE but silently NEVER MATCH (a profile using them loads and does nothing); `+` and `*` are honoured. A depth rule must therefore spell its group out literally (M0:9, `item-depth.md` §1).
- Seatbelt checks the RESOLVED full path: an exec rule matches the realpath (M0:3, `item-3a-g.md` 3g), a depth rule holds past PATH_MAX and for `chdir`- and `*at`-relative calls (`item-depth.md` §3), and a rename DESTINATION is checked as `file-write-create` while the depth of the moved subtree is never checked (`item-depth.md` §4).
- On this host every MUTATING metadata op is priced by the directory's depth (mkdir+rmdir pair 59 us at depth 100, 9.8 ms at 1000, 27.7 ms at 3000; `fstatat` constant ~3.7 us; `item-4.md`); the cleanup design keeps every mutation shallow (KD-18, M0:9). The cause was not isolated.
- bwrap applies mounts in argv order, later wins; a `--symlink` or bind cannot be created inside an already read-only bound tree -> spellings under a base bind are skipped by the builder.
- XNU refuses `setrlimit(RLIMIT_AS)` below the CURRENT VSZ of the calling process (decision 12). The caller is the sbx child (a python process, ~32.5 GiB VSZ), so the effective AS limit is `max(VSZ(interp), VSZ(sbx launcher)) + budget + slack`. Consequence measured in the code: today's default `_RLIMIT_AS_BYTES = 8 GiB` (sbx:1130) is INERT on macOS — `_lower_to` swallows the refusal (sbx:1163-1165, "skip, don't brick"). ADR 0031 records it.
- RLIMIT_NPROC is per real uid on both OSes (decision 11). <!-- UNVERIFIED: Linux >= 5.14 ucounts behaviour of RLIMIT_NPROC inside bwrap's user namespace on Ubuntu 22.04 kernel 5.15 -->
- Child frame ceiling in the proxy `_MAX_FRAME` 64 MiB (mcp-proxy.py:386); ai-soul response cap 32 MiB; the runner's worst-case response is bounded at config load (<= 16 MiB).
- `tools/list` must never fail because of skill content: the over-ceiling refusal travels inside the description.
- `_mcp_websearch.py` is a WHOLE source (`Scripts/amalgamate.py:113-114`): a host takes all its blocks in order or none, enforced by `whole-source-regions` (`tests/test_generated_region.py:638`, recorded at :892). This decides the sanitizer home (§4).

---

## 3. Captured Information

### Existing Patterns

**P1 — sbx pure-builder / single-I/O-site pattern** (sbx:605-660). Every filesystem read happens in a resolver called from `main()`; builders are pure functions of `Scope`. New resolvers (`resolve_reads`, `linux_base_reads`, `uid_process_count`) follow it.
```python
# sbx:641-649 (secret_paths)
home = [os.path.expanduser("~/.ssh"), ...]
paths = [(p, os.path.isdir(p)) for p in home if os.path.exists(p)]
```

**P2 — sbx injection floor** (`_INJECT_CHARS` sbx:775, `canonicalize` sbx:778-793). Every path that lands in SBPL or bwrap argv passes it; refusal = one stderr line, exit 2.
```python
for candidate in (dir_arg, resolved):
    if any(ch in _INJECT_CHARS for ch in candidate):
        sys.stderr.write("sbx: error: refusing --write scope %r: ..." % (dir_arg,))
        sys.exit(2)
```

**P3 — sbx fail-closed refusal + exit codes**: 2 usage (`_usage_error` sbx:834-837), 3 fail-closed (`_detect_engine` sbx:485-508; builder `ValueError` -> 3).

**P4 — Seatbelt builder ordering** (sbx:192-266): `(version 1)`, `(deny default)`, minimal ops, reads (sbx:201), writes, `/dev/null` literal (sbx:215), dir-secret denies, carve-outs, file-secret denies, shadow write denies, network last.

**P5 — bwrap builder ordering** (sbx:327-452): base `--ro-bind / /` (sbx:327), `--dev /dev --remount-ro /dev`, socket tmpfs, binds, masks, carve-outs, shadows, file masks, `--unshare-pid --unshare-ipc --proc /proc`, `--unshare-net`, `--new-session`, `--die-with-parent`, `--seccomp fd`, `--`, argv.

**P6 — sbx rlimit site** (sbx:1130-1196): best-effort, hard preserved, refusal swallowed. The NEW strict path must not reuse `_lower_to`'s swallow.
```python
# sbx:1155-1165
def _lower_to(which, desired):
    try:
        _soft, hard = resource.getrlimit(which)
    except (OSError, ValueError):
        return
    ...
    except (OSError, ValueError):
        pass                             # platform refused it; skip, don't brick
```

**P7 — Gate flag grammar** (sbx-gate.py:139-191): exact bare tokens for booleans (`--ro` :155, `--seccomp` :158, `--dry-run` :166), both `--write VALUE` (:178) and `--write=VALUE` (:185); `--net` -> `(False, "")` (:153); ANY unrecognised token -> hard prompt. `METACHARS` sbx-gate.py:67 (`=`, `:`, `+` are not metacharacters).

**P8 — Proxy config refusal style** (mcp-proxy.py:589-627): every refusal is `ConfigError(f"{where}: ...")` naming the key, never the value.
```python
unknown = sorted(str(key) for key in entry if key not in _CHILD_KEYS)
if unknown:
    raise ConfigError(f"{where}: unknown key(s): {', '.join(unknown)}")
```

**P9 — Proxy tool-set immutability** (mcp-proxy.py:1478-1485):
```python
names = frozenset(t["name"] for t in tools)
if names != self.tool_names:
    self.state = "disabled"
    self.disabled_reason = "tool set changed across restart"
    log.error("child=%s tool set changed across restart (added=%s removed=%s); disabled", ...)
```

**P10 — Token file read** (`_http_token_file` mcp-proxy.py:709-738): `os.open(path, O_RDONLY | O_NOFOLLOW)`, `fstat` on the fd, regular file, `st_uid == geteuid()`, `mode & 0o077 == 0`; refusals name the rule, never the content. mcp-skill's runner-token reader is a host-local rewrite of this logic.

**P11 — Sanitizer** (`Scripts/_mcp_websearch.py:94-116`):
```python
_WEB_LINE_SEPARATORS = "\N{LINE SEPARATOR}\N{PARAGRAPH SEPARATOR}\x85"

def _web_clean(text, keep):
    out = []
    for ch in text:
        code = ord(ch)
        if 0x20 <= code < 0x7f or ch in keep:
            out.append(ch)
        elif ch in _WEB_LINE_SEPARATORS: ...
```
Hosts take it only as part of the WHOLE 37-name websearch region (`Scripts/mcp-search.py:7092`, `Scripts/search_duckduckgo.py:87`). Parity precedent: `tests/test_mcp_search.py:639-646` row `F sanitizer-copies-agree` (corpus `range(0, 0x3100)` + `0xe0000-0xe0200` + `0xfe00-0xff00` + two astral points).

**P12 — Declared hand copy** (`Scripts/amalgamate.py:152-185`, `HAND_COPY_REASONS`), exact precedent for a WHOLE source:
```python
("llm-router.py", "_ch_address_refused"):
    "_mcp_chrome.py is a WHOLE source (G-c); the router needs its address "
    "classifier and none of the client",
```
The census row `hand-copies-are-named` (`tests/test_generated_region.py:747-754`) marks a hand copy `[UNDECLARED]` unless `(host, name)` is in the map — a copy only shows up if it keeps the canonical NAME.

**P13 — Fleet server shape** (`Scripts/MCP_SKELETON.md`): header §1 (:51), logging §2 (:93), RPC helpers §3 (:129), error envelope §3 (:173-275, top-level `"error"` key predicate, `log.exception` with a literal), dispatch §4 (:279), run loop §5 (:327; pool variant §5a :376), main §6 (:538), param hygiene §7 (:587; `_bool_param` §7b :679; two spellings refused §7c :704), generated regions §8 (:784), checklist §9 (:969).

**P14 — Canonical blocks to generate**: `_result` `Scripts/_mcp_json.py:150`, `_error` :155, `_ensure_dict` :302 (with its strict-JSON blocks and `_json_error_window` :279 on one marker); `_did_you_mean` `Scripts/_mcp_dispatch.py:68`, `_unknown_params_refusal` :111; `MAX_INFLIGHT_REQUESTS` `Scripts/_mcp_concurrency.py:72`; `_configure_logging` `Scripts/_mcp_logging.py:122`, `_log_value` :141.
**Not taken (R3:M3)**: `_bool_param` (`Scripts/_mcp_json.py:161-173`) and `_int_param` (:176-190) are LENIENT by design — `_int_param(True, d)` is `1`, any unconvertible value silently becomes the default, `_bool_param("no")` is False — which contradicts both new hosts' strict refusal contract (`timeoutMs` bool refused, T21). Neither host carries them; every parameter is validated with explicit `isinstance` checks (`isinstance(v, int) and not isinstance(v, bool)` for integers, `type(v) is bool` for booleans), a wrong type refused `invalid_params` naming the member.
**Window liveness (R3:M2)**: the `_ensure_dict` marker carries `_json_error_window`, and `tests/test_generated_region.py:779-811` (row `window-region-has-a-caller`, `WINDOW_NAME` :382, `WINDOW_RELAY` :392) FAILS a host that carries the window region but calls neither `_json_error_window(` nor `_ensure_dict(` outside its regions. Both new hosts therefore call `_ensure_dict(raw_params)` in their dispatcher (`handle_sandbox_call` / `handle_skill_call`), exactly as `Scripts/mcp-inspect.py:2473-2477` and `Scripts/mcp-search.py:8772-8776` do.

**P15 — Subprocess reclaim** (`Scripts/mcp-inspect.py`): `_Reclaim` :384-430 (adopted Popen killed on cancel), `_signal_group` :432-445, `_run_child` :458-499 (explicit `stdin=`, own session). mcp-sandbox extends `_Reclaim` with `adopt_pgid(pgid)` for the target pgid reported by the `spawned` status event. **`_run_child` cannot run an sbx spawn (R2:M2)**: it passes no `pass_fds` and blocks in `communicate()` until the child exits (:478-489), while every sbx spawn needs the status fd passed, a mid-run read of the `spawned` line and (for the VSZ probe) a held-open stdin. mcp-sandbox therefore has ONE adopting sbx-spawn helper, `_spawn_sbx` (written in Step 7 with the rest of the KILL machinery, R3:M1; first used in Step 11), shared by `run_one`, `measure_vsz`, the `--version` run and `self_test`; the host-local `_run_child` copy is kept ONLY for `/bin/ps`. The cancel arm to copy is `McpServer._serve`'s `except asyncio.CancelledError: reclaim.cancel(loop); raise` (`Scripts/mcp-inspect.py:2793-2799`), whose call path reaches `os.killpg` through `_Reclaim.cancel` -> `_signal_group` (:412-445); `tests/test_cancel.py:819-831` fails `KILL_NO_ARM` without the re-raising arm and `KILL_NO_SIGNAL` when the arm reaches no `os.killpg` (R3:L5). mcp-inspect's grace is `CANCEL_KILL_GRACE = 5.0` (:381); mcp-sandbox uses ONE grace, `_KILL_GRACE_S = 2.0`, for both the cancel escalation and the hard-deadline SIGTERM->SIGKILL (KD-17).

**P16 — Test harness** (`tests/run.py`): `run_python_suite(module_name, opts)` :71; wrappers `run_sbx_gate` :85, `run_sbx_seccomp` :89; `SUITES` :270 with `(name, fn, description, count)`; `sbx_gate` row 105 (:275-276); `sbx_seccomp` row :280; `mcp_proxy` row :726-738 (count `112` at :738) with a count-ledger comment above it. `tests/run.py` carries uncommitted user hunks (PC-2) and these anchors drift with them: **edit by content (match the quoted text), never by line number**. sbx loaded in tests via `_load_helper` (`tests/test_sbx_gate.py:288`), the gate via `_load_gate` (:282); live Seatbelt helper `_run_live_seatbelt` (:1486).

**P17 — HTTP test client seed** (`tests/test_mcp_proxy.py`): `read_sse` :2471, `sse_call` :2517, `HttpClient` :2572 (SDK header order: content-type, accept, authorization, mcp-session-id, mcp-protocol-version), `http_init` :2885. mcp-skill's `RunnerClient` is a bounded production rewrite (deadline, size caps, no header logging).

**P18 — Proxy stub child** (`tests/files/mcp_proxy/tf_stub_child.py`): flags `--tool`, `--state-dir` (events.jsonl per message incl. `method`), `--toolset-file` (read at start only), `--page-size`, `--cursor-loop`. Relist rows add knobs (Step 15). Group anchor: `GL = "L. hygiene"` `tests/test_mcp_proxy.py:126`, `group_l` :5015. The runner calls the groups in order a..l (`tests/test_mcp_proxy.py:5088-5099`) and group L is the hygiene snapshot (`group_l(suite, fixture_root, pyc_before, tree_before)` :5099), so a new group must run BEFORE it (R3:L1). The only delay knob today is `--init-delay` (`tf_parse_args` :378), applied at :325-326 and keyed on `state.prior` — the number of PRIOR PROCESS STARTS read from `<state-dir>/<tool>.pids` (:109-119), not on a per-process message count (R3:L6).

**P19 — Cancel classes** (`tests/test_cancel.py:178-183`): `TASK`, `KILL`, `LSP_CANCEL`, `PG_CANCEL`, `REPLY_ONLY`; mcp-inspect row :214 is the KILL template. Declared totals `DECLARED_KILL = 5` (:249) and `DECLARED_REPLY_ONLY = 4` (:252). The KILL class is AST-checked: `BLOCKING_SPAWNS = {"run", "call", "check_call", "check_output"}` (:303) flags any `subprocess.run`-style call in a KILL server (`KILL_BLOCKING` :308) unless `(file, enclosing function)` is in `KILL_EXEMPT` (:262-277), and an exempt function that adopts its child fails (`KILL_EXEMPT_ADOPTED` :311). The adoption check keys on a call NAMED `adopt` (`RECORD_CALLS = {"adopt"}` :302) that receives the Popen's variable inside the SAME function as the `Popen(...)` (:788-803), and the Popen must carry `start_new_session=True` (`KILL_NO_SESSION`, :804-809); `adopt_pgid` is not in `RECORD_CALLS` and neither helps nor hurts. The adopting helper to copy is mcp-inspect's `_run_child` (`Scripts/mcp-inspect.py:458-499`: `start_new_session=True`, `reclaim.adopt(proc)` when a request is current, run()'s timeout path verbatim); `_spawn_sbx` (Step 7, R3:M1) follows the same two rules. An undeclared KILL server with no adopted `Popen` anywhere fails `KILL_NO_SPAWN` (:804-806), which is why the helpers land with the file, not with their first caller.

**P20 — Read-loop declared table** (`tests/test_read_loop.py`): `FLEET` :190 (header :167-189 says "check this against docs/adr/0008's per-server table"), `DECLARED_POOL = 9` (:234), `DECLARED_COROUTINE = 8` (:235). The ADR check is MANUAL: no test reads `docs/adr/0008` (a search of `tests/` finds the path only in this comment and the module docstring, :6-8), so per-server rows written as a dated ADR 0008 addendum satisfy it; Step 7 updates the header comment to "docs/adr/0008's per-server table and its addenda" in the same commit as mcp-sandbox's FLEET row AND its ADR 0008 addendum, and Step 16 writes mcp-skill's addendum with its FLEET row, so the header never points at an addendum that does not exist yet (R3:I2).

**P22 — Fleet-table case formulas** (the `tests/run.py` ledger comments, edit by content, P16): `read_loop` 51 = 2 per server (group A gate :474-489 + group B declared :492-512, one case each per server) x 17 + 17 tail; `cancel` 88 = "2N + a fixed tail" (:583-585; groups A :1017 and B :1027 per server) = 34 + 54; `wire_log` 64 = 2N + tail (:594-596; groups A :889, B :894) = 34 + 30; `handler_crash` 62 = "26 declared sites + 17 servers + 4 roster + 11 control + 4 hygiene", sites = 17 layer-W + 9 layer-D (:605-608; group A one case per declared SITE :476, group B one per server :504), with `DECLARED_W = 17`, `DECLARED_D = 9` (`tests/test_handler_crash.py:248-249`); `protocol_version` 77 = "3 clauses x 17 servers + 2 fleet + 3 roster + 18 control + 3 hygiene" (:635-641); `py_deps` 61 includes one case per `NEW_39_SCOPE` file (`tests/test_py_deps.py:1184`, tuple :162-165). `wire_log` also types `DECLARED_FULL = 14` (`tests/test_wire_log.py:321`). `generated_region` (140) has NO per-host rows: every host loop sits inside one `suite.record` (e.g. `strict-region-in-every-host`, `tests/test_generated_region.py:3054-3065`); the only per-entry table, `STRICT_SITES` (:3281), is opt-in and this plan adds no row to it. `param_contract` (53) is an opt-in `HOSTS` list (`tests/test_param_contract.py:441`) with no tree walk; the plan does not join it (v1), so it stays 53.
**Discovery is by glob, not by table (R3:M1)**: `read_loop`, `cancel`, `wire_log`, `handler_crash` and `protocol_version` each enumerate `Scripts/` for `mcp-*.py` (`SERVER_GLOB_PREFIX = "mcp-"`: `tests/test_read_loop.py:153`/:471, `tests/test_cancel.py:1000-1003`, `tests/test_wire_log.py:240`/:846, `tests/test_handler_crash.py:470-473`, `tests/test_protocol_version.py:179`/:426). The moment `Scripts/mcp-sandbox.py` or `Scripts/mcp-skill.py` exists, each suite records cases for it, and an undeclared one FAILS ("not declared in FLEET" `tests/test_read_loop.py:497` and `tests/test_cancel.py:1031`; `NOT_DECLARED` `tests/test_handler_crash.py:508-512`; `NOT_DECLARED = "SERVER-NOT-IN-TABLE"` `tests/test_wire_log.py:378`/:872) and the typed count drifts. Every FLEET/WIRE row, `DECLARED_*` total and typed count for a server therefore lands in the commit that creates its file (Step 7, Step 16). `py_deps`'s `NEW_39_SCOPE` (`tests/test_py_deps.py:162-165`) is opt-in (a listed absent file is INFO, :1187-1190), but its row moves with the file too so the count history stays one commit per server.

**P23 — Nested-sandbox INFO path** (`tests/test_sbx_gate.py:1523-1531`, `_run_live_seatbelt` :1486): before any live row, one control run of the real helper (`--ro`); when it produces no result and `"sandbox_apply"` is in its stderr ("sandbox_apply: Operation not permitted", a nested Seatbelt), EVERY live row of the group is recorded INFO with that stderr (first 200 chars) — counted, never omitted — because that is the host, not the profile (`docs/subsystems/tests.md:228`).
```python
ctl, ctl_res = _sbx(["--ro"], os.path.join(outside_dir, "ctl"))
if ctl_res is None and "sandbox_apply" in ctl.stderr:
    _skip_all("sandbox-exec cannot apply a profile here: %s"
              % ctl.stderr.strip()[:200])
    return
```

### Type Definitions

sbx `Scope` today (sbx:56-120): `writes, net, ro, argv, secret_paths=None, carveouts=None, shadow_write_denies=None, engine=None, seccomp=False, seccomp_fd=None, file_mask_fds=None, cwd=None`. New fields (Step 2):
```python
    restrict_reads: bool = False
    read_rules: tuple = ()         # ((spelling, kind, link_value), ...) kind in {"symlink","dir","file"}; link_value only for symlink
    meta_literals: tuple = ()      # every proper ancestor of every allowed path, incl. "/"
    exec_rules: tuple = ()         # ((spelling, is_dir), ...) process-exec allow-list (restricted only)
    linux_base: tuple = ()         # (("ro-bind", path) | ("symlink", value, path)), resolved in main()
    env: dict = None               # None = inherit (legacy); dict = exact target env (--clean-env)
    timeout: float = None
    status_fd: int = None
    limits: tuple = ()             # (("RLIMIT_AS", int), ...) explicit, strict
    nproc_headroom: int = None     # from --rlimit-nproc +N; resolved to an absolute in the supervisor
```

mcp-proxy (mcp-proxy.py:469-475, :486):
```python
class ChildSpec(NamedTuple):
    name: str
    argv: Tuple[str, ...]
    env: Dict[str, str]
    call_timeout: float
    startup_timeout: float
    relist: bool = False            # NEW (Step 15)
_CHILD_KEYS = frozenset({"name", "command", "args", "env", "wrapper", "call_timeout", "startup_timeout", "relist"})
```
`ChildSlot` (mcp-proxy.py:1267): `tools: List[dict]`, `tool_names: FrozenSet[str]`, `state` in starting|ready|restarting|disabled, `disabled_reason`; `_bring_up` :1297, `_list_all_tools` :1354 (`_MAX_TOOL_PAGES = 100` :411).

mcp-sandbox (new):
```python
class InterpreterSpec(NamedTuple):   # from config
    name: str                        # "python3" | "bash" | "node"
    path: str                        # absolute spelling, e.g. /usr/local/bin/python3
    extra_reads: Tuple[str, ...]
class InterpreterPlan(NamedTuple):   # computed at startup, refreshed on signature change
    name: str; path: str; realpath: str; version: str
    argv_prefix: Tuple[str, ...]     # e.g. (path, "-I", "-S", "-B")
    reads: Tuple[str, ...]           # spellings passed as --read (every hop + every target)
    execs: Tuple[str, ...]           # passed as --exec; REALPATHS only (M0:3) + /bin, /usr/bin
    signature: Tuple[Tuple[str, int, int], ...]  # (path, st_ino, st_mtime_ns) of every closure file (lstat)
    vsz_baseline: int; as_limit: int # bytes
class PlanState(NamedTuple):
    plan: Optional[InterpreterPlan]  # None while refresh-failed
    state: str                       # "ready" | "refresh-failed"
    reason: Optional[str]
class Limits(NamedTuple):
    timeout_ms: int; max_timeout_ms: int; as_budget: int; as_mode: str  # "baseline" | "absolute"
    nproc_headroom: int; fsize: int; nofile: int
    stdout_bytes: int; stderr_bytes: int; max_files: int; max_files_bytes: int
    max_out_files: int; max_out_bytes: int; max_stdin_bytes: int; max_args: int; max_arg_chars: int
    max_concurrent: int; min_free_bytes: int
class SandboxConfig(NamedTuple):
    sbx: str; work_root: str; interpreters: Dict[str, InterpreterSpec]; limits: Limits
    seccomp: str  # "auto" | "on" | "off"
    log_content: bool
class RunRequest(NamedTuple):
    files: Tuple[Tuple[str, bytes, bool], ...]   # (relpath, data, executable)
    entry: str; interpreter: str; args: Tuple[str, ...]; stdin: Optional[bytes]; timeout_ms: int
class MachO(NamedTuple):
    dylibs: Tuple[Tuple[int, str], ...]   # (load command, install name)
    rpaths: Tuple[str, ...]
```
mcp-skill (new):
```python
class IndexEntry(NamedTuple):
    name: str; dir: str; description: str
    scripts: Tuple[Tuple[str, str], ...]    # (path "scripts/x.py", interpreter)
class Skill(NamedTuple):
    tier: str            # "household" | "private"
    entry: IndexEntry
class TierIndex(NamedTuple):
    tier: str; valid: Dict[str, IndexEntry]; invalid: Tuple[Tuple[str, str], ...]  # (label, reason)
    error: Optional[str]; budget_sum: int    # UTF-16 code units of f"{name} - {description}\n" over valid entries,
                                             # len(s.encode("utf-16-le")) // 2 (decision 6; R2:M4)
```

### Build System
- No compiled build. Gates run through forge: `mcp__mcp-forge__forge_call function=test` (fleet entry `tests/run.py`, `SUITES` :270; edit by content, P16). Generated regions: `python3 Scripts/amalgamate.py` and `--check`; `TARGET_GLOB = "mcp-*.py"` (`Scripts/amalgamate.py:69`) picks both new servers up automatically; `CANONICAL_NAMES` (:77-82) and `WHOLE_SOURCES` (:113-114) are NOT changed.
- New suite wrappers in `tests/run.py` beside `run_sbx_seccomp` (:89; edit by content): `run_sbx_restrict`, `run_mcp_sandbox`, `run_mcp_skill`.
- `project-forge.yaml` (R2:M7): "every suite has a matching `forge` target in `project-forge.yaml`, each requiring the `syntax` prerequisite" (`docs/subsystems/tests.md:211-212`). New `test` targets `sbx_restrict` (after `sbx_seccomp`, `project-forge.yaml:77-83`), `mcp_sandbox` and `mcp_skill` (before `mcp_proxy`, :351), each shaped like `sbx_seccomp`: `description`, `requires: [syntax]`, `commands: [python3 tests/run.py <suite>]`, `filter.grep: 'SUITE|AGGREGATE|cases|ALL|FAIL|INFO|SKIP|DRIFT|declared|PROBLEM'`, plus a `timeout` when the suite's measured time exceeds 400 s (measured + 50 %, rounded up to 60; the default is `settings.timeout: 600`, :23-24). Stale descriptions updated in the same commit as the counts they name: `read_loop` ("nine servers ... pool, eight ... coroutines", :256) -> ten/eight (Step 7) -> eleven/eight (Step 16); `cancel` (:264) adds sandbox to the kill list and skill to the reply-only list; `wire_log` ("fourteen servers at the canonical ... form", :272) -> sixteen; `handler_crash` ("all seventeen have ... which nine do ... the eight absences", :280) -> nineteen / eleven / eight; `protocol_version` ("the seventeen declared values ... would fail seventeen cases", :296) -> nineteen; `py_deps` (the `NEW_39_SCOPE` list, :320) adds `mcp-sandbox.py` and `mcp-skill.py`; `mcp_proxy` ("112 typed cases", :352) -> 123 and names group M (relist); its own `timeout: 420` (:354) is re-measured in Step 15 because group M adds real delays (R3:L7). Intermediate wording: the fleet descriptions are updated twice, once in Step 7 (mcp-sandbox: ten pool / fifteen at the canonical form / eighteen, ten, eight / eighteen declared values / kill list) and once in Step 16 (the final wording below), each in the commit that moves the counts they name (R3:M1). The `all` target's `timeout: 900` (:45; comment :42-44: "measured 568.6 s ... measured + 50 % (852.9 s), rounded up to a multiple of 60") is re-measured after the three live suites land (Step 20) and set to `max(900, ceil(1.5 * measured / 60) * 60)` with the comment rewritten to the new measurement.
