## 4. Alternative Approaches

### Selected: four layers, each owning one concern
`sbx` (confinement primitives: restricted reads, exec filter, clean env, supervised timeout, status protocol, strict rlimits) < `mcp-sandbox` (policy: limits, workdir lifecycle, output caps, Mach-O read set, VSZ baselines, plan refresh, concurrency) < dedicated `mcp-proxy --http` (HTTP, bearer, sessions, body cap) < `mcp-skill` (tiers, index, file hygiene, sanitizing, framing, runner client).
**Rationale**: every primitive lands where it is reusable and gate-able (sbx flags benefit every `sbx` user and are covered by the gate), HTTP security stays in the one audited front (ADR 0027/0029), and untrusted-text handling sits at the edge the model reads (decision 7). Each layer is testable alone; the escape suite tests the composition.
**Trade-offs**: two HTTP hops per run (negligible vs a 30 s budget); a second proxy instance to deploy; an MCP HTTP client inside a server (declared deviation, ADR 0032); sbx grows a second exec path (mitigated: both paths share one `_prepare_exec(scope) -> (engine_path, argv, env)`).

### Selected sub-decision: the sanitizer is a DECLARED HAND COPY (S1), not a generated region (S2)
**Verified evidence**:
- `Scripts/amalgamate.py:102-114`: `_mcp_websearch.py` is in `WHOLE_SOURCES` — "a host carrying some of their blocks and not others is a violation, never a choice".
- `tests/test_generated_region.py:638` (`whole_source_problems`: "*names* must be ALL of *source*'s blocks, in order") and the FAIL row `whole-source-regions` (:892) enforce it on every `mcp-*.py` and declared host.
- Both existing hosts take the full 37-name list (`Scripts/mcp-search.py:7092`, `Scripts/search_duckduckgo.py:87`), so there is no partial-region precedent.

**Therefore** a two-name region `_mcp_websearch.py :: _WEB_LINE_SEPARATORS, _web_clean` in `mcp-skill.py` would fail the generated_region suite. mcp-skill keeps `_WEB_LINE_SEPARATORS` and `_web_clean` under their CANONICAL names, byte-identical to `Scripts/_mcp_websearch.py:94-116`, declared by two `HAND_COPY_REASONS` rows with the reason `"_mcp_websearch.py is a WHOLE source (G-c); mcp-skill needs its sanitizer and none of the search"` — the llm-router/`_mcp_chrome.py` precedent (P12). Drift is pinned by two parity rows: a source-text row (host block text == `amalgamate.load_blocks(Scripts/_mcp_websearch.py)` text, `load_blocks` :217) and a behaviour row (the P11 corpus with `keep` in `("", "\t\n")`).

**Why the other two drafts were wrong**:
- risk-first (S2, generate the two names): its premise "hosts already take partial name lists from a source" is false for this source; the region would fail `whole-source-regions`. Taking the whole websearch source instead would drag the search engine, Bing/DDG parsers and network code into a read-only skill server.
- mvp-first (hand copy renamed `_SKILL_LINE_SEPARATORS` / `_skill_clean`): a renamed copy is invisible to `--census hand-copies` (the walk matches canonical block NAMES, `tests/test_generated_region.py:747-751`), so the third copy would never land on the census page. Keeping the canonical names makes it a DECLARED row.

### Rejected: a fifteenth canonical source for the sanitizer (`_mcp_text.py` / `_mcp_untrusted.py`)
**Reason**: it would have exactly one host: the two existing copies cannot migrate, because their sibling blocks call `_web_clean`/`_code_clean` from inside WHOLE sources and a region may not resolve names from another source (ADR 0014). Still needs a parity row plus `CANONICAL_NAMES`, the test mirror, the generated-regions page and the census. Kept as a roadmap item.

### Rejected: lift the Streamable HTTP MCP client into a canonical source now
**Reason**: one production host (mcp-skill); the seed is test code (P17), not a generation host. Generation buys drift protection only between two hosts. Roadmap: "lift when a second host appears".

### Rejected: runner with its own HTTP front
**Reason**: duplicates the hardened front (`_mcp_httpfront.py`, ADR 0029); decision 1 forbids HTTP code in mcp-sandbox.

### Rejected: skill server calling sbx directly (no runner)
**Reason**: the concurrency cap must be global across every per-uid skill proxy; the Mach-O/VSZ plan would be computed N times; a long-lived process would both read untrusted skill files and spawn them.

### Rejected: coarse Homebrew-prefix reads (`--read /usr/local`)
**Reason**: `/usr/local` holds every keg, `etc/`, `var/` (databases, logs). The measurement shows a precise set works.

### Rejected: `--read` realpath'd inside sbx (and sbx walking symlink chains)
**Reason**: Seatbelt needs the symlink FILE itself readable (dyld/`lstat` touch the spelling) as well as the target; a realpath'd read loses the hop. Keeping the spelling makes sbx dumb and each hop one explicit rule supplied by the caller (mvp-first KD-3).

### Rejected: `--status-file PATH` instead of `--status-fd N`
**Reason**: the unsandboxed sbx parent would write a caller-chosen path — a write outside every scope the gate would have to reason about. An inherited fd grants nothing.

### Rejected: `/bin/sh -c 'ulimit -v ...; exec "$@"'` trampoline for RLIMIT_AS on macOS
**Reason**: inserts a shell into the security-critical exec chain and needs `/bin/sh` in every exec allow-list. Kept as the documented fallback if M0 shows the bash effective budget is unacceptable (R-3). **M0 (M0:11, `item-2.md`)**: the effective budget is ~1.27 GiB for every interpreter (bash 1.267/1.268 GiB), far below 8 GiB — R-3 not triggered, the fallback stays a deferred roadmap item.

### Rejected: Seatbelt bounded quantifier for the depth cap (`(regex #"^<WD>/([^/]+/){100}")`) (M0:9)
**Reason**: measured to compile and silently never match (`item-depth.md` §1: even `(d/){2}` let mkdir reach 12 levels). Only the group written out literally 100 times works.

### Rejected: deny directory unlink in the write scope to close the rename-chaining bypass (M0:9)
**Reason**: `(deny file-write-unlink (require-all (vnode-type DIRECTORY) (regex #"^<WD>/")))` does stop a directory rename (its source is checked as a directory unlink), but it also refuses every `rmdir` inside the workdir (`shutil.rmtree`, `TemporaryDirectory` cleanup) — SBPL cannot tell the two apart (`item-depth.md` §4). The bypass is accepted and bounded at the remover instead (KD-18 flatten, M0:14).

### Rejected: JSON-RPC errors for busy/sandbox-unavailable (as the spec suggests)
**Reason**: ADR 0010 fleet invariant (`Scripts/MCP_SKELETON.md:173-275`) and the smoke harness. A symbolic `error.code` in an `isError` result carries the same distinction.

### Rejected: relist by polling or by honouring `notifications/tools/list_changed`
**Reason**: the proxy has no upstream push channel (no GET stream) and drops `list_changed` today (mcp-proxy.py:1037-1038); polling creates load with no reader. Pull-on-`tools/list` is the only moment a fresh description is consumed.

---

## 5. Implementation Strategy

### Overview
Close every escape path at the lowest layer that can enforce it (sbx), then add policy and bounded I/O in the runner, then the HTTP hop (existing proxy + `relist`), then the untrusted-text edge (mcp-skill). Each milestone starts with red escape rows; nothing ships behind a row never seen failing. M0 measurements gate design forks before code is written.

### Preconditions (blocking, before M1)
- **PC-1** The sbx `/dev/null` work (sbx:208-215, uncommitted in the tree today) must be committed by Zoltán before M1 starts. The golden "legacy-unchanged" rows (`D4`, `F9`) are pinned against the committed sbx. **MET (M0:15, `results.md`)**: `git log --oneline -1` on sbx -> `4d3640c fix(sbx): --ro keeps the /dev/null sink writable on Seatbelt (bwrap parity)`, `git status --porcelain` on sbx empty; the line is sbx:215.
- **PC-2** Files carrying uncommitted user hunks — `tests/run.py`, `ClaudeCode/skills/sandbox-run/SKILL.md`, `docs/adr/0005-approve-the-wrapper-not-the-command.md`, `docs/subsystems/skills.md`, `docs/subsystems/tests.md` (and `tests/test_sbx_gate.py`, which this plan does not touch) — are edited HUNK-LEVEL only (Edit tool, never Write), never reformatting or reverting foreign hunks, and our hunks are staged with `git apply --cached` (per the repo's concurrent-agents rule) so the user's hunks stay unstaged.
- **PC-3** Never touch 192.168.2.245; never edit `~/mcp` or live proxy configs.

### Key Design Decisions
- **KD-1 One status protocol + exit 124.** Today sbx exit 2/3 collides with the target's own 2/3 (sbx:1279-1290 passes the status through). `--status-fd` carries UTF-8 JSON lines (each `\n`-terminated, <= 512 bytes): exactly one `spawned` event `{"sbx": 1, "event": "spawned", "pid": P, "pgid": P}` (the target's pid is also its pgid because the child `setsid()`s; on Linux it is the bwrap pid/pgid), then exactly one terminal `exit` event with `outcome` in `exited|signaled|timeout|refused`. Humans get exit 124 + `sbx: timeout: wall-clock limit of %gs reached; process group killed`. A signaled target maps to exit `128 + signo`. mcp-sandbox ALWAYS uses the status fd and never interprets sbx's exit code alone; it adopts the reported pgid (P15 `adopt_pgid`) so it can kill the group even if sbx itself dies.
- **KD-2 Kill before reap.** The sbx supervisor kills the group while the leader is still an UNREAPED zombie, so the pgid cannot be recycled between reap and kill. Per platform (one function `_wait_exit_unreaped(pid, deadline) -> bool`, the only platform branch):
  - **Linux**: `os.waitid(os.P_PID, pid, os.WEXITED | os.WNOWAIT | os.WNOHANG)` polled every 20 ms.
  - **darwin**: the path is SELECTED by `sys.platform == "darwin"` (with `hasattr(select, "kqueue")` asserted), NEVER by the absence of `os.waitid` (M0:1): measured `hasattr(os, "waitid")` is True on python3 3.14.0 and False on 3.9.21 (`item-1.md`) — Python >= 3.13 exposes `waitid` on darwin, so "no `waitid`" is not a darwin test. `kq = select.kqueue()`; `kq.control([select.kevent(pid, filter=select.KQ_FILTER_PROC, flags=select.KQ_EV_ADD | select.KQ_EV_ONESHOT, fflags=select.KQ_NOTE_EXIT)], 0, 0)` registered right after `fork()` in the parent. An `ESRCH` on registration means the child already exited (a zombie, still unreaped, also answers `ESRCH`): treat as exited. Measured shapes (`item-1.md`): with `nevents == 0` (the call above) Python raises `OSError(ESRCH)` (`ProcessLookupError`) — the ONE shape the code catches; with `nevents >= 1` it would instead return an `EV_ERROR` event with `data == ESRCH`, so registration never passes `nevents >= 1`. Then `kq.control(None, 1, remaining)` blocks until exit or deadline. `NOTE_EXIT` reports the exit WITHOUT reaping (measured: event after ~0.30 s, child still `Z`).
  Then on both: `os.killpg(pid, SIGKILL)` with `ESRCH` AND `EPERM` ignored (macOS returns `EPERM` from `killpg` on a group whose only member is a zombie — measured on 3.14 and 3.9, `item-1.md`), then `os.waitpid(pid, 0)` reaps and yields the status. M0 item 1 MEASURED kqueue `NOTE_EXIT` usable on this host (the setsid grandchild died at `killpg`, `waitpid` reaped; R-5 STOP not met; M0:1). mcp-sandbox's own `killpg(target_pgid)` fires ONLY when sbx ended without a terminal `exit` event (sbx died); the normal path never signals the target group from the runner.
- **KD-3 Strict rlimits set soft == hard.** A script cannot raise its soft limit back. Any requested limit the kernel refuses = exit 3 (decision 12). A requested value above the current hard is refused, never silently clamped.
- **KD-4 AS limit = `max(VSZ(interp), VSZ(sbx launcher)) + budget + 256 MiB slack` on macOS**, measured under the exact production launch path and re-measured on every plan refresh; Linux absolute 1 GiB with an `"as_mode": "baseline"` fallback if node cannot start under 1 GiB (R-4).
- **KD-5 Clean env at TWO layers; the unsandboxed engine always runs with `{}`.** The runner spawns sbx with `env={"PATH": "/usr/bin:/bin", "LANG": "C.UTF-8"}`; sbx gives the target exactly `--env`:
  - **darwin (trampoline, C-M7 = S-M1)**: `sandbox-exec` is exec'd with `env = {}` (nothing reaches the unsandboxed engine) and the target argv becomes `["/usr/bin/env", "-i", "K1=V1", ..., "Kn=Vn", <argv0>, *rest]` (keys sorted). `/usr/bin/env -i` starts from an empty environment INSIDE the profile, applies exactly the pairs and `execvp`s the absolute argv0. sbx adds `(literal "/usr/bin/env")` to the exec rules whenever `--clean-env` is given on darwin. Because BSD `env` treats any leading `NAME=VALUE` word as an assignment, argv0 must contain no `=` (refused, exit 2); keys keep the `^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]{0,127}$` regex (so no key starts with `-`) and `LD_*`/`DYLD_*` stay refused. Rejected alternative: an sbx-side key allow-list passed through the engine env — it still hands caller-controlled values to the unsandboxed `sandbox-exec` process; no concrete blocker to the trampoline was found. **Measured live (M0:7, `item-3h-j.md` 3h)**: under the restricted profile with `env={}`, `/usr/bin/env -i A=1 B=2 <interp>` runs; `/usr/bin/env` needs an exec literal but NO read rule; python3 and node see exactly `A`, `B`; `/usr/bin/env -i` and dyld add nothing.
  - **Linux**: bwrap exec'd with `{}` and `--clearenv --setenv K V`, so `LD_PRELOAD` can never reach the unsandboxed bwrap.
- **KD-6 Exec filter in restricted mode.** Measured: unfiltered `process-exec` lets a script exec any binary whose own file needs no read rule. Restricted mode emits `(allow process-exec (literal ...) (subpath ...))` only for argv0's spelling plus `--exec` entries (mcp-sandbox passes the interpreter's REALPATH, the framework launcher's `Python.app/Contents/MacOS/Python` realpath, `/bin`, `/usr/bin`). A binary the script writes into its workdir is NOT exec-able (row `mcp_sandbox:E21`; measured, `item-3a-g.md` 3b: a planted binary AND a planted `#!/bin/sh` script both fail EPERM at exec — the filter checks the script's own path). **Measured (M0:3, `item-3a-g.md` 3g, `item-3h-j.md` 3i)**: the filter matches the REALPATH; a spelling literal is neither needed nor sufficient (Homebrew bash, python3 and a test symlink: spelling only -> exit 71, realpath only -> runs). So exec-filter literals are realpaths (+ `.../Versions/<v>/Resources/Python.app/Contents/MacOS/Python` for the framework python, without which python3 exits 1); the symlink hops are NOT exec entries. Symlinked PARENT directories in the hop chain (e.g. `/usr/local/opt/node` in `/usr/local/bin/node -> /usr/local/opt/node/bin/node`) are READ-set literals, not exec literals — without the read literal `sandbox-exec` exits 71 (`item-6.md` side finding 1). **Declared (S-L1)**: the `/bin` and `/usr/bin` subpaths make every system binary there exec-able inside the profile; each one still runs under the same profile (restricted reads, `(deny network*)`, no `mach-lookup` allow, one write scope), so it gains no capability the script lacks. Kept because scripts legitimately call `/bin/sh`, `/usr/bin/env` and coreutils; ADR 0031 records it. Narrowing to the self-test set is rejected (it would break ordinary scripts for no containment gain).
  **Setuid binaries (R2:S-L2, CWE-250)**: `/bin` and `/usr/bin` hold setuid-root binaries (e.g. `/usr/bin/crontab`, `/usr/bin/at`, `/usr/bin/sudo`). **MEASURED (M0:10, `item-3k.md`)**: Seatbelt REFUSES a setuid/setgid exec itself — kernel operation `forbidden-exec-sugid`, `EPERM`; exec'd by `/bin/sh` inside the profile (the production path) each of `crontab`, `at`, `sudo` gives sh exit **126** with `Operation not permitted`; as `sandbox-exec`'s argv0 exit 71. It holds under the KD-6 exec filter, under an unfiltered `(allow process-exec)` and under `(allow default)`. No setuid binary ever ran, so none read outside the profile and none gained euid 0 — stronger than "runs under the same profile". The KD-6 STOP rule did not fire; the `/bin` + `/usr/bin` exec subpaths stay. Row `mcp_sandbox:E34 setuid-exec-stays-confined` pins the REFUSAL live. Linux: bwrap always sets `PR_SET_NO_NEW_PRIVS` (bubblewrap's documented behaviour; not live-verified on the target, decision 13), so a setuid bit grants nothing there. ADR 0031 records both.
  **Linux has NO exec filter (R2:L8)**: bwrap has no `process-exec` equivalent, so on Linux a script CAN exec a binary it wrote into its workdir (the workdir is not mounted `noexec`); `mcp_sandbox:E21` is a macOS-only proof (INFO on Linux). The binary still runs inside the same namespaces, seccomp filter, rlimits and the read-only base, so it gains no capability the script lacks; declared in T5 and in ADR 0031's limits.
- **KD-7 Drain-and-discard after the output cap**, not close-the-pipe: the script's exit status stays its own (no induced SIGPIPE), server memory stays flat, CPU bounded by the wall clock.
- **KD-8 Don't wait for EOF.** The runner waits for sbx's exit (terminal status event + `Popen.wait`), then drains pipes for at most 500 ms and closes them. An escaped orphan holding a pipe cannot pin a concurrency slot (row E10). Measured necessary (M0:15, `item-3a-g.md` 3d): a `setsid` grandchild survives `killpg` of the original group and keeps the inherited stdout/stderr write ends open, so a read-to-EOF blocks until it exits (the probe's first version hung 30 s on exactly that).
- **KD-9 All filesystem work fd-relative.** Workdir creation, materialisation, `out/` collection, skill reads: `os.open(..., dir_fd=...)` with `O_NOFOLLOW`, `fstat` on the opened fd, `S_ISREG`, `st_nlink == 1`. No path string is re-resolved after a script ran.
- **KD-10 Nonce framing.** `<<<SKILL-DATA {nonce} ...>>>` / `<<<END SKILL-DATA {nonce}>>>` with a fresh `secrets.token_hex(8)`, redrawn if the body contains it.
- **KD-11 The index never fails `tools/list`.** Over the ceiling, the description carries the refusal; `load`/`run` refuse with the same message; `list`/`validate` keep working.
- **KD-12 Relist coalesced and rate-bounded.** One in-flight relist per child; within `_RELIST_MIN_INTERVAL_S = 1.0` of the last relist ATTEMPT — successful or failed, the stamp is taken when the attempt starts (R4:L3) — the cache is served; failure serves the last good descriptors (stale is safe; only a changed NAME SET disables).
- **KD-13 Interpreter plans refresh themselves** (maintainability-first KD-9). Signature = `(path, st_ino, st_mtime_ns)` via `os.lstat` of every symlink hop of the interpreter spelling, the realpath, every Mach-O image in the closure, every keg root, every `extra_reads` path. Each `run_script` re-walks the signature (cheap: tens of `lstat`s); on mismatch it recomputes closure + VSZ + self-test under a per-interpreter lock. **One plan term per run (R4:M2)**: `run_one` fixes `plan_deadline = <plan-phase start> + _REFRESH_DEADLINE_S` once and passes it to `PlanCache.get(name, plan_deadline)`; that ONE deadline covers waiting on another run's refresh, any refresh this run then performs itself (after the other refresh was cancelled, or when the signature changed again meanwhile), and any further wait or refresh — it is never re-armed, so a run can spend the 30 s term at most once. An exhausted `plan_deadline` is judged as in Step 12 `PlanCache.get` and KD-17(a): a refresh this run performs with its full term that exhausts the deadline -> `refresh-failed` with reason `refresh deadline`; a term partly spent waiting on another run's refresh -> the run is refused `sandbox_unavailable` ("refresh deadline") with no plan state change and no failure timestamp (R5:L1). Failure -> `PlanState("refresh-failed")`: runs for that interpreter refuse `sandbox_unavailable`, `capabilities` shows `state` + `reason`; the next run retries the refresh. Startup failure still exits 1. **A cancel is not a refresh failure (R3:L4 = S-L1)**: a refresh runs inside the request that triggered it, so a `notifications/cancelled` for that request makes `_spawn_sbx` raise `RequestCancelled` (or kills a spawn already running); `PlanCache.get` re-raises `RequestCancelled` BEFORE its generic failure arm, leaves the plan in its PRIOR state, records no failure timestamp (so the R-11 5 s suppression does not start) and releases the per-interpreter lock — the next run retries the refresh. Otherwise one cancelled request would turn into `sandbox_unavailable` for every caller for 5 s.
- **KD-14 `--read` keeps the spelling** (mvp-first KD-3; FR-2). sbx classifies each spelling once in `main()` (`os.lstat`: symlink/dir/file) and never realpaths; the caller supplies each hop.
- **KD-16 Engine-stage failure after `spawned` is a DECLARED limit, reported as a normal result (C-M5).** Chosen over a status-protocol distinction. Reason: after `execve` of the engine, sbx no longer runs code before the target; telling "sandbox-exec rejected the profile" from "the target printed the same text and exited 1" would require sbx to interpose on the target's stderr (breaking the runner's capped drain, KD-7) or an in-profile sentinel the script could forge. Fail-closed still holds: an engine that fails never runs the target, so nothing ran unconfined. The profile shape a run uses is the shape the startup / refresh self-test proves (same builder, same flags; only the workdir path and the entry differ), so only path-dependent failures remain. Row `mcp_sandbox:I11 engine-failure-after-spawned-is-declared-result` pins the shape (fixture sbx that writes `spawned`, prints `sandbox-exec: ...` to stderr and exits 1 -> result `exitCode: 1`, stderr carries the message, runner not degraded). ADR 0031 records the limit.
- **KD-17 Strictly increasing timeout chain (C-L6; enforced, R2:L6).** Max run wall time (runner-enforced run deadline) = degraded pre-run quarantine retry (`_DEGRADED_RETRY_S = 2.0`, only while degraded, R3:M4) + plan refresh or refresh wait (`_REFRESH_DEADLINE_S = 30.0`, R-11) + `max_timeout_ms` (<= 120 s, decision 12) + 10 s hard-deadline slack + `_KILL_GRACE_S = 2.0` SIGTERM->SIGKILL (the ONE grace, also used by the cancel escalation, R3:L5) + 0.5 s drain + cleanup (`_CLEANUP_DEADLINE_S = 10.0`, which also carries the opportunistic post-run quarantine retry) = **174.5 s**, under the **175 s** bound < runner proxy `call_timeout` **190** < mcp-skill `--runner-timeout-s` **200** (default) < skill proxy `call_timeout` **220**. Every term is ENFORCED, not a target: (a) the refresh itself (VSZ probe + `--version` + self-test) runs against a deadline 30 s from its start — each spawn gets `timeout = remaining`, an exhausted deadline is `refresh-failed` with reason `refresh deadline`; **the term is per RUN, not per refresh (R4:M2)**: one `plan_deadline = <plan-phase start> + _REFRESH_DEADLINE_S` is passed to `PlanCache.get(name, plan_deadline)` and covers the wait on another run's refresh plus any refresh this run performs itself afterwards (the other run was cancelled, or the signature changed again during the wait) — without it, run B could wait 30 s on run A's refresh, see A cancelled, and refresh under a fresh 30 s of its own: 60 s, a 204.5 s bound above the runner proxy's `call_timeout` 190; exhausted = `sandbox_unavailable`; (b) `remove_tree` takes a `deadline` (`_CLEANUP_DEADLINE_S` from the run's cleanup start, shared with the opportunistic quarantine retry, KD-18) and checks it before EVERY mutating operation (M0:9; it replaces "every 256 operations", which measured a 3.18 s overshoot of the 10 s deadline when one rmdir cost ~70 ms, against 13-38 ms with a per-op check, `item-4.md` finding 3 — the per-op check only tightens the 174.5 s bound, which is unchanged); a hit deadline quarantines the workdir (KD-18), never blocks the reply; (c) the degraded pre-run retry (KD-18 step 6) runs against `now + _DEGRADED_RETRY_S` and is the first work after the run deadline is armed, so it always starts with >= 172.5 s of the run deadline left; it never borrows from the refresh, script or cleanup terms (R3:M4 = S-M2). `load_config` refuses `max_timeout_ms > 120000`; the example configs below carry 190 / 200 / 220.
  **Startup bound (R3:M4 = S-M2)**: the runner must answer `initialize` within the runner proxy's `startup_timeout` **120 s** (example config below), or the proxy restarts it and its restart budget eventually DISABLES the child — a permanent DoS. Startup = the work-root sweep under ONE shared deadline `_STARTUP_SWEEP_DEADLINE_S = 20.0` (all leftovers and quarantine entries together; entries not tried when it expires stay quarantined and are retried later) + every interpreter plan (closure, VSZ, `--version`, self-test) under ONE shared `_STARTUP_PLANS_DEADLINE_S = 75.0` (each plan's spawns additionally capped by the 30 s refresh rule; an exhausted plan deadline = refuse start, exit 1, fail closed) = **95 s** + interpreter/import/config/flock time (expected well under 1 s; measured in Step 12), a ~25 s margin under 120. Step 12 measures the three-interpreter startup (expected < 20 s) and ADR 0032 records both numbers beside `startup_timeout`.
- **KD-18 Cleanup is an iterative, fd-relative remover (C-H5 = S-H2).** `shutil.rmtree` is not used (recursion depth on a 10k-level tree; `chmod 000` dirs it cannot enter). `remove_tree(root_fd: int, name: str, abs_root: str, deadline: float) -> bool`: an explicit stack of frames `(parent_fd, name, fd, (st_dev, st_ino))`, never recursion. Steps 1-5 apply per directory before descending (step 5 also per leaf); step 6 applies to the whole workdir once `remove_tree` has given up.
  **Flatten strategy (M0:9, Zoltán's decision 2026-10-11; measured as item 4b, `results.md` "Item 4b", probe `.claude/tmp/m0/flatten/flatten_probe.py`)** — why: on this host every mutating op is priced by its depth (§2 Constraints, `item-4.md`), so a bottom-up remover on a deep chain never meets the deadline (10 000 levels: 445.66 s; 2079 levels: 7.84 s), and a restricted script can still build such a chain despite the depth cap through rename-chaining (`item-depth.md` §4). The remover therefore keeps every mutation at most 50 levels below the workdir:
  (a) **walk a segment** from its top, fd-relative (`O_RDONLY | O_DIRECTORY | O_NOFOLLOW`, steps 1-4 per directory), at most `_FLATTEN_DEPTH = 50` levels deep; the open frames of that walk ARE the fd window, so at most 50 directory fds are open at once (plus the root, quarantine and lock fds) — which also removes the measured EMFILE at depth 252 under a soft `RLIMIT_NOFILE` of 256 that one-fd-per-frame hit (`item-4.md` finding 4);
  (b) **cut**: every subdirectory found at relative depth 50 is LIFTED with one fd-relative rename to a fresh top-level name in the workdir, `os.rename(name, lift, src_dir_fd=parent_fd, dst_dir_fd=wd_fd)`, `lift = ".sbx-flat-%07d" % counter` (the reserved `.sbx*` prefix, C rows; a script may still have created such a name at run time, so on `EEXIST`/`ENOTEMPTY`/`ENOTDIR` the next counter value is tried, bounded by `_RM_MAX_OPS`); **flags first**: an immutable entry cannot be renamed, so on `EPERM` from the cut rename the remover clears flags with `os.lchflags(<spelled path>, 0)` on the entry and on its parent — under the step-3 prefix rule (every stacked frame's `(st_dev, st_ino)` equals `os.lstat` of its spelled prefix immediately before the call) — and retries the rename once; a cut through a `uchg` directory was NOT measured (M0:14);
  (c) repeat (a)-(b) over every top-level segment until each is at most 50 levels deep;
  (d) **remove each segment bottom-up** (leaves `os.unlink(n, dir_fd=fd)`, `os.rmdir(n, dir_fd=parent_fd)` on the way out), then the workdir itself.
  Rules on every path: `fchmod` only when needed (step 4 below); the deadline is checked before EVERY mutating op (KD-17(b)); `_RM_MAX_OPS` bounds the total. Progress is monotone across retries: lifted segments persist under their top-level names, so a quarantined workdir resumes where the last attempt stopped. **Measured (item 4b, rename-chained trees, default modes, no flags/ACLs)**: 2079 levels removed in 0.294 s / 0.278 s (10 436 ops, 41 cuts, 42 segments, max 50 fds), 4059 levels in 0.556 s / 0.550 s (20 376 ops, 81 cuts, 82 segments, max 50 fds), nothing left, no deadline hit — linear at ~0.14 ms per level, against 7.84 s for 2079 levels bottom-up. Not measured: the conditional `fchmod` never fired, no cut met a `uchg`/ACL'd directory, nothing deeper than 4059 levels was built (M0:14).
  1. `os.open(name, O_RDONLY | O_DIRECTORY | O_NOFOLLOW, dir_fd=parent_fd)`;
  2. on `EACCES`/`EPERM`: `os.chmod(name, 0o700, dir_fd=parent_fd, follow_symlinks=False)` (darwin: `fchmodat(AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW)` is supported; Linux, where `os.chmod not in os.supports_follow_symlinks`: `os.stat(name, dir_fd=parent_fd, follow_symlinks=False)` must be `S_ISDIR`, then `os.chmod(name, 0o700, dir_fd=parent_fd)` — race-free there because the PID namespace is gone, no sandboxed writer survives); retry the open;
  3. darwin only, when the chmod itself fails `EPERM` (an immutable flag): Python's `os.chflags` has no `dir_fd`, so `os.lchflags(<abs_root>/<stack spellings>/<name>, 0)` on the spelled path, issued only after every stacked frame's `(st_dev, st_ino)` equals `os.lstat` of its spelled prefix; then retry 2. The same flag clear is applied to a leaf whose `unlink` fails `EPERM`.
  4. **`os.fstat(fd)` on every opened directory fd, then `os.fchmod(fd, 0o700)` BEFORE listing it ONLY when `(st_mode & 0o700) != 0o700` (R2:M3, made conditional by M0:9)** — race-free (both act on the opened inode, never a name). A `0500` directory opens fine and then fails every `unlink` inside it with `EACCES`; a `0100` (search-only) directory fails the open and is handled by step 2, then gets the same check. The owner may always `fchmod` its own inode. Why conditional (`item-4.md` finding 2): `fchmod` is a MUTATION and depth-priced; "always" consumed the whole 10 s walking 2560 levels of the 10k chain and removed nothing, so every retry re-paid it; the `fstat` is constant-cost (~3.7 us) and the safety is the same.
  5. **darwin: strip extended ACLs before giving up (R2:S-H1c)** — when a directory open (after steps 2-3), a leaf `unlink` or a directory `rmdir` still fails `EACCES`/`EPERM`, the entry may carry a deny ACE (`chmod +a "everyone deny delete"`, or `acl_set_file` through `ctypes`, both reachable inside the profile while the write scope permits ACL writes). Strip it with an EMPTY extended ACL, fd-relative where possible: `libc = ctypes.CDLL(None, use_errno=True)` (the process's own namespace; listed, not judged, by `py_deps`, so no `SYSTEM_LIBS` row), `acl = libc.acl_init(0)`, `libc.acl_set_fd_np(fd, acl, ACL_TYPE_EXTENDED)` with `ACL_TYPE_EXTENDED = 0x00000100`, `libc.acl_free(acl)` (`restype`/`argtypes` set explicitly: `acl_init` returns `c_void_p`). Prototypes verified in `/Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/SDKs/MacOSX14.2.sdk/usr/include/sys/acl.h:99-100` (`ACL_TYPE_EXTENDED = 0x00000100`), `:150-151` (`acl_free`, `acl_init`), `:198-199` (`acl_set_fd_np`, `acl_set_file`). For a directory the fd is the opened directory (open it first; a deny of `list` makes the open fail, see below); for a LEAF, `os.open(n, O_RDONLY | O_NOFOLLOW | O_NONBLOCK, dir_fd=fd)` then `acl_set_fd_np` on that fd (a FIFO opens non-blocking; a symlink is never followed, a symlink leaf is unlinked as a link and carries no ACL that matters for its own unlink — it falls to the spelled-path call if needed). **Spelled-path fallback (M0:8, replaces `acl_set_link_np`)**: when the entry cannot be opened at all (its ACL denies `read`/`list`), strip it by spelled path with `chmodx_np(<spelled path>, fsec)` where `fsec = filesec_init()` + `filesec_set_property(fsec, FILESEC_ACL, _FILESEC_REMOVE_ACL)` (`FILESEC_ACL = 5`, `_FILESEC_REMOVE_ACL = (void *)1`; `MacOSX14.2.sdk/usr/include/sys/fcntl.h:585`, `:615`, `:617`, `:620`, `:623`; `chmodx_np` `sys/stat.h:413`) then `filesec_free(fsec)`, or with `acl_set_file(<spelled path>, ACL_TYPE_EXTENDED, acl_init(0))` (`sys/acl.h:199`). Both FOLLOW symlinks, so the step-3 identity rule is applied IMMEDIATELY before the call and extended to the entry itself: every stacked frame's `(st_dev, st_ino)` equals `os.lstat` of its spelled prefix AND `os.lstat(<spelled path>)` is not a symlink and carries the `(st_dev, st_ino)` the remover recorded for that entry. Then retry the failed operation once. **Measured (`item-3l.md` §4)**: a script-set `everyone deny writesecurity` and/or `delete` ACE does NOT block the owner's `acl_set_fd_np`; but ONE broad ACE that also denies `read`/`list` (defeats the open) and `readattr` or `readsecurity` (defeats `acl_set_link_np`, `EACCES`) made the planned pair `acl_set_fd_np` + `acl_set_link_np` fail, while `acl_set_file`, `chmodx_np(_FILESEC_REMOVE_ACL)` and `chmod -N` stripped every case measured — hence the fallback above. With the `file-write-acl` deny (Step 3, M0:8) a script cannot set any of those ACEs in the first place (`item-3l.md` §2), so the strip is defence in depth. The R3:S-M1 STOP condition did NOT fire: an adoptable op exists (`file-write-acl`).
     **Linux is not a vector**: a POSIX ACL cannot deny the file's owner anything beyond the mode bits (the `ACL_USER_OBJ` entry IS the owner's mode bits), the owner resets those with `fchmod`, and `chattr +i` needs `CAP_LINUX_IMMUTABLE`, which bwrap's unprivileged user namespace does not grant over host inodes; the PID namespace is gone, so no writer races the remover. No Linux ACL code.
  6. **Quarantine instead of global degraded (R2:S-H1a, CWE-400/CWE-732)**: when `remove_tree` returns False (an entry still resists after steps 1-5, `_RM_MAX_OPS` hit, or the cleanup deadline of KD-17 hit), the runner moves the whole workdir aside and KEEPS SERVING: `os.rename(name, name, src_dir_fd=root_fd, dst_dir_fd=quarantine_fd)` into `<work_root>/quarantine/` (created 0700 by `prepare_work_root`, opened `O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW` once, `fstat` owner euid + `mode & 0o077 == 0`). If the rename itself fails (e.g. a deny-`delete` ACE on the workdir that step 5 could not strip), the workdir stays in place and is recorded in the in-memory quarantine set by name. The runner goes `degraded = "quarantine full"` (runs refuse `sandbox_unavailable`) ONLY when the quarantine count (entries in `quarantine/` + stuck in-place names) exceeds `_QUARANTINE_MAX = 16` (a module constant, not a config key: it bounds disk, not policy). Retries, every one bounded by ONE shared deadline, never one deadline per entry (R3:M4 = S-M2): (i) at startup `sweep_work_root` walks the leftovers and the quarantine entries against the single `_STARTUP_SWEEP_DEADLINE_S = 20.0` (KD-17 startup bound; entries not reached stay quarantined); (ii) while degraded, before each run, `Quarantine.retry(now + _DEGRADED_RETRY_S)` (2.0 s, a KD-17 run term; the run refuses `sandbox_unavailable` if the count is still above the cap afterwards); (iii) after each run's own cleanup, at most once per 60 s, inside that run's remaining `_CLEANUP_DEADLINE_S`. `remove_tree` checks the deadline it is handed before every mutating operation (M0:9), so a single hostile entry cannot overrun it; a removed entry leaves the count. `capabilities` reports `"quarantine": {"count": N, "max": 16}`.
  Leaves: `os.unlink(n, dir_fd=fd)`; subdirectories pushed (at most 50 deep per segment, flatten (a)); `os.rmdir(n, dir_fd=parent_fd)` on the way out. `O_NOFOLLOW` everywhere: a symlink is unlinked, never followed. Bounded total operations (`_RM_MAX_OPS = 1_000_000`) and the KD-17 cleanup deadline checked before every mutating op; the walk repeats until the workdir is gone or a bound is hit, then step 6 quarantines. The `shutil.rmtree.avoids_symlink_attacks` dependency is dropped. Declared residual (with T11): on darwin a surviving `setsid` orphan could race the step-3 / step-5 spelled-path prefix check; it could redirect a flag clear, an ACL strip or a `chmod 0700` to a path it names, never a read; recorded in ADR 0031 beside the orphan limit. Spelled-path calls cannot work past PATH_MAX (1024; `item-4.md` finding 5); flatten keeps them at most 50 levels below a top-level segment, and an entry they cannot reach resists and is quarantined.
  **Declared limit — hostile depth (M0:14; ADR 0032 limits, Step 20; T19, R-13)**: the depth cap (Step 3) does not bound what rename-chaining builds. A deliberately hostile script can build roughly 1 000 000 levels in a 120 s run (~11 ms per 99-level round sandboxed, `results.md` item 4b; a linear extrapolation, UNMEASURED past 4059 levels). Removal is linear (~0.14 ms/level, item 4b), so such a tree needs ~140 s of removal: cleanup spills into quarantine and is finished over several later retries (startup sweep, degraded pre-run retry, post-run retry), each making monotone progress; the 16-entry quarantine ceiling (step 6) makes repeated abuse VISIBLE as `degraded`. No background reaper: decided, because the only trigger is deliberate hostility of an APPROVED skill. Also untested: a cut through a `uchg` directory (the remover clears flags with `os.lchflags(<spelled>, 0)` before the rename, flatten (b)), a cut through an ACL'd directory, and trees with non-default modes under flatten. Linux has no depth cap (bwrap has no SBPL) and Linux mutation cost by depth is unmeasured; the same flatten remover runs there.
- **KD-15 Cancel classes.** mcp-sandbox = `KILL` (adopts every sbx Popen through `_spawn_sbx` and the reported target pgid; `McpServer._serve`'s re-raising `except asyncio.CancelledError: reclaim.cancel(loop); raise` arm reaches `os.killpg` through `_signal_group`, P15; `_Reclaim.cancel` SIGTERMs each adopted sbx group and SIGKILLs survivors after `_KILL_GRACE_S = 2.0` — it never signals an adopted TARGET pgid, which stays the run thread's post-`wait()` sweep (KD-1), so the R2:L4 recycling window is not widened; all of it lands in Step 7 with the file; workdir removed, or quarantined, in `finally`); mcp-skill = `REPLY_ONLY` (the runner call runs to its own deadline in the worker thread; hanging up is a roadmap item, the proxy already turns a dropped connection into a child cancel).

### Threat model and escape matrix

Attacker capabilities: (a) arbitrary script content + args + stdin + file names (malicious or careless skill author, or a compromised ai-soul request); (b) arbitrary text in household `skills.json`/`SKILL.md`/refs — accidents and prompt injection; (c) a local network peer reaching the loopback ports without the token. Not in scope: a hostile process already running as the same uid (declared, ADR 0032).

Row IDs are always written `<suite>:<row>` (C-L5), because the same letter is reused across suites (e.g. `sbx_restrict:E*` are Seatbelt golden rows, `mcp_sandbox:E*` are live escape rows). Suites: `sbx_restrict` (groups A-F, L, S, T, U, V), `mcp_sandbox` (A-I, E), `mcp_skill` (A-J), `mcp_proxy` (M).

| # | Path | Mitigation (layer) | Red-first row(s) |
|---|---|---|---|
| T1 | Read `~/.ssh`, store root, repos, `/etc/passwd` | restricted reads, no carve-outs (sbx); Linux restricted mode also drops every `shadow_write_denies` `--ro-bind` (sbx:396-397) whose target lies outside every write scope, since on an empty root it would be a READ grant of the repo/plugin trees (C-H4 = S-H1) | `sbx_restrict:L1-L4`, `sbx_restrict:F8`, `sbx_restrict:F10`, `mcp_sandbox:E1-E3` |
| T2 | Existence/size oracle via `stat` | metadata literals on ancestors only, never global (sbx) | `sbx_restrict:L5`, `sbx_restrict:E4`, `mcp_sandbox:E4`, `mcp_sandbox:E32` |
| T3 | Write outside workdir (interpreter keg, work-root siblings, `/tmp`) | one write scope = workdir | `mcp_sandbox:E5`, `mcp_sandbox:E31` |
| T4 | Network TCP / UDP / unix socket | `(deny network*)`; Linux `--unshare-net` + socket tmpfs (existing) | `mcp_sandbox:E6`, `mcp_sandbox:E7` |
| T5 | Exec a planted or foreign binary; exec a setuid binary | process-exec allow-list (KD-6, macOS only); DECLARED (S-L1): every binary under `/bin` and `/usr/bin` is exec-able but runs under the same profile (no network, no mach-lookup, one write scope); a setuid exec is refused by Seatbelt itself (`forbidden-exec-sugid`, sh exit 126; measured M0 3k, `item-3k.md`, M0:10; R2:S-L2; Linux: bwrap `NO_NEW_PRIVS`); DECLARED (R2:L8): Linux has NO exec filter — a script may exec a binary it wrote into the workdir, which runs under the same namespaces, seccomp and rlimits | `sbx_restrict:L6`, `mcp_sandbox:E21` (macOS; INFO on Linux), `mcp_sandbox:E34` |
| T6 | Env leakage (tokens, `MCP_PROXY_TOKEN`, canary) | two-layer clean env; engine env `{}` on both platforms, darwin `/usr/bin/env -i` trampoline (KD-5) | `sbx_restrict:T1-T7`, `mcp_sandbox:D4`, `mcp_sandbox:E16` |
| T7 | `LD_PRELOAD`/`DYLD_INSERT_LIBRARIES` into the unsandboxed engine | engine exec'd with `{}` on both platforms; `--env` refuses `LD_*`/`DYLD_*`; bwrap `--clearenv` | `sbx_restrict:A5`, `sbx_restrict:A6`, `sbx_restrict:T3`, `sbx_restrict:T4`, `sbx_restrict:S9` |
| T8 | fd inheritance (status fd, runner MCP stdio, bwrap mask fds) | `close_fds=True`, `pass_fds=(status_w,)`; sbx child closes the status fd before exec | `sbx_restrict:U4`, `mcp_sandbox:E19`, `mcp_sandbox:E23` |
| T9 | Forge the status record | status fd closed in the child; sbx parent is the only writer; runner ignores unknown shapes | `mcp_sandbox:E23` |
| T10 | Signal the runner / other processes | `(allow signal (target same-sandbox))` only — processes outside the profile (the runner, sbx's unsandboxed parent, anything else) stay unsignalable; `(target self)` was measured too narrow (`kill $!` of the script's own background job EPERM, `item-3a-g.md` 3c; M0:2); whether a CONCURRENT run (its own `sandbox-exec` invocation of the same profile text) counts as "same-sandbox" was not measured, so `E22` also signals a sibling run's target pid and asserts EPERM <!-- UNVERIFIED: same-sandbox scope across two sandbox-exec invocations not measured in M0 -->; Linux PID ns | `mcp_sandbox:E22` |
| T11 | Process-group escape (`setsid`, double fork) | Linux: PID-ns teardown + `--die-with-parent`. macOS: group kill + RLIMIT_CPU + NPROC; the orphan stays inside Seatbelt (irrevocable); DECLARED limit, roadmap sweep | `mcp_sandbox:E10` (macOS: PASS on no-hang, INFO on survival; Linux PASS on death) |
| T11a | **exec -> `spawned` window** (C-L9): sbx is killed after the target exec'd but before the runner has read the `spawned` line, so the runner has no pgid to sweep | the window is the sbx parent's setup-pipe EOF -> one `os.write` of <= 512 bytes; Linux: `--die-with-parent` still tears the PID namespace down; macOS: the target keeps running until its RLIMIT_CPU (`timeout + 1` s CPU) or its own exit — DECLARED limit beside T11 (ADR 0031), roadmap sweep | — (declared; `mcp_sandbox:E33` covers the post-`spawned` case) |
| T12 | pid/pgid reuse between reap and kill | kill-before-reap in sbx (KD-2: Linux `waitid(WNOWAIT)`, darwin kqueue `NOTE_EXIT`, `killpg` with `ESRCH`/`EPERM` ignored, then `waitpid`); runner `killpg` only when sbx died without a terminal event. DECLARED (R2:L4): once sbx has died, the target's group leader is an orphan reaped by init/launchd as soon as it exits, so between that reap and the runner's `killpg(adopted pgid)` the pgid can in principle be recycled (requires a full pid wrap in that window); the runner keeps the window short (it signals right after `Popen.wait()` returns) but cannot close it without being the leader's parent — ADR 0031 limits | `sbx_restrict:U6`, `mcp_sandbox:E9`, `mcp_sandbox:E33` |
| T13 | Runner/sbx killed mid-run leaves target alive | sbx parent traps SIGTERM/SIGINT/SIGHUP -> `killpg(SIGKILL)`; runner escalates SIGTERM -> 2 s -> SIGKILL to sbx, then `killpg(adopted pgid)` if no terminal event (KD-1); Linux `--die-with-parent` | `sbx_restrict:U7`, `mcp_sandbox:I9`, `mcp_sandbox:E33` |
| T14 | Output exhaustion of the server | capped while reading, drain-discard (KD-7); status buffer cap 4 KiB | `mcp_sandbox:E11` (RSS delta < 32 MiB) |
| T15 | Memory/CPU/fork/file/fd bombs | strict AS/CPU/NPROC/FSIZE/NOFILE/CORE (KD-3/4) + wall clock | `mcp_sandbox:E8`, `mcp_sandbox:E12`, `mcp_sandbox:E24-E27` |
| T16 | Disk fill via many files in workdir | FSIZE per file + timeout; free-space precheck `min_free_bytes` (1 GiB); DECLARED, roadmap quota | `mcp_sandbox:H9` |
| T17 | Path tricks in `files[]` (`..`, absolute, `a//b`, `./`, NUL, leading `-`, reserved `out/`/`tmp/`, case/NFD collisions, file-vs-dir prefix) | pure validator + fd-relative `O_EXCL` creation (KD-9) | `mcp_sandbox:C1-C22` |
| T18 | Symlinks/hardlinks/FIFOs/devices in `out/`, `out` replaced by a symlink | `O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK`, `fstat` regular, `st_nlink == 1`, walk bounded (depth 8, 4096 entries) | `mcp_sandbox:E13-E15`, `mcp_sandbox:H4-H7` |
| T19 | Workdir cleanup defeated (`chmod 000`, `0500`/`0100` dirs, nested `chmod 000`, `chflags uchg`, a deny-`delete` ACL via `chmod +a` or `acl_set_file`, 10k-level trees, rename-chained trees deeper than the depth cap, more entries than `_RM_MAX_OPS`) -> permanent runner DoS (R2:S-H1, A04, CWE-400/CWE-732) | iterative fd-relative FLATTEN remover (cut at <= 50 levels, fd-relative lift, segments removed bottom-up, fd window <= 50; M0:9), no recursion, `chmod 0700` on an open refusal + `fchmod(fd, 0o700)` only when `(st_mode & 0o700) != 0o700` (R2:M3, M0:9), darwin flag clear `lchflags(0)` (also before a cut rename) and empty-ACL strip via `acl_set_fd_np`, spelled-path fallback `chmodx_np(_FILESEC_REMOVE_ACL)` / `acl_set_file` with an inode re-check (R2:S-H1c, M0:8), bounded ops + cleanup deadline checked before every mutating op (KD-17/KD-18); `shutil.rmtree` not used; a workdir that still resists is QUARANTINED and the runner keeps serving; `degraded` only when the quarantine holds more than 16 entries (R2:S-H1a); restricted mode denies ACL writes in the write scope at the source with `file-write-acl` ONLY — `file-write-flags` is NOT denied (it breaks `shutil.copy2`/`copytree`; the owner clears `uchg` with `lchflags(0)`, measured) (R2:S-H1b, M0:8); restricted Seatbelt caps directory depth at 100 (M0:9); the M0 3l STOP did not fire (R3:S-M1). DECLARED (M0:14): a rename-chained tree of up to ~1M levels per 120 s run spills its removal into quarantine over several retries; no background reaper | `mcp_sandbox:E17`, `mcp_sandbox:H8`, `mcp_sandbox:H11`-`H19`, `sbx_restrict:E10`, `sbx_restrict:L11`, `sbx_restrict:L12` |
| T20 | Concurrency DoS (incl. refresh storms) | non-blocking slot acquire, `busy` immediately; the slot is taken BEFORE the plan signature check, so refresh self-tests count against the cap (C-M6 = S-M2); pool >= slots + 2 so `capabilities`/cancel still answer | `mcp_sandbox:E18`, `mcp_sandbox:F6`, `mcp_sandbox:G12` |
| T21 | Timeout clamping abuse (`timeoutMs` huge/bool/negative) | int-only (bool refused), >= 1, clamped to `max_timeout_ms` | `mcp_sandbox:C17-C18` |
| T22 | Request/response size blow-up (JSON escapes, text + structuredContent, base64) | config-load worst-case response bound <= 16 MiB (< proxy 64 MiB frame, < ai-soul 32 MiB); mcp-skill picks the smaller serialized encoding per file and refuses a request over `--runner-max-body` BEFORE sending (C-M2b = S-M4) | `mcp_sandbox:A9`, `mcp_skill:J9`, `mcp_skill:J10` |
| T23 | Config/sbx/work-root tampering, two runners on one work root | config & sbx: regular, owner == euid (or root for sbx), not group/world-writable; work root 0700, owned, not a symlink; exclusive `flock` on `<work_root>/.lock` (C-M1) | `mcp_sandbox:A5-A8`, `mcp_sandbox:G4-G5`, `mcp_sandbox:G11` |
| T24 | Mach-O parser abuse (malformed images, cycles, huge `ncmds`, huge kegs) | bounded parser, strict offsets, BFS with visited set and image cap; targeted keg image dirs with a measured cap (C-H3); any parse failure on a required image = refuse start / refresh-failed | `mcp_sandbox:B1-B20` |
| T25 | Index/description prompt injection, control chars, bidi | strict schema; description must equal `_web_clean(desc, "")`, single line, <= 120 code points; preamble frames the index as labels; DECLARED: plain-word semantic injection is not preventable | `mcp_skill:C6`, `mcp_skill:D1-D8` |
| T26 | Skill file symlink/hardlink/TOCTOU, `..`, absolute, nested dirs | fd-relative walk with `O_NOFOLLOW|O_DIRECTORY`; leaf grammar; `st_nlink == 1`; group/world-writable refused | `mcp_skill:F1-F14` |
| T27 | Body forging the frame end marker | nonce framing (KD-10) | `mcp_skill:G3` |
| T28 | Sanitizer gap / drift (third copy) | declared hand copy under canonical names + source-text parity row + behaviour parity row; census shows DECLARED | `mcp_skill:H1`, `mcp_skill:H2`, `mcp_skill:H3-H6` |
| T29 | Token leakage (logs, errors, argv, env) | token only from a 0600 file; never logged; error text names the rule. DECLARED (S-INFO): the runner token IS the boundary — any same-uid holder of it can call `run_script` directly and so bypasses `--allow-private-scripts`; recorded in ADR 0032 under the same-uid declaration | `mcp_skill:I5`, `mcp_skill:I3-I4` |
| T30 | Runner URL redirect/SSRF | loopback LITERAL hosts only — `127.0.0.1` and `::1`, never `localhost`, whose answer depends on the resolver (R2:S-L3, CWE-918) — `http` only, no redirects, response cap 32 MiB | `mcp_skill:I1`, `mcp_skill:I2`, `mcp_skill:I7`, `mcp_skill:I8` |
| T31 | Relist abuse (name-set swap, description flood, list storms incl. against a child whose `tools/list` keeps failing, relist during shutdown) | frozen name set; coalescing + min interval stamped per ATTEMPT, so failing relists are bounded too (R4:L3, CWE-400); existing page/frame caps (`_list_all_tools` mcp-proxy.py:1354); `ChildSlot.stop()` cancels the relist task | `mcp_proxy:M1-M11` |
| T32 | Private-tier scripts run without consent | `--allow-private-scripts` off by default; checked after shadow resolution | `mcp_skill:J3` |
| T33 | Interpreter chosen by the script (shebang, mode bits) | interpreter from the index -> fixed absolute path; entry passed absolute (no option injection) | `mcp_sandbox:C10`, `mcp_skill:J4` |
| T34 | TTY injection (TIOCSTI), `/dev/tty` | child `setsid()` (no controlling tty); no tty read rule; Linux `--new-session` | `mcp_sandbox:E20` |
| T35 | Seatbelt `sysctl-read` blanket (needed by node) | DECLARED info leak (hostname, hw model); no write sysctls. Measured necessary (M0:6, `item-3h-j.md` 3h, `item-6.md`): without it node aborts in V8 (`IsPowerOfTwo(commit_page_size_)`, SIGTRAP); python3 and bash ran without it; the sysctl names were not narrowed, so the rule stays unfiltered | — (ADR 0031 limit) |
| T36 | `/` top-level listing via read-data literal | DECLARED (measured: dyld needs it) | `sbx_restrict:L7` (INFO) |
| T37 | Runner-proxy session exhaustion (`--max-sessions`) by skill-server restarts | mcp-skill `DELETE`s its session on shutdown (best effort); the runner proxy runs with `--session-idle 900` (C-M2 = S-M3) | `mcp_skill:I11` |
| T38 | Engine-stage failure after `spawned` misreported as a script result | DECLARED (KD-16): nothing ran unconfined; the run's profile shape is the one the self-test proves | `mcp_sandbox:I11` |
| T39 | sbx parent writes JSON into an arbitrary inherited fd (`--status-fd` via the Bash tool) | the gate PROMPTS on `--status-fd` (R2:S-L1, CWE-668); only mcp-sandbox, which spawns sbx directly with a fresh pipe, uses it unprompted | `sbx_restrict:S16` |
| T40 | Signal to sbx between `fork()` and handler installation leaves an unsupervised target | SIGTERM/SIGINT/SIGHUP handlers installed BEFORE `fork()`; the child restores `SIG_DFL` before `exec` (R2:L3) | `sbx_restrict:U7` |
| T41 | Quarantine retries stretch startup past the proxy's `startup_timeout` 120 -> crash loop -> the proxy's restart budget disables the child (permanent DoS); a degraded runner's pre-run retry stretches a run past KD-17 (R3:M4 = S-M2, CWE-400) | ONE shared startup-sweep deadline (20 s) and ONE shared startup-plans deadline (75 s), 95 s < 120; untried entries stay quarantined; the degraded pre-run retry is a fixed 2 s KD-17 term (174.5 s <= 175 s) | `mcp_sandbox:H17`, `mcp_sandbox:H18` |
| T42 | A cancel during a plan refresh marks the interpreter refresh-failed and the R-11 suppression refuses every caller for 5 s (R3:L4 = S-L1); a waiter whose refresh owner was cancelled spends a SECOND 30 s refresh term, 204.5 s > the runner proxy `call_timeout` 190 (R4:M2) | `RequestCancelled` re-raised before the failure arm; plan state unchanged, no failure timestamp; one per-run `plan_deadline` covers the wait and any own refresh | `mcp_sandbox:G13` |

### Risk Mitigation
- **R-1 Homebrew dylib closure edge cases** (`@rpath` against the loader chain's `LC_RPATH` stack, `@loader_path` of the LOADING image, `@executable_path` of the main executable, re-exports, weak dylibs missing, kegs moving under `brew upgrade`) -> rpath stack per load chain; weak missing skipped, strong missing refused; extension modules inside each added keg walked too (python `_ssl`/`_hashlib` pull `openssl@3`) — through TARGETED image directories, not a blanket keg walk (C-H3: the python keg is ~82 MB and `lib/python3.14/test` alone has 513 entries; node carries `lib/node_modules/npm`), see Step 8; `brew upgrade` handled by KD-13 self-refresh (no restart).
- **R-2 Timeout/kill semantics differ per platform** -> one supervisor code path; only WHO is killed differs; macOS orphan survival declared (T11); Linux live rows INFO.
- **R-3 macOS AS for bash may be loose** (bash VSZ << launcher VSZ) -> `capabilities` reports `effectiveBudgetBytes`; M0 measures it; if > 8 GiB, ADR 0031 records it and the roadmap holds the trampoline fallback. **Measured, NOT triggered (M0:11, `item-2.md`)**: idle VSZ ~32.5 GiB for python3, node, both bashes and the sbx-sized launcher (the launcher within ~32 MiB of python3 and bash); `effectiveBudgetBytes` python3 1.279 GiB, node 1.250 GiB, bash 1.267 (Homebrew) / 1.268 (system) GiB — ~1.27 GiB; deferred item 11 stays deferred.
- **R-4 node under Linux absolute 1 GiB AS may not start** -> the startup self-test fails closed; `"as_mode": "baseline"` config switch. <!-- GAP: Linux baseline VSZ measurement (/proc/<pid>/status VmSize of the bwrap-inner target) not verifiable offline; implement behind as_mode, INFO-tested -->
- **R-5 `os.waitid` cannot be relied on for darwin** (M0:1: measured ABSENT on python3 3.9.21 but PRESENT on 3.14.0, `item-1.md` — Python >= 3.13 exposes it on darwin; and even where present, the kqueue path is the chosen darwin mechanism, selected by `sys.platform == "darwin"`, never by `hasattr(os, "waitid")`) -> RESOLVED by design, not a fallback: darwin observes the exit with kqueue `KQ_FILTER_PROC`/`KQ_NOTE_EXIT`, which reports the exit without reaping, so the leader stays an unreaped zombie and its pgid stays reserved while `killpg` sweeps; `EPERM` from `killpg` (macOS's answer for a zombie-only group) is treated like `ESRCH`; `waitpid` reaps last (KD-2). A `waitpid(WNOHANG)` poll is NOT a fallback: it reaps on success, which is exactly the reuse window KD-2 closes. If M0 item 1 shows `NOTE_EXIT` unusable, M1 stops and the design is re-opened (no silent downgrade). M0 item 1: usable on 3.14 and 3.9, STOP not met (M0:1).
- **R-1a Framework launcher re-exec (C-H2)**: Homebrew's `/usr/local/bin/python3` -> `../Cellar/python@3.14/3.14.0_1/bin/python3` resolves to `.../Frameworks/Python.framework/Versions/3.14/bin/python3.14`, a pythonw-style launcher that `posix_spawn(POSIX_SPAWN_SETEXEC)`s `.../Versions/3.14/Resources/Python.app/Contents/MacOS/Python` (verified on this host 2026-10-10: launcher present, 12544-byte `Python.app/Contents/MacOS/Python`, 5.3 MiB framework image `Versions/3.14/Python`). Under a filtered `process-exec` that second exec is denied unless listed. Rule in `plan_interpreter`: when the realpath lies inside a `*.framework` directory, add `<framework>/Versions/<v>/Resources/Python.app/Contents/MacOS/Python` to `execs` (and its hops to `reads`) and add the framework's main image `<framework>/Versions/<v>/Python` as an extra closure ROOT; images are recognised by Mach-O magic, never by file extension. The seed `min-py-final.sb` used an unfiltered `process-exec`, so it never exercised this path; M0 item 3i runs python3 under the exec filter via the real launch path. **Measured FAIL-then-PASS (M0:3, `item-3h-j.md` 3i)**: hops + realpath only -> exit 1; + the `Python.app/Contents/MacOS/Python` literal -> `print(1)` runs. The refusal surfaces as `python3: posix_spawn: <Python.app path>: Undefined error: 0` (errno 0), NOT `Operation not permitted`, so no code or row may key on the EPERM text. The closure-ROOT half (`Versions/<v>/Python`) was not separately measured (it sat inside the keg read subpath); `mcp_sandbox:B18`/`B19` cover it.
- **R-6 Sanitizer drift** -> source-text parity row `H1` + behaviour row `H2`; census lists the copy as DECLARED.
- **R-7 NPROC delta race** -> count taken in the sbx parent immediately before fork; more user processes = less headroom (fails safe); concurrent runs share one per-uid counter. Declared: a fork bomb can starve the user's other processes up to the timeout (decision 11).
- **R-8 Proxy relist vs ADR 0027 "relays, never composes"** -> relist forwards the child's own `tools/list` verbatim; opt-in per child; default byte-identical (row M4 + the existing 112).
- **R-9 Linux unverified pieces** (`--clearenv` in bwrap 0.6.1, usr-merge symlinks, NPROC under userns, seccomp allow-list vs node/python, binds under symlinked ancestors) -> golden argv offline, `liveVerified: false`, INFO rows, roadmap item.
- **R-10 Concurrent agents editing shared files** -> PC-2 (hunk-level, `git apply --cached`); counts updated in the same commit as rows.
- **R-11 Plan refresh thrash** (a file in the closure touched repeatedly) -> refresh under a per-interpreter lock; a refresh younger than 5 s is not repeated for the same signature; runs during a refresh wait on the lock until their own per-run `plan_deadline` (plan-phase start + 30 s, R4:M2) then refuse `sandbox_unavailable`; a run whose wait ends with the other refresh cancelled (or a second signature change) refreshes itself under the REMAINDER of the same `plan_deadline`, never a fresh 30 s. A waiting run already HOLDS its run slot (C-M6), so refresh storms are bounded by `max_concurrent`; the one 30 s plan term (wait + own refresh together) is part of the KD-17 run bound. A refresh ended by a CANCEL is not a failure and does not start the 5 s suppression (R3:L4, KD-13; row `mcp_sandbox:G13`).
- **R-12 Smoke/footprint startup budget (C-M4)** -> the smoke harness waits `READ_TIMEOUT = 8.0` s per response line (`Scripts/_mcp_smoke_test.py:139`), and mcp-sandbox computes plans before it answers `initialize`. The harness config has ONE interpreter, `bash -> /bin/bash` (system path: no Mach-O closure, no keg), so startup is one VSZ probe + one version run + one self-test; M0 item 6 measures it. If it exceeds 6 s, the exception is documented as a per-entry `read_timeout` key in `SERVERS` (a harness change named in Step 14), never by raising the global value. **M0 estimate (M0:11, `item-6.md`, loaded host)**: the raw `sandbox-exec` planning spawns cost median 78.6 ms (cold 118.0 ms) for `/bin/bash`, 172.2 ms python3, 214.9 ms node; the three-interpreter set median 465.6 ms, worst 592.6 ms — well under 6 s, so NO per-entry `read_timeout` is planned (the global 8.0 s suffices), subject to the Step 12 re-measurement of the real runner (task-066: + its own start, the Mach-O walk, the sweep).
- **R-13 Hostile directory depth (M0:9, M0:14)** -> restricted Seatbelt caps `file-write-create` at 100 levels under the workdir (Step 3); rename-chaining still builds deeper trees (99-deep carrier renamed under a 99-deep parent: depth 2079 in 237 ms sandboxed, `item-depth.md` §4), so the remover flattens (KD-18, linear ~0.14 ms/level, item 4b). DECLARED: ~1M levels per 120 s run are buildable (extrapolated, unmeasured); removal then spills over several quarantine retries and the 16-entry ceiling surfaces repeated abuse as `degraded`; no background reaper (decided: the trigger is deliberate hostility of an approved skill only); a cut through a `uchg` directory is untested — ADR 0032 limits (Step 20).

### Testing Strategy
- Red-first per milestone: write the group, run it, record RED (each row fails for its stated reason, not ImportError), then implement. ADRs record red counts.
- Offline rows are PURE where possible (builders, validators, Mach-O parser on synthetic bytes, config loader, gate grammar) and run on both OSes.
- Live Seatbelt rows run on macOS; on Linux they record INFO (counts stable across hosts, the `sbx_seccomp` precedent `tests/run.py:280-283`).
- **Nested-sandbox INFO path (R2:M8)**: every live Seatbelt group adopts `sbx_gate`'s control (P23, `tests/test_sbx_gate.py:1523-1531`): one control run of the real sbx with a trivial restricted invocation first; no result plus `"sandbox_apply"` in its stderr = a nested Seatbelt (e.g. Claude Code's own command sandbox) that refuses to apply ANY profile, so every row of that group is recorded INFO with the stderr's first 200 chars — counted, never omitted, never FAIL. Applies to `sbx_restrict` groups L, U, V and rows T1, T2, T7 (R3:L2: T1 and T2 RUN a target — T1 asserts the exact environment a real python3/bash/node sees, T2 that a canary in sbx's own env never reaches it — so they are live, macOS PASS / Linux INFO, not pure; T3-T6 are pure golden/refusal rows); `mcp_sandbox` groups E, I, G and rows F6, H15, H16 (both run real scripts through sbx) (for the runner the control is run BEFORE the runner is started, since a nested sandbox makes its startup self-test fail with exit 1); `mcp_skill` row J1. The offline rows of the same suites are unaffected.
- Escape fixtures never touch real secrets: canary files under `.claude/tmp/<suite>-canary-<rand>/` and a canary env var; the real `~/.ssh/known_hosts` row only asserts denial, never prints content.
- Every live row asserts the work root is empty afterwards ("empty" = only `.lock` and an empty `quarantine/`, R2:S-H1a; a row that quarantines on purpose, `H15`/`H16`, empties it itself).

Live evidence is restored by rows: `mcp_sandbox` `F9 handler-crash-live-jsonrpc-iserror-keeps-serving` (141 -> **142**, F 8 -> 9), and `mcp_skill` group K (75 -> **77**): `K1 handler-crash-internal-and-log-exception` (in-process, mcp_sandbox F7's shape) and `K2 handler-crash-live-jsonrpc-iserror-keeps-serving`. F9 and K2 drive a framed `tools/call` through `McpServer.run()` on gated in-memory stdin/stdout, following mcp_search group G's `run-catch-all-live-32603`. > **IMPL: security round 1 follow-up (2026-10-11)** — the gated security review of the working tree (`.claude/tmp/security-verdicts-20261011-1750.md`, verdict REVISE) was fixed in one round; every added row was run red first. **Gate** (`ClaudeCode/hooks/sbx-gate.py`): the raw command must be printable ASCII + TAB and is split on `[ \t]+` only (G1), every word must match `[A-Za-z0-9._/=+:,@%~-]+` and `[ ]` joined `METACHARS` (G2), a `~` is only a bare `~` or a leading `~/` (G3), `--rlimit-core` auto-allows only `0` (R1), `--rlimit-nproc` only an absolute `N <= 512` (R2) — `sbx_gate` 105 -> **128**. **sbx**: `floor_cwd` refuses a cwd or its realpath carrying `_INJECT_CHARS` (now every C0 control and DEL) with exit 3 in both modes and under `--dry-run`, and `_sbpl_path` is the one refusal point for every interpolated SBPL path (P1); `socket_read_refusal` checks every hop and the realpath of a `--read`/`--exec` against `SOCKET_REFUSED_DIRS` (`SOCKET_DIRS` + `/var/run`) and refuses `S_ISSOCK`, exit 2, on both OSes (P2); `_strict_rlimits` refuses a value above a finite soft limit, exit 3 (R1); the `--write` tilde rule mirrors the gate's (G3); the `--env` help says values travel in argv (P4); `_write_status` halves the reason and never slices the encoded line (M1) — `sbx_restrict` group X, 104 -> **113**. **mcp-sandbox**: the darwin fallbacks are fd-relative only (`setattrlistat` + `FSOPT_NOFOLLOW` on the parent fd, `fchflags`/`acl_set_fd_np` on the parent; the spelled-path `lchflags`/`chmodx_np`/`acl_set_file`, `_strip_acl_spelled` and `_dirent_inode` removed; W1/W2); `_sweep_survivors` kills the run's session and cwd-in-tree euid processes before every removal and quarantine retry, `_SWEEP_ROUNDS` 5, inside the cleanup deadline (W6); the closure walk is capped (`_RPATH_STACK_MAX` 256, `_KEG_SCAN_MAX_DIRS` 4096, `_KEG_SCAN_MAX_DEPTH` 32, `_KEG_DIR_MAX_ENTRIES` 4096) and checks cancel and the plan deadline per image and directory (C1); `collect_out` lists while scanning and `_Remover.list_dir` reads in `_RM_LIST_BATCH` 1024 batches holding at most `_RM_PENDING_MAX` 65536 (Q1); an sbx refusal answers the fixed `sbx refused the run (exit N)` and logs the raw line only (O2); the A9 reply bound prices a byte at `_REPLY_BYTE_WORST` 13 and an `out/` path at `_OUT_PATH_MAX_BYTES` 512 — `mcp_sandbox` A11, B21-B23, H20-H24, 142 -> **151**. W4 was measured instead of fixed: Seatbelt refuses a hard link from an outside user-owned file into the workdir (`EPERM`) in restricted, legacy and default mode, macOS 14.2.1. **mcp-skill**: an index entry holds at most `_INDEX_MAX_SCRIPTS` 64 scripts, a duplicate path is refused, and the check is fstat-only (`scripts-too-many`, `script-duplicate`; K3); the bundle is listed with a bounded `scandir` (K4); `_RUNNER_MAX_RESPONSE` is the runner's 16 MiB bound + 64 KiB framing (M2); the run answer is held to `_RUN_ANSWER_MAX_CHARS` 100000 (ADR 0013 verbatim class) with head-kept frames, cut lines outside the frame and at most 64 out-file frames (M2); a runner refusal renders `the runner refused the run (<code>).` with the message only in a `kind=runner-error` frame (O2) — `mcp_skill` B9, B10, I12, J11, J12, J13, 77 -> **83**. **mcp-proxy**: `--require-token-file` (HTTP-only) refuses a set `MCP_PROXY_TOKEN` even beside `--token-file` and requires `--token-file`, exit 2; the runner launch line passes it (T2) — `mcp_proxy` J50, 123 -> **124**. **Declared by decision, not fixed**: T1, the runner token sent to whatever listens on the loopback port (a cross-uid squat while the runner proxy is down; a unix-socket transport is a roadmap item), and T3, `sbx`'s fixed deny set (the runner token file belongs under `~/.claude`), both in ADR 0032. L1 (the fleet-wide `--log-file` `O_NOFOLLOW` gap in `Scripts/_mcp_logging.py`) and C2 (the 512 vs 256 image/read cap mismatch) are outside this round. Recorded in ADR 0031, ADR 0032, the ADR 0027 addendum and the component pages.
