All numbers are planned; the final number of every suite is fixed at its red run. **The two new suites grow step by step, and each step that adds a group moves the typed count in `tests/run.py` with it (R4:L4)** — a stale count is CASE COUNT DRIFT, a hard failure (`tests/run.py:811`): `sbx_restrict` 31 (Step 2) -> 51 (Step 3) -> 75 (Step 4) -> 91 (Step 5) -> 103 (Step 6) (M0:9: +L12 in Step 6; the "without E10/L11" alternatives are gone, M0:8); `mcp_sandbox` 18 (Step 7) -> 38 (Step 8) -> 60 (Step 9) -> 79 (Step 10) -> 94 (Step 11) -> 107 (Step 12) -> 141 (Step 13) (M0:9: +H19 in Step 10); `mcp_skill` 30 (Step 16) -> 54 (Step 17) -> 75 (Step 18) (R5:M1). A row written red in one step and turned green in a later one (R4:M3) counts from the step that writes it. **The `mcp_sandbox` suite — and therefore `forge_call test all` — is EXPECTED FAILING from Step 7 through Step 11 with exactly the rows recorded RED (`F5`, `F6`, `H15`, `H16`, `H18`, `I1`-`I11`), and green from Step 12 on; any other failing row in that window is a real failure, and the fleet suites stay green in every commit (R5:L2).**

### Error handling, config & launch shape

**mcp-sandbox: one JSON config file, no policy flags** (no two spellings of one setting, ADR 0015). CLI: `--config PATH` (required) plus the fleet logging flags. The config file must be regular, owner == euid, `mode & 0o022 == 0`; unknown keys at every level refused; duplicate keys refused; every numeric key range-checked; worst-case response computed.

**Strict JSON on every parse (R2:L10, P24)**: in both new hosts every `json.loads` goes through the generated `_strict_loads` (finite numbers only, bounded int literals, every refusal a `json.JSONDecodeError`) — the sbx status lines and every runner HTTP body (application/json and each SSE `data:` payload) included — and every frame write through `_strict_dumps`. The two parses that must ALSO refuse duplicate keys — mcp-sandbox's config and mcp-skill's `skills.json` — call `_strict_loads(text)` first and then ONE host-local helper `_refuse_duplicate_keys_parse(text)` = `json.loads(text, object_pairs_hook=_no_duplicate_keys)` over text `_strict_loads` already accepted; that single bare parse per host is declared in `STRICT_JSON_EXCEPTIONS` (`tests/test_generated_region.py:428`), in the `mcp-proxy.py` `load_config` precedent's shape (:445-447):
```python
("mcp-sandbox.py", "_refuse_duplicate_keys_parse", "loads"):
    "a second pass over the operator's config text _strict_loads already "
    "accepted, only to refuse duplicate keys; _strict_loads takes no "
    "object_pairs_hook",
("mcp-skill.py", "_refuse_duplicate_keys_parse", "loads"):
    "a second pass over a tier's skills.json text _strict_loads already "
    "accepted, only to refuse duplicate keys; _strict_loads takes no "
    "object_pairs_hook",
```
No other bare parse exists in either host. mcp-skill's request-body serialisation for the runner (`fn_run`, C-M2b) uses `_strict_dumps` semantics with `separators=(",", ":"), ensure_ascii=False` — written as `json.dumps(obj, separators=(",", ":"), ensure_ascii=False, allow_nan=False)` — this ONE spelling everywhere the body is serialized, both for the size check and for the bytes `_post` sends (Step 18, R4:L4) — outside the frame tier, which group H does not gate (`strict_violations` skips an emit outside `McpServer`, :537-539).
```json
{
  "version": 1,
  "sbx": "/Users/zoltan.ponekker/.claude/skills/p/skills/sandbox-run/scripts/sbx",
  "work_root": null,
  "seccomp": "auto",
  "log_content": false,
  "interpreters": {
    "python3": {"path": "/usr/local/bin/python3"},
    "bash":    {"path": "/usr/local/bin/bash"},
    "node":    {"path": "/usr/local/bin/node", "extra_reads": ["/usr/local/etc/openssl@3/openssl.cnf"]}
  },
  "limits": {
    "timeout_ms": 30000, "max_timeout_ms": 120000,
    "as_budget_bytes": 1073741824, "as_mode": "baseline",
    "nproc_headroom": 64, "fsize_bytes": 16777216, "nofile": 256,
    "stdout_bytes": 65536, "stderr_bytes": 65536,
    "max_files": 64, "max_files_bytes": 4194304,
    "max_out_files": 16, "max_out_bytes": 1048576,
    "max_stdin_bytes": 1048576, "max_args": 64, "max_arg_chars": 4096,
    "max_concurrent": 2, "min_free_bytes": 1073741824
  }
}
```
node's `extra_reads` entry is MEASURED necessary (M0:6, `item-3h-j.md` 3h): without a read of `/usr/local/etc/openssl@3/openssl.cnf` node exits 1 (`OpenSSL configuration error ... fopen(/usr/local/etc/openssl@3/openssl.cnf, rb)`); `--version` does not touch it, `-e` does (`item-6.md`). The literal was sufficient in 3h; item 6 granted the whole `/usr/local/etc/openssl@3` directory — if node's self-test still fails on a host, widen this entry to that directory, nothing wider. node also needs `(allow sysctl-read)` and `/dev/null` read+write, both emitted by restricted sbx itself (Step 3; T35, M0:5). `work_root: null` selects the A6 default; EVERY test, smoke and footprint config sets `work_root` explicitly to a directory under `.claude/tmp/` (C-M1). Ranges: `timeout_ms` 100..`max_timeout_ms`; `max_timeout_ms` 100..120000 (decision 12's 120 s maximum; with the 2 s degraded pre-run retry, the enforced 30 s refresh deadline, 12.5 s kill/drain slack and 10 s cleanup deadline the run bound is 174.5 s <= 175 s, KD-17; R2:L6, R3:M4); `as_budget_bytes` 64 MiB..16 GiB; `as_mode` "baseline" (macOS forced) | "absolute" (Linux default); `nproc_headroom` 8..512; `fsize_bytes` 1 MiB..1 GiB; `nofile` 32..4096; output caps 1 KiB..4 MiB; `max_files` 1..256; `max_files_bytes` <= 16 MiB; `max_out_files` 0..64; `max_out_bytes` <= 4 MiB; `max_stdin_bytes` <= 4 MiB; `max_args` <= 256; `max_arg_chars` <= 65536; `max_concurrent` 1..8; `seccomp` "auto" (on iff Linux x86_64) | "on" | "off". Worst case `2 * (6 * (stdout + stderr) + 6 * max_out_bytes + 4096 * max_out_files + 8192)` must be <= 16 MiB, else ConfigError.

**Error contract** (`sandbox_call` result, ADR 0010 top-level `"error"` predicate):
```json
{"error": {"code": "invalid_params", "message": "files[3].path: '..' component refused"}}
{"error": {"code": "invalid_params", "message": "Unknown function 'run_scrip'. Did you mean 'run_script'? Available: run_script, capabilities"}}
{"error": {"code": "busy", "message": "2 runs in flight (max_concurrent=2)", "retryAfterMs": 1000}}
{"error": {"code": "sandbox_unavailable", "message": "interpreter node: plan refresh failed (MachOError: ...)"}}
{"error": {"code": "internal", "message": "<ExceptionType>"}}
```

**sbx exit/status contract**: exit 0 dry-run; 2 usage (incl. every new flag's value refusal); 3 fail-closed (engine missing, builder ValueError, strict setrlimit refused, uid process count failed, exec of engine failed); 124 timeout (fork mode); 128+N target killed by signal N; else the target's exit code. Status fd lines:
```json
{"sbx": 1, "event": "spawned", "pid": 12345, "pgid": 12345}
{"sbx": 1, "event": "exit", "outcome": "exited", "code": 0, "signal": null, "durationMs": 812}
{"sbx": 1, "event": "exit", "outcome": "signaled", "code": null, "signal": 9, "durationMs": 40}
{"sbx": 1, "event": "exit", "outcome": "timeout", "code": null, "signal": 9, "durationMs": 30004}
{"sbx": 1, "event": "exit", "outcome": "refused", "code": 3, "signal": null, "durationMs": 0, "reason": "setrlimit RLIMIT_AS refused (ValueError)"}
```
A `refused` outcome is written without a preceding `spawned` (nothing ran).

**Token file convention**: `~/mcp/tokens/` 0700; one file per proxy instance, 0600, >= 32 chars and >= 8 distinct (mcp-proxy.py `_TOKEN_MIN_LEN`/`_TOKEN_MIN_DISTINCT`), generated with `python3 -c "import secrets; print(secrets.token_urlsafe(36))"`.

**Example `~/mcp` proxy configs** (documentation only; deployment is Zoltán's):

Runner proxy `~/mcp/sandbox-proxy.json`:
```json
{
  "root": "/Users/zoltan.ponekker/mcp/sandbox-root",
  "scripts": "/Users/zoltan.ponekker/mcp/Scripts",
  "children": [
    {"name": "sandbox", "command": "python3",
     "args": ["-I", "-B", "{scripts}/mcp-sandbox.py", "--config", "/Users/zoltan.ponekker/mcp/sandbox.json"],
     "call_timeout": 190, "startup_timeout": 120}
  ]
}
```
Launch: `python3 ~/mcp/Scripts/mcp-proxy.py --config ~/mcp/sandbox-proxy.json --http --port 3010 --token-file ~/mcp/tokens/sandbox.token --max-body-bytes 8388608 --session-idle 900` (`--session-idle` verified in the proxy's HTTP argparse group, `Scripts/mcp-proxy.py:2928`, default `_HTTP_SESSION_IDLE_S = 86400.0` :426, range-checked at :809; 900 s bounds the sessions of skill servers that die without their shutdown `DELETE`, C-M2 = S-M3). Timeout chain (KD-17): run bound 175 s < this `call_timeout` 190 < mcp-skill `--runner-timeout-s` 200 < the skill proxy's `call_timeout` 220. `startup_timeout: 120` is above the runner's bounded startup (sweep 20 s + plans 75 s = 95 s, KD-17 "Startup bound", R3:M4); lowering it below ~100 s re-opens the crash-loop of T41.

Per-uid skill proxy `~/mcp/skill-proxy-<uid>.json`:
```json
{
  "root": "/Users/zoltan.ponekker/mcp/skill-root",
  "scripts": "/Users/zoltan.ponekker/mcp/Scripts",
  "children": [
    {"name": "skill", "command": "python3", "relist": true,
     "args": ["-I", "-B", "{scripts}/mcp-skill.py",
              "--household-root", "/Users/zoltan.ponekker/ai-soul/store/skills/household",
              "--private-root", "/Users/zoltan.ponekker/ai-soul/store/skills/private/<uid>",
              "--runner-url", "http://127.0.0.1:3010/mcp",
              "--runner-token-file", "/Users/zoltan.ponekker/mcp/tokens/sandbox.token",
              "--runner-timeout-s", "200", "--runner-max-body", "8388608"],
     "call_timeout": 220}
  ]
}
```
<!-- UNVERIFIED: ai-soul store paths are illustrative -->

**mcp-skill flags**: `--household-root DIR`, `--private-root DIR` (either may be absent), `--allow-private-scripts`, `--max-description N` (default 4096, range 512..4096, UTF-16 code units — never above ai-soul's 4096 cut, decision 6; R2:M5), `--runner-url URL` (scheme `http`, host exactly `127.0.0.1` or `[::1]`, never `localhost`; R2:S-L3), `--runner-token-file PATH`, `--runner-timeout-s S` (default 200; range 20..900; must stay below the skill proxy's `call_timeout` and above the runner proxy's, KD-17), `--runner-max-body N` (default 8388608 = the runner proxy's `--max-body-bytes`; range 65536..67108864; C-M2b = S-M4), fleet logging flags. `--runner-url` without `--runner-token-file` (or vice versa) = usage error exit 2. Without a runner, `run` answers isError `sandbox_unavailable` ("runner not configured").

### Deferred to roadmap (one deduplicated list; each item names its ADR)
1. Dedicated sandbox OS uid for the runner (Linux deployment) — ADR 0031/0032 (decision 11).
2. Linux live verification on 192.168.2.245 with Zoltán's OK, incl. node under an absolute 1 GiB AS, `--clearenv` in bwrap 0.6.1, NPROC under userns; flips `liveVerified` — ADR 0031.
3. Canonical sanitizer home (a fifteenth source) replacing the declared hand copy + parity rows — ADR 0032.
4. Canonical MCP Streamable HTTP client, lifted when a second host appears — ADR 0032.
5. macOS `setsid` orphan sweep (clean only with the dedicated uid) — ADR 0031.
6. Per-request token re-read in mcp-proxy (spec "preferably") — ADR 0027 addendum.
7. Debug content-logging switch beyond `log_content` (spec allows a debug switch; v1 logs metadata only) — ADR 0032.
8. Skill-proposal Claude Code skill — ai-soul's `skill_propose` schema has been received (`.claude/tmp/r0020/skill-propose-schema.md`, R4:CONTRACT); the skill itself stays out of v1.
9. mcp-skill hang-up on cancel (REPLY_ONLY -> close the runner connection) — ADR 0032.
10. Per-run disk quota and RSS watchdog — ADR 0031.
11. bash AS trampoline, only if M0 shows the bash effective budget > 8 GiB — ADR 0031. (M0:11: measured ~1.27 GiB, not triggered; stays a `later` roadmap item.)
12. **PULLED INTO v1 (R2:S-H1b; decided by M0 3l, M0:8)**: Seatbelt deny of ACL writes in the write scope, `(deny file-write-acl (subpath <write scope>))`, in restricted mode, removing the `chmod +a` / `acl_set_file` cleanup vectors at their source (Step 3, rows `sbx_restrict:E10`, `sbx_restrict:L11`). Measured (`item-3l.md`): `file-write-acl` stops both ACL vectors (EPERM) and every self-test (python3, the plan one-liner, bash, node) passes with it; `file-write-flags` would stop `chflags uchg` but is NOT denied, because it breaks `shutil.copy2` / `shutil.copytree` (copystat's `chflags` EPERM) while the owner clears `uchg` with `os.lchflags(p, 0)` in every case measured; `file-write-xattr` stops neither vector; `file-write-mode` breaks `chmod` (never adopted). Item 12 is no longer a roadmap item — ADR 0031.
13. Close the exec -> `spawned` window on macOS (T11a), e.g. by having the sbx parent pass the pgid to the runner before the exec — NOT in v1. Risk while deferred: an sbx killed inside that window leaves the target running until its RLIMIT_CPU or exit — ADR 0031.
14. Unix-socket transport for the runner proxy (security finding T1, added at close-out 2026-10-11): mcp-skill sends its bearer token to whatever listens on the loopback runner port, so a cross-uid process that squats the port first receives the token; the squat is declared in ADR 0032. A unix socket with filesystem permissions removes it — ADR 0032.
15. Pre-check the node closure against sbx's read cap (finding C2, added at close-out 2026-10-11): mcp-sandbox admits a closure of up to `_CLOSURE_IMAGES_MAX` = 512 images, but sbx refuses more than `_READ_CAP` = 256 `--read` paths, so a keg with roughly 250+ native modules makes node unplannable, surfacing only as sbx's `too many --read entries` usage error far from its cause. Check the computed reads against 256 in mcp-sandbox and fail the plan with a named error — ADR 0031/0032.

---

## 6. Step-by-Step Plan

Milestones: **M0** measurements (Step 1) · **M1** sbx (Steps 2-6) · **M2** mcp-sandbox (Steps 7-14) · **M3** proxy relist (Step 15) · **M4** mcp-skill (Steps 16-19) · **M5** docs/ADRs/roadmap (Step 20). In every step the red rows are written FIRST, run red, then implemented. M3 is independent of M1/M2 and may run in parallel.

### Step 1: M0 — measurements that decide the design forks (and preconditions)
**Files**: `.claude/tmp/m0/` (create; probe scripts + `results.md`, temp only); reads `ClaudeCode/skills/sandbox-run/scripts/sbx:1130-1196`, `.claude/tmp/rlimit-probe/probe.py` (seed), `.claude/tmp/seatbelt-probe/min-py-final.sb` (seed)
**Dependencies**: none
**Description**: Confirm PC-1 (sbx `/dev/null` hunk committed; `git status` shows sbx clean) before M1. Then run (via `p:minion-builder`, never on 192.168.2.245):
1. kqueue `NOTE_EXIT` (KD-2, darwin): for `/usr/local/bin/python3` and `python3.9` if present, record `hasattr(os, "waitid")` (expected `False` on macOS) and `hasattr(select, "kqueue")`; then fork a child that `setsid()`s, spawns a sleeping grandchild, and `_exit(0)`s; register `KQ_FILTER_PROC`/`KQ_NOTE_EXIT` on the child pid; observe the event; assert the child is still unreaped (`ps -o stat= -p <pid>` shows `Z`); `os.killpg(pid, SIGKILL)` kills the grandchild; record the errno `killpg` returns once only the zombie remains (expected `EPERM`); `waitpid` reaps. Also the ESRCH-on-registration case (child exits before `kevent` is added). A failure stops M1 (R-5).
2. VSZ baselines (`/bin/ps -o vsz= -p PID`, KiB) of python3, node, `/usr/local/bin/bash`, `/bin/bash`, and of an idle `python3 -I -B sbx`-sized process. Decides R-3.
3. Under a restricted SBPL (seed `min-py-final.sb` + process-exec filter + `(allow process-fork)` + `(allow signal (target self))`): (a) python `subprocess.run(["/bin/sh","-c","echo ok"])` works with `/bin` exec-allowed; (b) exec of a binary copied into the workdir is denied; (c) bash `sleep 5 & kill %1` works; (d) `os.setsid()` grandchild survives a `killpg` of the original group (expected yes); (e) `chflags uchg` on a workdir file is permitted; (f) `/dev/fd` shows only 0-2; (g) does `(allow process-exec (literal <symlink spelling>))` alone suffice, or is the realpath literal also required; (h) the darwin env trampoline (KD-5): `sandbox-exec` exec'd with `env={}` runs `/usr/bin/env -i A=1 B=2 <python3> -c 'import os; print(sorted(os.environ))'` under the restricted profile with `(literal "/usr/bin/env")` exec-allowed, output exactly `['A', 'B']` plus whatever `env`/dyld/the framework launcher adds — record EACH added key by name and per interpreter (expected, to be confirmed: python3's framework launcher may set `__PYVENV_LAUNCHER__`; a bash target exports its own `PWD`, `SHLVL` and `_` to what it runs; node adds nothing it is not given) — the list becomes the `ENV_ADDITIONS` table of `sbx_restrict:T1`/`T7` and `mcp_sandbox:E16` (R2:L5); (i) **python3 under the exec filter via the REAL launch path** (C-H2): `/usr/local/bin/python3 -c 'print(1)'` with the filter listing only the spelling's hops + realpath FAILS (the launcher's `posix_spawn(SETEXEC)` of `Python.app/Contents/MacOS/Python` is denied), and passes once `Python.app/Contents/MacOS/Python` is added to `execs` and `Versions/<v>/Python` to the closure roots; (j) `/bin/sh -c 'echo ok'` under restricted reads: record whether `/private/var/select/sh` (the macOS `/bin/sh` dispatcher's selection symlink) must be readable; the planned fallback is one extra literal `--read /private/var/select/sh` (plus its hops) added by mcp-sandbox to every plan (C-L8); (k) **setuid exec under the restricted profile (R2:S-L2)**: with `/usr/bin` exec-allowed, run `/usr/bin/crontab -l`, `/usr/bin/at -l` and `/usr/bin/sudo -n true` (each with stdin `/dev/null`, 5 s timeout) and record per binary: exit code, stderr, and whether a probe the binary would need outside the profile (its spool/`/etc/sudoers` read, a mach service) was denied — the expected and required result is that the sandbox PERSISTS across the setuid exec (each fails or answers within the profile); a binary that reads outside the profile STOPS M1 (KD-6); (l) **the SBPL operations that stop ACL and flag writes (R2:S-H1b)**: in a restricted profile whose write scope is the workdir, append `(deny <op> (subpath <workdir>))` for each candidate — `file-write-acl`, `file-write-flags`, `file-write-xattr`, `file-write-mode` — one at a time, and record which one makes `chmod +a "everyone deny delete" f`, a `ctypes` `acl_set_file` and `chflags uchg f` fail with `EPERM` (record the EXACT operation name accepted by `sandbox-exec`; a name it rejects is recorded as "no such operation"); then run the python3, bash and node self-tests (and `python3 -c 'import os; open("x","w").write("1"); os.chmod("x", 0o755)'`) with the chosen deny(s) present and record that they still pass. `file-write-mode` is recorded but never adopted (scripts need `chmod +x`). Also record whether an ACE `everyone deny writesecurity` set by the script blocks the OWNER's later `acl_set_fd_np` strip (KD-18 step 5). Outcome decides deferred item 12: if an op stops both vectors and every self-test passes, restricted mode emits it (Step 3); else item 12 stays deferred and ADR 0031 records why. **STOP rule (R3:S-M1, mirrors 3k)**: if NO profile op is adoptable (none stops the vectors, or an interpreter's self-test needs it) AND a script-set `everyone deny writesecurity` (or `delete`) ACE blocks the owner's `acl_set_fd_np` strip, such a workdir is never removable, 17 hostile runs leave the runner permanently degraded, and M1 does NOT start: the design is re-opened and the chosen redesign recorded in ADR 0031 before any code. Candidate redesigns to evaluate then (none pre-chosen): (1) refuse the RESULT and never reuse the slot for a workdir whose entries carry any extended ACL — detect with `acl_get_fd_np` (`MacOSX14.2.sdk/usr/include/sys/acl.h:194`) after the run and hand the workdir to (2); (2) move an unremovable workdir OUT of the quarantine cap into a dated `<work_root>/trash-<YYYYMMDD>/` that does not count toward `_QUARANTINE_MAX`, bounded instead by `min_free_bytes`, with `capabilities` carrying a `trash` count and a warning the operator clears (as root or after reboot); (3) a per-run disposable APFS volume / disk image as the write scope, unmounted and deleted wholesale — heavier, needs measurement. ADR 0031 records which was taken and why the others were not.
4. Cleanup (KD-18): build in `.claude/tmp/m0/rm/` a 10 000-level directory chain, a tree of nested `chmod 000` directories (3 levels), a `chflags uchg` file inside a `chmod 000` dir, a `0500` and a `0100` directory each holding a file, and a file plus a directory each carrying `chmod +a "everyone deny delete"`; time the iterative fd-relative remover prototype (incl. the `fchmod` and empty-ACL strip steps 4-5) on each and record the op counts and wall time against `_CLEANUP_DEADLINE_S = 10`. (Replaces the dropped `shutil.rmtree.avoids_symlink_attacks` check.)
5. Keg sizes (C-H3): for the python3, node and openssl@3 kegs record total file count, max depth and the file count of each TARGETED image directory (`lib/*.dylib`, `lib/python*/lib-dynload`, `Frameworks/*.framework/Versions/*/` top level, `lib/node_modules/**/*.node`) and how many of those are Mach-O by magic. The image-dir cap (`_KEG_SCAN_MAX_FILES`) is set to `max(measured) * 4`, rounded up to a power of two, and recorded.
6. Startup-time ESTIMATE for mcp-sandbox's planning phase (R-12): three raw `sandbox-exec` runs (VSZ probe, `--version`, self-test) of `/bin/bash` with the seed profile, and the same for the three-interpreter set; the real number is re-measured at Step 12 (verification) and decides at Step 14 whether `READ_TIMEOUT = 8.0` (`Scripts/_mcp_smoke_test.py:139`) suffices.
**Pattern to follow**: `.claude/tmp/rlimit-probe/probe.py`.
**Verification**: `.claude/tmp/m0/results.md` lists each item with value and the fork chosen (incl. 3k setuid outcome, 3l operation names, the owner-strip result and the item-12 decision, 3h `ENV_ADDITIONS`); neither the 3k nor the 3l STOP condition is met (or the plan was re-opened); pasted into ADR 0031 "Measurements" in Step 20.
**Result (DONE 2026-10-11, M0:1-M0:15)**: `.claude/tmp/m0/results.md` (task-010 gate) consolidates `item-1.md` .. `item-6.md`, `item-3a-g.md`, `item-3h-j.md`, `item-3k.md`, `item-3l.md`, `item-depth.md` and item 4b (flatten remover, `.claude/tmp/m0/flatten/`); host macOS 14.2.1 (Darwin 23.2.0, x86_64), APFS, Python 3.14.0. No STOP rule fired (R-5, KD-6, R3:S-M1); PC-1 met. Its "Decisions applied to the plan" 1-11 are applied in place throughout this plan, each tagged `M0:<n>`; the extra item `item-depth.md` (Seatbelt depth cap) and the depth-cap decision (Zoltán, 2026-10-11) became M0:9.

### Step 2: sbx — Scope, flag grammar, pure resolvers (red rows first)
**Files**: `ClaudeCode/skills/sandbox-run/scripts/sbx:56-120` (Scope fields), `:524-542` (`_build_parser`), `:778-793` (add `canonicalize_read` beside `canonicalize`), `:818-831` (`_format_plan`), `:1199-1234` (`main` up to dry-run) (modify); `tests/test_sbx_restrict.py` (create); `tests/run.py` wrapper beside `run_sbx_seccomp` (:89) + `SUITES` row after the `sbx_seccomp` row (:280) (modify, hunk-level, PC-2; edit by content, P16); `project-forge.yaml:77-83` (add the `sbx_restrict` target after `sbx_seccomp`) (modify)
**Dependencies**: Step 1 (and PC-1)
**Description**:
- Parser additions (`action="append"` for repeatables, `type=str`, values validated by our own functions): `--restrict-reads` (store_true), `--read PATH`, `--exec PATH`, `--clean-env` (store_true), `--env KEY=VALUE`, `--timeout SECONDS`, `--status-fd FD`, `--rlimit-as`, `--rlimit-cpu`, `--rlimit-nproc`, `--rlimit-fsize`, `--rlimit-nofile`, `--rlimit-core`. Add `allow_abbrev=False` to the `argparse.ArgumentParser(...)` call in `_build_parser` (sbx:526-530; today it passes only `prog`, `usage`, `description`, so `--rlimit-a` or `--restrict` would silently resolve to a full flag the gate never saw). Legacy full spellings are unaffected (`sbx_restrict:D4`).
- Pure functions (no I/O):
  - `canonicalize_read(path_arg: str) -> str` — absolute required, `_INJECT_CHARS` floor (P2), `os.path.normpath`; NO realpath (KD-14); refusal -> exit 2.
  - `parse_env(raw: list, clean: bool) -> Optional[dict]` — `None` when not clean; `--env` without `--clean-env` -> `_usage_error`; key `^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]{0,127}$`; `LD_`/`DYLD_` prefix refused; duplicate key refused; value no NUL, <= 32768 chars; <= 64 entries.
  - `parse_limits(args) -> Tuple[tuple, Optional[int]]` — decimal `^[0-9]{1,20}$` (nproc also `^\+[0-9]{1,5}$`); AS >= 64 MiB, CPU 1..86400, NPROC 1..1000000 or +1..+4096, FSIZE 0..2**62, NOFILE 3..1048576, CORE 0..2**62; refusal -> exit 2.
  - `parse_timeout(raw) -> Optional[float]` — `^[0-9]{1,6}(\.[0-9]{1,3})?$`, 0 < t <= 86400.
  - `parse_status_fd(raw, timeout) -> Optional[int]` — `^[0-9]{1,4}$`, >= 3, requires `--timeout`.
  - `metadata_ancestors(paths) -> tuple` — every proper ancestor (incl. `/`) of every read spelling, write scope, exec path and the cwd; deduplicated, sorted.
  - `validate_restricted(args, argv0, cwd, writes)`: `--read`/`--exec` without `--restrict-reads` -> exit 2; `--restrict-reads` with `--net` -> exit 2; with `--clean-env` argv0 must be absolute -> exit 2, and on darwin argv0 must not contain `=` (the `/usr/bin/env -i` trampoline would read it as an assignment, KD-5) -> exit 2; restricted mode requires the cwd inside a write or read scope -> exit 3.
- Resolver (I/O, `main()` only): `resolve_reads(reads, execs, argv0) -> (read_rules, exec_rules)` — each spelling: `os.lstat` (missing -> exit 2); symlink -> `(p, "symlink", os.readlink(p))`; dir -> `(p, "dir", None)`; file -> `(p, "file", None)`; caps <= 256 reads, <= 32 execs. `exec_rules` = argv0 spelling + each `--exec` with its `is_dir` bit (no chain walking, KD-14).
- `linux_base_reads() -> tuple` (I/O, main only): for `/usr`, `/lib`, `/lib64`, `/lib32`, `/bin`, `/sbin`: symlink -> `("symlink", os.readlink(p), p)`; dir -> `("ro-bind", p)`; absent -> skipped. `/etc` set: `ld.so.cache`, `ld.so.conf`, `ld.so.conf.d`, `localtime`, `ssl/certs`, `alternatives` (existence-filtered; a spelling covered by a base bind skipped).
- `_format_plan` prints `restrict_reads`, `read_rules`, `meta_literals`, `exec_rules`, env KEYS only, `timeout`, `status_fd`, `limits`.
- Rows in `tests/test_sbx_restrict.py` (load sbx via `test_sbx_gate._load_helper`, `tests/test_sbx_gate.py:288`), groups A-D, offline:
  - A parser/validation (15): `A1 read-without-restrict-refused`, `A2 exec-without-restrict-refused`, `A3 env-without-clean-env-refused`, `A4 env-bad-key-refused`, `A5 env-ld-preload-refused`, `A6 env-dyld-refused`, `A7 env-duplicate-key-refused`, `A8 env-nul-value-refused`, `A9 status-fd-without-timeout-refused`, `A10 status-fd-below-3-refused`, `A11 timeout-zero-refused`, `A12 rlimit-non-decimal-refused`, `A13 rlimit-nproc-plus-form-parsed`, `A14 restrict-with-net-refused`, `A15 abbreviated-flag-refused` (`--restrict`, `--rlimit-a 1`, `--dry` each exit 2; C-L1).
  - B spelling/ancestors (8): `B1 ancestors-of-nested`, `B2 ancestors-include-root`, `B3 symlink-read-is-literal-not-followed`, `B4 dir-read-is-subpath`, `B5 read-keeps-spelling-normpath-only`, `B6 read-relative-refused`, `B7 read-missing-refused`, `B8 read-injection-char-refused` (fixtures under `.claude/tmp/`).
  - C argv0/cwd (4): `C1 clean-env-relative-argv0-refused`, `C2 restricted-cwd-outside-scopes-refused`, `C3 restricted-cwd-inside-write-ok`, `C4 read-cap-256`.
  - D dry-run (4): `D1 dry-run-prints-restricted-fields`, `D2 dry-run-env-values-never-printed`, `D3 import-has-no-side-effects`, `D4 legacy-argv-unchanged` (no new flag -> `_seatbelt_argv`/`_bwrap_argv` byte-identical to a pinned fixture of the committed sbx).
- Register `("sbx_restrict", run_sbx_restrict, "sbx restricted mode: reads, exec filter, clean env, supervised timeout, strict rlimits, gate grammar", 31)` right after `sbx_seccomp` (31 = A15 + B8 + C4 + D4, the rows that exist at this step; Steps 3-6 raise it with their groups, R4:L4), with a `# TYPED: a fixed case table (A 15, B 8, ...)` ledger comment above it in the `sbx_seccomp` style; `def run_sbx_restrict(opts): return run_python_suite("test_sbx_restrict", opts)` beside `run_sbx_seccomp` (:89; edit by content).
- `project-forge.yaml` (R2:M7): add `test.sbx_restrict` right after `sbx_seccomp` (:77-83), same shape (description naming the groups, the live-row INFO rules and "writes only under .claude/tmp", `requires: [syntax]`, `commands: [python3 tests/run.py sbx_restrict]`, the `sbx_seccomp` filter grep).
**Pattern to follow**: P1, P2, P3; P16.
**Verification**: `forge_call test` filter `sbx_restrict`: RED first (behavioural failures, no ImportError), then GREEN; `sbx_gate` 105 unchanged.

### Step 3: sbx — restricted Seatbelt and bwrap builders (golden output)
**Files**: `ClaudeCode/skills/sandbox-run/scripts/sbx:138-266` (`_seatbelt_argv`), `:269-452` (`_bwrap_argv`) (modify); `tests/test_sbx_restrict.py` (groups E-F); `tests/run.py` (update the `sbx_restrict` typed count 31 -> 51 and its ledger comment; hunk-level, edit by content, PC-2; a stale typed count is CASE COUNT DRIFT, a hard failure, `tests/run.py:811`; R4:L4) (modify)
**Dependencies**: Step 2; Step 1 item 3l (M0:8: the deny is `file-write-acl` only), items 3c/3h (M0:2, M0:5, M0:6) and `item-depth.md` (M0:9)
**Description**:
- `_seatbelt_argv(scope)` when `scope.restrict_reads`:
  ```
  (version 1)
  (deny default)
  (allow process-exec (literal "<argv0 spelling>") (literal "/usr/bin/env") (literal "<--exec file>")... (subpath "<--exec dir>")...)
      ; "/usr/bin/env" only when --clean-env (darwin trampoline, KD-5)
      ; the filter matches REALPATHS (M0:3): the argv0 spelling literal is inert, callers pass realpaths as --exec
  (allow process-fork)
  (allow signal (target same-sandbox))                         ; M0:2 (target self) breaks `kill $!`, 3c
  (allow sysctl-read)                                          ; M0:6 node aborts without it
  (allow file-read-data (literal "/"))
  (allow file-read-metadata (literal "<ancestor>") ...)       ; meta_literals
  (allow file-read-metadata (subpath "<--exec dir>") ...)     ; M0:4 ONLY when the target env carries PATH
  (allow file-read* (literal "<symlink or file spelling>") ...)
  (allow file-read* (subpath "<dir spelling>") ...)
  (allow file-read* (subpath "<write scope>") ...)            ; unless ro
  (allow file-write* (subpath "<write scope>") ...)           ; unless ro (existing loop)
  (deny file-write-acl (subpath "<write scope>") ...)         ; R2:S-H1b + M0:8, restricted only; NO file-write-flags
  (deny file-write-create (regex #"^<scope-re>/[^/]+/[^/]+/ ... [^/]+/") ...)
                                                              ; M0:9 depth cap, restricted only, one per write
                                                              ; scope; "[^/]+/" written out EXACTLY 100 times
  (allow file-read* file-write* (literal "/dev/null"))         ; M0:5 emitted by the restricted branch itself
  <dir-secret denies>  <NO carve-outs>  <file-secret denies>  <shadow write denies>
  (deny network*)
  ```
  Non-restricted output stays byte-identical (`D4`). Carve-outs are skipped when restricted.
- `_bwrap_argv(scope)` when restricted: replace `["bwrap", "--ro-bind", "/", "/"]` (sbx:327) with `["bwrap"]` + `linux_base` entries; then the existing `--dev`/socket sequence; per read rule not covered by a base bind: symlink -> `--symlink <value> <spelling>`, dir/file -> `--ro-bind <spelling> <spelling>`; a read under `/run`, `/var/snap` or `/tmp` -> `ValueError` (exit 3; would re-open socket dirs); writes, secret masks as today; carve-outs skipped; **shadow re-binds skipped** — the `for path in scope.shadow_write_denies or (): argv += ["--ro-bind", path, path]` loop (sbx:396-397; targets from `shadow_write_denies()`, body sbx:750-769: the plugin tree and `<repo>/ClaudeCode`, `<repo>/Scripts`) skips, when `scope.restrict_reads`, every shadow target NOT covered by a write scope (`_covered(path, w)` for no `w` in `scope.writes`), because on an empty root (no `--ro-bind / /`) such a `--ro-bind` is a READ grant of a repo tree (C-H4 = S-H1). A shadow target that IS inside a write scope keeps its re-bind (it is readable through that scope anyway, and the re-bind is what keeps it unwritable); outside every write scope nothing is mounted writable in restricted mode, so the skipped re-binds protected nothing; file masks as today; `--proc`, unshares; ADD `--unshare-uts --hostname sbx` and `--unshare-cgroup-try`; `--new-session --die-with-parent`; when `scope.env is not None`: `--clearenv` + `--setenv K V` per sorted key; `--chdir <cwd>`; seccomp; `--`; argv. Pure helper `_covered(path, bound) -> bool` (commonpath-based).
- Rows:
  - E Seatbelt golden (10): `E1 restricted-profile-golden` (M0: the golden carries `(target same-sandbox)`, the PATH-gated exec-dir metadata subpaths, `file-write-acl`, the literal 100-group depth regex and `/dev/null`), `E2 no-global-read-subpath-root`, `E3 no-unfiltered-process-exec`, `E4 no-global-file-read-metadata`, `E5 carveouts-dropped-when-restricted`, `E6 denies-after-allows`, `E7 dev-null-read-write-literal`, `E8 ro-restricted-no-write-scope`, `E9 network-deny-last`, `E10 restricted-denies-acl-writes-in-write-scope` (R2:S-H1b, M0:8: `(deny file-write-acl ...)` follows the write allow for every write scope, is absent from the legacy profile, NO `file-write-flags` (and no `file-write-mode`/`file-write-xattr`) deny appears anywhere in the profile, and `/dev/null` keeps read+write).
  - The ACL deny (R2:S-H1b, M0:8) is emitted in restricted mode only, right after the write-allow loop and before the `/dev/null` literal, one `(deny file-write-acl ...)` over the same `(subpath ...)` list as the write allow; with `--ro` (no write scope) nothing is emitted. Seatbelt is last-match-wins (sbx:142-161), so the deny after the allow is what takes effect. `file-write-flags` is NOT denied: measured, it breaks `shutil.copy2`/`shutil.copytree` (copystat calls `chflags` even with flags 0 and tolerates only ENOTSUP), and the owner clears `uchg` with `os.lchflags(p, 0)` (`item-3l.md` §3, §5); `file-write-mode` is never denied (`chmod +x` must keep working).
  - **Depth cap (M0:9, Zoltán's decision 2026-10-11)**: restricted Seatbelt denies `file-write-create` beyond 100 directory levels under every write scope: built as `'(deny file-write-create (regex #"^%s/%s"))' % (_depth_cap_regex(scope), "[^/]+/" * 100)`, the group `[^/]+/` WRITTEN OUT LITERALLY 100 times, because the bounded quantifiers `{n}`, `{n,}`, `{m,n}` compile and silently never match (`item-depth.md` §1) — a `{100}` rule would load and protect nothing. `<scope-re>` is the write scope's canonical (resolved) spelling with every regex metacharacter of the set `\ . ^ $ * + ? { } [ ] | ( )` backslash-escaped by a pure helper `_depth_cap_regex(scope: str) -> str` (the `_INJECT_CHARS` floor already refuses quotes, P2). Measured with this rule (`item-depth.md` §2-§3): `mkdir` to level 100 succeeds, level **101 gets EPERM** — absolute, `chdir`-relative and `*at`-relative alike, past PATH_MAX too — and a rename whose DESTINATION is at level 101 gets EPERM (checked as `file-write-create`, §4); no regular file can be created inside a level-100 directory, so entries exist only at depth <= 100; work at levels 1-10 and the python self-test are unaffected (§5). It does NOT bound rename-chaining (only the destination's top entry is checked; KD-18 flatten, M0:14). Emitted after the ACL deny, before `/dev/null`; with `--ro` nothing is emitted; Linux (bwrap) has no equivalent (declared, ADR 0031). Golden: `sbx_restrict:E1` pins the exact 100-group text.
  - **Exec-dir metadata (M0:4, `item-3a-g.md` 3c)** — the RULE: a bare command name is looked up by `stat`ing `<dir>/<name>` for each `$PATH` entry, and an exec allow is not a read-metadata allow, so without metadata on the exec dirs every bare-name lookup fails (`bash: sleep: command not found`, 127) while absolute paths work. sbx therefore emits `(allow file-read-metadata (subpath "<d>"))` for every `--exec` DIRECTORY entry (`/bin`, `/usr/bin` from mcp-sandbox) ONLY when the target's environment can carry a `PATH` — `scope.env is None` (inherited) or `"PATH" in scope.env` — and never otherwise (no PATH, no lookup, no oracle). The interpreter's own PATH dir needs nothing extra: its spelling hops are read literals, and keg `bin` dirs sit inside the keg read subpaths. Under `env={}` bash has NO PATH at all (`item-6.md` side finding 4), which is why every bash SELF-TEST uses builtins only (Step 12). Metadata of `/bin` and `/usr/bin` entries is the existence of system binaries — not a secret, and never global (`sbx_restrict:E4` still holds).
  - **`/dev/null` (M0:5)**: the restricted branch EMITS `(allow file-read* file-write* (literal "/dev/null"))` itself; it does not rely on the legacy line (sbx:215, `--ro` fix `4d3640c`) being inherited, because both are separate builder branches and `sbx_restrict:E7` must pin the restricted output on its own. Measured necessary: bash background jobs (`cannot redirect standard input from /dev/null`, 3c) and node (`uv_loop_init` assertion, SIGABRT, 3h).
  - F bwrap golden (10): `F1 restricted-argv-golden-usr-merged` (Ubuntu 22.04 shape fixture), `F2 restricted-argv-golden-split-usr`, `F3 no-ro-bind-root`, `F4 spelling-under-base-skipped`, `F5 clearenv-setenv-sorted`, `F6 chdir-emitted`, `F7 unshare-uts-cgroup`, `F8 carveouts-dropped`, `F9 legacy-argv-unchanged`, `F10 shadows-dropped-when-restricted` (a `Scope` with `restrict_reads=True`, write scope `/w`, and `shadow_write_denies` `("/r/ClaudeCode", "/w/sub")` yields no `--ro-bind /r/ClaudeCode /r/ClaudeCode` but keeps `--ro-bind /w/sub /w/sub`; the same scope without `restrict_reads` emits both — C-H4 = S-H1).
  - > **IMPL:025** — the env flags above were listed only under the restricted branch, so on Linux `--clean-env --env K=V` WITHOUT `--restrict-reads` exec'd bwrap with `{}` and no `--clearenv`/`--setenv`: the target got an empty env and the `--env` pairs were dropped (narrower, not a leak, but it contradicted FR-4 and the darwin trampoline, which applies whenever `--clean-env` is given). `_bwrap_argv` now emits `--clearenv` + sorted `--setenv` whenever `scope.env is not None`, in both branches; `--chdir` stays restricted-only, and with `env None` the legacy argv is byte-identical (`sbx_restrict:D4`, `F9`). Pinned by the added row `F11 clean-env-without-restrict-reads-keeps-setenv` (legacy golden + `--clearenv --setenv A 1 --setenv B 2` before `--`; `env {}` -> `--clearenv` alone; `env None` -> unchanged), written red first; F group is 11 rows, `sbx_restrict` 92.
  - The Seatbelt builder keeps its shadow rules in restricted mode (they are `(deny file-write* ...)`, grant nothing); `sbx_restrict:E1`'s golden pins that. The darwin `--clean-env` golden includes `(literal "/usr/bin/env")` in the exec rule (`sbx_restrict:T4`).
**Pattern to follow**: P4, P5 (order is load-bearing; keep the existing comment style).
**Verification**: groups E-F green; `D4`/`F9` legacy byte-identity; `sbx_gate` live rows (`_run_live_seatbelt` `tests/test_sbx_gate.py:1486`) still green.

### Step 4: sbx — supervised fork mode, status protocol, strict rlimits, clean-env exec
**Files**: `ClaudeCode/skills/sandbox-run/scripts/sbx:1130-1196` (rlimits), `:1199-1290` (`main`) (modify); new `_prepare_exec`, `_strict_rlimits`, `uid_process_count`, `_supervise`, `_write_status`; `tests/test_sbx_restrict.py` (groups U, V, T); `tests/run.py` (update the `sbx_restrict` typed count 51 -> 75 and its ledger comment; hunk-level, edit by content, PC-2; `tests/run.py:811` CASE COUNT DRIFT otherwise; R4:L4) (modify)
**Dependencies**: Step 3; Step 1 items 1 (kqueue `NOTE_EXIT`) and 3h (env trampoline)
**Description**:
- `uid_process_count(uid: int) -> int` (I/O): Linux scans `/proc/[0-9]*/status` `Uid:` (vanished pids ignored); macOS `subprocess.run(["/bin/ps", "-A", "-o", "ruid="], env={}, stdin=subprocess.DEVNULL, capture_output=True, timeout=5)` counting lines == uid. Failure -> exit 3.
- `_strict_rlimits(limits: tuple) -> Optional[str]` (runs in the child after fork, or right before exec in non-fork mode): per `(name, value)`: `which = getattr(resource, name)`; `_soft, hard = getrlimit(which)`; value > finite hard -> return refusal; `setrlimit(which, (value, value))`; on `(ValueError, OSError)` return `"setrlimit %s refused (%s)" % (name, type(exc).__name__)`. The legacy `_apply_rlimits()` still runs first for resources NOT requested (P6 unchanged).
- `_prepare_exec(scope) -> (engine_path, argv, env)` shared by both paths: when `scope.env is not None`, `engine_path` = the absolute engine `_detect_engine()` resolved and `env = {}` on BOTH platforms (C-M7 = S-M1); on darwin the target part of the argv (after the profile) is `["/usr/bin/env", "-i", *("%s=%s" % (k, scope.env[k]) for k in sorted(scope.env)), *scope.argv]`; on linux the env travels as `--clearenv --setenv K V` (Step 3). Else today's `execvp` behaviour. **The legacy (non-fork, no `--clean-env`) exec site stays EXACTLY `os.execvp(argv[0], argv)`** (sbx:1285, R2:L9, anchor corrected R3:L6): `sbx_seccomp`'s `_Stubbed` replaces precisely `os.execvp` with a two-argument recorder (`tests/test_sbx_seccomp.py:669-676`), so the legacy path may not switch to `os.execve`/`os.execv` or pass an env argument; `_prepare_exec` returns the engine/argv/env triple, but `main()`'s legacy branch keeps the literal `os.execvp(argv[0], argv)` call (the `--clean-env` non-fork branch uses `os.execve(engine_path, argv, {})`, which no existing suite stubs).
- `_supervise(scope, engine_path, argv, env) -> int` (when `scope.timeout`):
  1. `r, w = os.pipe()` (CLOEXEC) — setup-error channel.
  2. NPROC `+N`: `count = uid_process_count(os.getuid())`; add `("RLIMIT_NPROC", count + N)`.
  3. **Handlers for SIGTERM/SIGINT/SIGHUP installed BEFORE `fork()` (R2:L3)**: each sets a flag (`signal.signal(sig, _on_signal)`, previous handlers saved); so a signal that arrives between the fork and the `spawned` write is never lost — the supervisor loop (7) sees the flag on its first check and kills the group.
  4. `pid = os.fork()`. Child: FIRST restore `signal.SIG_DFL` for SIGTERM/SIGINT/SIGHUP (the target must not inherit the parent's Python handlers, and a default disposition is what `execve` would leave anyway); close `r`; close `scope.status_fd`; `os.setsid()`; `err = _strict_rlimits(limits)` -> on err write it to `w`, `os._exit(127)`; `os.execve(engine_path, argv, env)`; on OSError write and `_exit(127)`.
  5. Parent: close `w`; read `r` to EOF; non-empty -> reap, write `exit/refused` (no `spawned`), one stderr line, return 3. (A flag set while reading `r` is honoured right after: `_sweep(pid)` + `refused`/`signaled` as the child's state says.)
  6. Write `spawned` `{"pid": pid, "pgid": pid}`.
  7. `exited = _wait_exit_unreaped(pid, deadline)` (KD-2; the only platform branch): **Linux** polls `os.waitid(os.P_PID, pid, os.WEXITED | os.WNOWAIT | os.WNOHANG)` every 20 ms; **darwin** — selected by `sys.platform == "darwin"`, never by `hasattr(os, "waitid")` (True on Python 3.14 darwin, M0:1, `item-1.md`) — uses a `select.kqueue()` with one `select.kevent(pid, filter=select.KQ_FILTER_PROC, flags=select.KQ_EV_ADD | select.KQ_EV_ONESHOT, fflags=select.KQ_NOTE_EXIT)` registered immediately after `fork()` with `kq.control([ev], 0, 0)` (a `ProcessLookupError` / `OSError` `ESRCH` at registration = the child already exited = exited, still unreaped; with `nevents == 0` this is the only shape, M0:1), then `kq.control(None, 1, min(remaining, 0.1))` in a loop so the trapped-signal flag is checked at least every 100 ms. Neither path reaps. Exit observed -> `_sweep(pid)` = `os.killpg(pid, SIGKILL)` with `ESRCH` and `EPERM` ignored (macOS answers `EPERM` for a zombie-only group) -> `os.waitpid(pid, 0)`; outcome exited/signaled. Deadline or trapped signal -> `_sweep(pid)`; linux also `os.kill(pid, SIGKILL)`; `waitpid`; outcome `timeout` (or `signaled`). The kqueue fd is `CLOEXEC` by construction and closed in a `finally`.
  8. Terminal `exit` line; stderr marker on timeout; restore the saved handlers; return per the exit contract.
  `_write_status(fd, obj)`: `json.dumps(obj, separators=(",", ":"))`, <= 512 bytes, `os.write` loop, EPIPE ignored.
- `main()`: validate the status fd is open (`os.fstat`) else exit 2; `scope.timeout` -> `return _supervise(...)`; else legacy path with `_strict_rlimits` before exec (refusal -> exit 3).
- **`main()` source shape is gated (R3:I1)**: `sbx_gate` row Q "setrlimit-before-execvp ordering (KD-4)" (`tests/test_sbx_gate.py:1428-1437`) takes `inspect.getsource(helper.main)` and FAILS unless the text `_apply_rlimits()` occurs and its FIRST occurrence precedes the FIRST occurrence of `os.execvp(`. So: the zero-argument call `_apply_rlimits()` stays textually inside `main()` and before the one legacy `os.execvp(argv[0], argv)`; no new `os.execvp(` spelling (not even in a comment with the paren) appears in `main()` above it; the `_supervise` and `--clean-env` branches call `os.execve(` inside helpers, not `os.execvp(`. `_apply_rlimits` keeps its zero-argument signature, because `_main_in` (`tests/test_sbx_gate.py:350-379`) stubs exactly `os.execvp` (two-argument recorder), `helper._apply_rlimits = lambda: None`, `helper._detect_engine` and `helper._file_mask_fds` and then runs `helper.main(argv)` with legacy flags: on a legacy invocation `main()` may call no other new I/O (no `uid_process_count`, no `resolve_reads`, no `linux_base_reads` — they run only when the new flags ask for them).
- Rows (live rows macOS-PASS / Linux-INFO and vice versa):
  - U supervise (9): `U1 timeout-exit-124-and-marker`, `U2 status-fd-spawned-pgid-then-exit`, `U3 status-outcome-exited-code-passthrough` (target exits 3; status disambiguates), `U4 status-fd-not-inherited`, `U5 signaled-maps-128-plus`, `U6 kill-before-reap` (background child of the target dead after a normal exit), `U7 sbx-sigterm-kills-group`, `U8 exec-failure-refused-status-no-spawned`, `U9 linux-pidns-teardown` (INFO on macOS).
  - V rlimits (8): `V1 strict-hard-equals-soft`, `V2 target-cannot-raise-soft`, `V3 refused-limit-exit-3` (AS below current VSZ on macOS -> refused, nothing ran), `V4 nproc-plus-form-applied`, `V5 cpu-limit-kills-busy-loop`, `V6 fsize-efbig`, `V7 nofile-emfile`, `V8 core-zero`.
  - T env (7; T1, T2, T7 live — macOS PASS, Linux INFO, INFO under a nested sandbox, R3:L2; T3-T6 pure): `T1 clean-env-exact-set` (the asserted set is exactly the `--env` keys plus the M0-3h `ENV_ADDITIONS` for that interpreter, each addition named with its source, R2:L5 — never "a superset of"; MEASURED table (M0:7, `item-3h-j.md` 3h), keys only, values never asserted (bash 5 hands a child `SHLVL=0` when it exec-optimises its last command): `ENV_ADDITIONS = {"python3": (), "node": (), "bash": ("PWD", "SHLVL", "_")}` — python3's framework launcher leaves NO `__PYVENV_LAUNCHER__` in `os.environ`; the bash entry is the set a CHILD of bash sees (`/usr/bin/env` run by bash; `_` = the path bash executes), bash's own `compgen -e` shows only `PWD SHLVL`, so a bash row always observes through a child; `/usr/bin/env -i` and dyld add nothing), `T2 canary-not-inherited`, `T3 engine-env-empty-linux-golden`, `T4 darwin-engine-env-empty-trampoline-golden` (`_prepare_exec` returns `env == {}` and the argv tail `["/usr/bin/env", "-i", "A=1", "B=2", "/abs/argv0", ...]`, keys sorted; replaces the old "darwin engine env is the target env" row), `T5 legacy-env-inherited-unchanged`, `T6 darwin-argv0-with-equals-refused` (exit 2), `T7 darwin-trampoline-live-exact-env` (live macOS: target sees exactly the `--env` keys, a canary in sbx's own env absent; INFO on Linux).
    - > **IMPL:024 follow-up (2026-10-11)** — the `ENV_ADDITIONS` table above holds for the **restricted** profile only, and the test now keys it per profile (`tests/test_sbx_restrict.py` `ENV_ADDITIONS = {"restricted": {...}, "legacy": {...}}`, `LIVE_ENV_PROFILE = "legacy"`). Under restricted, Seatbelt denies the read of `/usr/share/locale/UTF-8/LC_CTYPE`, so python3's PEP 538 C-locale coercion fails silently and python3 adds nothing (M0:7). The live T1/T2/T7 rows run the **legacy** profile (no `--restrict-reads`), where the locale file is readable: python3 coerces the empty (C) locale and exports `LC_CTYPE` itself after exec. Re-measured live 2026-10-11 (macOS 14.2, python3 3.14.0): `env -i A=1 B=2 python3` shows it outside any sandbox and `PYTHONCOERCECLOCALE=0` removes it, so sbx adds nothing. The legacy row is `{"python3": ("LC_CTYPE",), "node": (), "bash": ("PWD", "SHLVL", "_")}`, and every addition is still named with its source (`ENV_ADDITIONS_SOURCE`); node and bash measure the same as M0:7.
  - `U6 kill-before-reap` is the live proof of the kqueue path on darwin (a background child of the target is dead after a normal exit) and of `waitid` on Linux (INFO on macOS for the Linux half — one row, platform-selected path).
**Pattern to follow**: P3, P6 (the strict path must NOT swallow), sbx:1273-1290 ordering comment style.
**Verification**: groups U/V/T green on macOS (Linux-only rows INFO); `sbx_gate` 105 (incl. row Q "setrlimit-before-execvp ordering", R3:I1) + `sbx_seccomp` 33 unchanged.

### Step 5: sbx-gate — new flags, narrowing policy, parser parity (rows in `tests/test_sbx_restrict.py`)
**Files**: `ClaudeCode/hooks/sbx-gate.py:139-191` (flag loop; add module constants `_BOOL_FLAGS`, `_VALUE_FLAGS`, `_PROMPT_FLAGS`, `_ENV_KEY_RE`; the LEGACY flags move into them too — `--ro`, `--seccomp`, `--dry-run` into `_BOOL_FLAGS`, `--write` into `_VALUE_FLAGS`, `--net` into `_PROMPT_FLAGS` (with the new `--status-fd`, R2:S-L1), and the existing loop branches for them (:153, :155, :158, :166, :178, :185) are driven by those tables with unchanged behaviour), `:1-55` (docstring invariant list) (modify); `tests/test_sbx_restrict.py` (group S); `tests/run.py` (update the `sbx_restrict` typed count 75 -> 91 and its ledger comment; hunk-level, edit by content, PC-2; `tests/run.py:811` CASE COUNT DRIFT otherwise; R4:L4) (modify). `tests/test_sbx_gate.py` is NOT modified (stays 105).
**Dependencies**: Step 2
**Description** (policy: auto-allow only what narrows; ANY doubt -> `(False, "")`):
- Booleans as exact bare tokens: `--restrict-reads`, `--clean-env` (equals forms prompt, like `--seccomp` :158).
- Valued flags, both `--flag VALUE` and `--flag=VALUE` (split on first `=`), dangling value -> prompt:
  - `--read`, `--exec`: the value must start with `/` (C-L3; a relative value prompts, sbx would refuse it anyway) and carry no `METACHARS` character (sbx-gate.py:67); otherwise any value is allowed (they only narrow; sbx refuses them outside restricted mode). Restricted mode drops the `~/.claude` carve-outs, which is itself narrowing.
  - `--env`: value must match `^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*=` and the key must not start `LD_`/`DYLD_`, else prompt.
  - `--timeout` (`^[0-9]+(\.[0-9]+)?$`): sbx has no wall clock today, so any timeout narrows.
  - `--status-fd` (either form, any value) -> `(False, "")`, ALWAYS (R2:S-L1, CWE-668): it makes the UNSANDBOXED sbx parent write JSON into whatever fd number the Bash tool's process happens to have inherited — a write the gate cannot see the target of. It is not narrowing; only mcp-sandbox, which spawns sbx itself with a fresh pipe and never goes through the gate, uses it. It sits in a `_PROMPT_FLAGS = {"--net", "--status-fd"}` table beside `_BOOL_FLAGS` / `_VALUE_FLAGS`.
  - `--rlimit-fsize`, `--rlimit-nofile`, `--rlimit-core` (`^[0-9]+$`): no sbx default exists; sbx refuses a value above the inherited hard, so any accepted value narrows.
  - `--rlimit-as` <= `8 * 1024**3` (`_RLIMIT_AS_BYTES` sbx:1130), `--rlimit-cpu` <= 900 (`_RLIMIT_CPU_SECONDS` sbx:1131), `--rlimit-nproc` `^\+?[0-9]+$` with absolute <= 512 or `+N` with N <= 512 (`_RLIMIT_NPROC_HEADROOM` sbx:1135). A value ABOVE sbx's current default raises a limit relative to today and is NOT narrowing: prompt.
  - Digit checks use `re.fullmatch(r"[0-9]+", v)` (not `str.isdigit`, which accepts non-ASCII digits).
- `--net` still returns `(False, "")` (sbx-gate.py:153), also alongside `--restrict-reads`. Write containment unchanged.
- Group S (16): `S1 restrict-reads-allowed`, `S2 read-space-form-allowed`, `S3 read-equals-form-allowed`, `S4 exec-allowed`, `S5 clean-env-allowed`, `S6 env-allowed`, `S7 env-equals-form-allowed`, `S8 env-bad-key-prompts`, `S9 env-ld-preload-prompts`, `S10 timeout-allowed` (both forms), `S11 rlimit-as-above-default-prompts`, `S12 rlimit-nproc-plus-allowed`, `S13 net-with-restrict-still-prompts`, `S14 gate-flag-table-matches-parser` (load sbx via `_load_helper`, collect every `option_strings` of `_build_parser()._actions` except `-h/--help`, assert set equality with `_BOOL_FLAGS | _VALUE_FLAGS | _PROMPT_FLAGS` — the set therefore includes the legacy `--write`, `--ro`, `--seccomp`, `--dry-run`, `--net` and the new `--status-fd`, so the row fails if either side gains or loses any flag, old or new; C-L2), `S15 read-or-exec-relative-prompts` (`--read rel/x`, `--exec=bin/x` -> `(False, "")`; C-L3), `S16 status-fd-prompts` (`--timeout 5 --status-fd 3` and `--timeout=5 --status-fd=9`, with and without `--restrict-reads` -> `(False, "")`; R2:S-L1). Gate loaded via `test_sbx_gate._load_gate` (`tests/test_sbx_gate.py:282`).
**Pattern to follow**: P7 (exact tokens, equals-form rule).
**Verification**: group S green inside `sbx_restrict`; `sbx_gate` = 105 unchanged and green.

Rows (12): `L1 ssh-known-hosts-read-denied` (assert denial only), `L2 home-listing-denied`, `L3 etc-passwd-denied`, `L4 repo-file-denied` (repo `CLAUDE.md`), `L5 stat-oracle-denied` (EPERM, not ENOENT/size), `L6 exec-planted-binary-denied`, `L7 root-listing-declared` (INFO), `L8 python-runs`, `L9 bash-runs-with-subshell`, `L10 node-runs-require-os`, `L11 acl-writes-denied-flags-allowed-in-write-scope` (R2:S-H1b, reworded by M0:8: inside the write scope `chmod +a "everyone deny delete" f` and a `ctypes` `acl_set_file` each fail `EPERM`, while `chflags uchg f` SUCCEEDS (flags are deliberately not denied; the test then clears it with `os.lchflags(f, 0)` as the owner, outside the profile, before its cleanup), `shutil.copy2` and `shutil.copytree` succeed, and an ordinary write and `chmod +x` succeed — `item-3l.md` §2-§3), `L12 depth-cap-101-refused` (M0:9, `item-depth.md` §2-§4: inside the profile a python3 probe builds a `d/` chain in the workdir — level 100 created, the level-101 `mkdir` fails `EPERM` for each of the absolute, `chdir`-relative and `os.mkdir(..., dir_fd=)`-relative forms, a regular file created inside the level-100 directory fails `EPERM`, and an `os.rename` of a level-1 directory whose DESTINATION is at level 101 fails `EPERM`; a control under the same profile at levels 1-10 succeeds; the probe's chain is removed by the test as the owner; macOS live, Linux INFO, nested-sandbox INFO via P23).
**Pattern to follow**: `_run_live_seatbelt` `tests/test_sbx_gate.py:1486`.
**Verification**: group L green on macOS; `sbx_restrict` typed count set (planned 103; final at the red run).
  - > **IMPL:029** — group L landed with 12 rows and `sbx_restrict` is **104**, not 103: Step 3 already moved it to 92 (`F11`, IMPL:025). No sbx code changed. The first live run (the red run) FAILED two rows for reasons in the plan's own assertions, not in sbx. Both are measured on macOS 14.2.1 and declared, never asserted away:
    (1) **`L5` — Seatbelt is an existence oracle.** It checks the TERMINAL vnode only. An existing path outside every grant answers `EPERM`, which gives no size and no times, under an ungranted directory and under a metadata-granted ancestor alike. A MISSING name answers `ENOENT` in both positions, because there is no vnode to deny. So the plan's "EPERM, not ENOENT" cannot hold for a missing name, and no path rule can close it. `L5` gates the half that holds (an existing path -> `EPERM`, i.e. no metadata) and records the missing-name answers as a declared detail. To be declared in ADR 0031.
    (2) **`L11` — `chmod +a` never reaches the kernel under the restricted profile.** It fails with `Unable to translate 'everyone' to a UUID` (rc 1), because there is no mach-lookup to opendirectoryd. That is the same mechanism `item-3l.md` §4 measured for `user:<owner>`; the M0 profile had a broad `file-read*` and let the `everyone` translation through. The `file-write-acl` deny itself is proven by the ctypes `acl_set_file` -> `EPERM`. For `chmod +a`, `L11` gates the OUTCOME: the command is refused, and neither file carries an extended ACL, checked by the owner with `acl_get_link_np` against a positive owner-set control. `chflags uchg`, `shutil.copy2`/`copytree`, the write and `chmod +x` all succeed as M0:8 planned.
    `L12` passes exactly as planned and claims only the destination cap; the rename-chaining bypass stays declared (R-13 / ADR 0032).
    The probes are copied into a fresh write scope per run, so no repo path is granted. The read set is spelled out by the test from `otool -L` and the symlink hops: the realpath argv0 plus python's `Python.app` launcher as `--exec`, `/bin` and `/usr/bin` as exec dirs, every hop as a literal, every keg root as a subpath, node's `openssl.cnf`, `/private/var/select/sh`, and `--clean-env --env PATH=/usr/bin:/bin`.

### Step 7: mcp-sandbox — skeleton, config loader, dispatcher, error envelope (red rows first)
**Files**: `Scripts/mcp-sandbox.py` (create); `tests/test_mcp_sandbox.py` (create, groups A and F); `tests/files/mcp_sandbox/` (create: config TEMPLATES only — the test renders each into `.claude/tmp/test_mcp_sandbox/` with `work_root` set to a fresh directory there, never `null`, C-M1; `tests/files/mcp_sandbox/config.json.tmpl` already exists in the tree from task-030, see the template contract below, M0:13); `tests/run.py` (wrapper + SUITES row with a `# TYPED: a fixed case table (...)` ledger comment, typed count 18 = A10 + F8 at this step, R4:L4; hunk-level, edit by content); `project-forge.yaml` (add `test.mcp_sandbox` before `mcp_proxy`, :351, R2:M7); `tests/test_generated_region.py:428-448` (one `STRICT_JSON_EXCEPTIONS` row for `_refuse_duplicate_keys_parse`, R2:L10) (modify); **the glob-discovered fleet tables, in THIS commit because the file now exists (R3:M1, P22)**: `tests/test_read_loop.py:190` FLEET + `DECLARED_POOL` :234 + the header comment :168; `tests/test_cancel.py:199` FLEET + `DECLARED_KILL` :249; `tests/test_handler_crash.py:194` FLEET + `DECLARED_W`/`DECLARED_D` :248-249; `tests/test_wire_log.py:270` WIRE + `DECLARED_FULL` :321; `tests/test_py_deps.py:162` `NEW_39_SCOPE`; `tests/run.py` typed counts + ledger comments of `read_loop`, `cancel`, `wire_log`, `handler_crash`, `protocol_version`, `py_deps` (edit by content, hunk-level, PC-2); `project-forge.yaml` descriptions of `read_loop` :256, `cancel` :264, `wire_log` :272, `handler_crash` :280, `protocol_version` :296, `py_deps` :320 (modify); `docs/adr/0008-a-serialized-read-loop-looks-like-a-dead-server.md` (append ONE dated addendum via `addendum.py` only, P25, R3:I2)
**Dependencies**: none code-wise (parallel with M1); live groups need Step 4
**Description**:
- File shape per `Scripts/MCP_SKELETON.md` §1-§6 (P13), pool variant §5a, pool size `limits.max_concurrent + 2`; McpServer modelled on `Scripts/mcp-inspect.py` (P15).
- Generated regions (markers per §8 :784): `_mcp_json.py :: _result, _error`, the strict-JSON + `_json_error_window` + `_ensure_dict` block as other hosts list it, `_mcp_dispatch.py :: _did_you_mean, _unknown_params_refusal`, `_mcp_concurrency.py :: MAX_INFLIGHT_REQUESTS`, `_mcp_logging.py` block as other hosts. NOT `_bool_param` / `_int_param` (R3:M3, P14: lenient by design; no host-local caller needs them, and a region nobody calls is dead code). Fill with `python3 Scripts/amalgamate.py`.
- Tool `sandbox_call`, input `{"function": {"enum": ["run_script","capabilities"]}, "params": {"type": "object"}}`; description states limits, the four error codes, "a failing script is a normal result".
- `handle_sandbox_call(arguments: dict) -> dict` (R3:M2; the `function` branch R4:M1). The `function` member, in this order:
  ```python
  function = arguments.get("function")
  if function is not None and not isinstance(function, str):
      return _tool_refusal("invalid_params", "function must be a string")
  function = (function or "").strip()
  # ... params decoded through _ensure_dict (below) ...
  if not function:                       # omitted, null, "" or whitespace
      return {"__raw_text__": _status_text()}   # UNFLAGGED: isError false
  ```
  An omitted (or empty) `function` is NOT a refusal: it answers an unflagged status reply — the function list (`run_script, capabilities`) plus a short status (configured interpreter names and their plan `state`, `degraded`, in-flight count; never paths, never the read set) and "call capabilities for details" — exactly the mcp-inspect shape (`if not function: return {"__raw_text__": _status_text(project_root)}`, `Scripts/mcp-inspect.py:2479-2480`, after its `_ensure_dict` at :2474-2477). The smoke harness's control probe sends `{"params": {}}` and asserts `omitted-function -> isError falsy (control)` (`Scripts/_mcp_smoke_test.py:343-347`); row `mcp_sandbox:F8` pins the same. A `function` that is present but not a `str` (a number, list, object, bool) is `invalid_params`. The elided params step (it runs BEFORE the status branch, as in mcp-inspect): `raw_params = arguments.get("params") or {}`; `params = _ensure_dict(raw_params)` with `except ValueError as exc: return _tool_refusal("invalid_params", str(exc))`. **A JSON-encoded object STRING for the tool's `params` member is accepted**, decoded through `_strict_loads` inside `_ensure_dict`: that is the fleet's behaviour (`Scripts/mcp-inspect.py:2473-2477`, `Scripts/mcp-search.py:8772-8776`), some MCP clients stringify nested objects, the decoded object then passes the same strict member validation as an object would (Step 9), and the call keeps the window region alive for `window-region-has-a-caller` (`tests/test_generated_region.py:779-811`, P14). A string that is not a JSON object is refused `invalid_params` with `_ensure_dict`'s own "Near the failure" window (the caller's own text, never a secret). This is the TOOL-level `params`; a non-object JSON-RPC `params` on `tools/call` stays a protocol-level -32602 (Contract item 1, smoke `forced_exc_code`). After it, layer D: one `except Exception` -> `log.exception("sandbox_call handler failed")` + `_tool_refusal("internal", type(exc).__name__)` (handler_crash `D_PRESENT`).
- **KD-17 timing block** (module constants, never config keys; defined here so every later row can assert the arithmetic, R3:M4, R3:L5): `_KILL_GRACE_S = 2.0`, `_REFRESH_DEADLINE_S = 30.0`, `_CLEANUP_DEADLINE_S = 10.0`, `_DEGRADED_RETRY_S = 2.0`, `_HARD_DEADLINE_SLACK_S = 10.0`, `_DRAIN_S = 0.5`, `_RUN_DEADLINE_S = 175.0`, `_STARTUP_SWEEP_DEADLINE_S = 20.0`, `_STARTUP_PLANS_DEADLINE_S = 75.0`. `load_config` additionally refuses a config whose `max_timeout_ms / 1000` plus the fixed terms exceeds `_RUN_DEADLINE_S` (unreachable with the 120000 cap; it pins the arithmetic in code).
- Parameter values are checked with explicit `isinstance` tests, never coerced (R3:M3): integers `isinstance(v, int) and not isinstance(v, bool)`, booleans `type(v) is bool`, strings `isinstance(v, str)`; a wrong type is `invalid_params` naming the member.
- **KILL machinery, in this step so the `cancel` KILL row is green from the commit that creates the file (R3:M1, R3:L5)**: a host-local `_Reclaim` (copy of `Scripts/mcp-inspect.py:384-430`, `adopt`, `cancel`, `_escalate`, plus `adopt_pgid(pgid)` which only records the pgid for the run thread), `_signal_group` (:432-445), `_REQUEST_RECLAIM: contextvars.ContextVar`, `class RequestCancelled(Exception)`, the constant `_KILL_GRACE_S = 2.0` (replaces inspect's `CANCEL_KILL_GRACE = 5.0` :381 — ONE grace for cancel and hard deadline, KD-17), `_spawn_sbx` (signature and body as specified in Step 11 — written HERE, first called in Step 11) and the host-local `_run_child` (Step 11 text). `McpServer._serve` (pool variant §5a) makes one `_Reclaim` per request, publishes it with `_REQUEST_RECLAIM.set(reclaim)` + `contextvars.copy_context()`, and carries exactly inspect's arm (`Scripts/mcp-inspect.py:2793-2799`):
  ```python
  except asyncio.CancelledError:
      reclaim.cancel(loop)   # SIGTERM each adopted sbx group, SIGKILL after _KILL_GRACE_S
      raise
  ```
  whose call path reaches `os.killpg` through `_signal_group` — what `tests/test_cancel.py:819-831` requires (`KILL_NO_ARM` without a re-raising arm, `KILL_NO_SIGNAL` when the arm reaches no `os.killpg`); `_spawn_sbx` gives the analyser its adopted `Popen` with `start_new_session=True` (`KILL_NO_SPAWN` :804-806 otherwise). Until Step 11 nothing calls `_spawn_sbx`; the analyser counts the site, not the caller.

No `KILL_EXEMPT` row (C-M3): if the AST check flags a spawn, route it through `_spawn_sbx`/`_run_child` rather than add an exemption. `tests/run.py` `cancel` 88 -> 90.

The test renders it with `text.replace("{sbx}", json.dumps(sbx_path))`, `text.replace("{work_root}", json.dumps(work_root))`, `text.replace("{interpreters}", json.dumps(interpreters))` — `json.dumps` supplies the quoting and escaping, so a path with `"` or `\` cannot break the JSON — and NEVER with `str.format` (the template's own `{` `}` JSON braces would be read as fields). ### Step 8: mcp-sandbox — stdlib Mach-O reader and dylib closure
**Files**: `Scripts/mcp-sandbox.py` (`_macho_slice`, `parse_macho`, `_resolve_install_name`, `dylib_closure`, `_homebrew_keg`, `_keg_images`, `_framework_launcher`, `_symlink_hops`); `tests/test_mcp_sandbox.py` (group B); synthetic fixtures built by a helper in the test (no checked-in binaries); `tests/run.py` (update the `mcp_sandbox` typed count 18 -> 38 and its ledger comment; hunk-level, edit by content, PC-2; `tests/run.py:811` CASE COUNT DRIFT otherwise; R4:L4) (modify)
**Dependencies**: Step 7; Step 1 item 5 (keg cap) and item 3i (launcher)
**Description**:
- Constants: `MH_MAGIC=0xfeedface`, `MH_MAGIC_64=0xfeedfacf`, `FAT_MAGIC=0xcafebabe`, `FAT_MAGIC_64=0xcafebabf` (fat headers big-endian); `CPU_TYPE_X86_64=0x01000007`, `CPU_TYPE_ARM64=0x0100000c` from `platform.machine()`; `LC_LOAD_DYLIB=0xc`, `LC_LOAD_WEAK_DYLIB=0x80000018`, `LC_REEXPORT_DYLIB=0x8000001f`, `LC_LAZY_LOAD_DYLIB=0x20`, `LC_LOAD_UPWARD_DYLIB=0x80000023`, `LC_RPATH=0x8000001c`. Caps: `sizeofcmds` <= 1 MiB; `ncmds` <= 4096; `nfat_arch` <= 32; images <= 512; `_KEG_SCAN_MAX_FILES = 512` (M0:11, `item-5.md`: the largest targeted image dir is python3's `lib-dynload`, 76 files, all Mach-O; 76 x 4 = 304, rounded up to a power of two; keg totals python3 4162 files / depth 14, node 2602 / 13, openssl@3 7609 / 7), applied to the TARGETED image directories only — there is no blanket keg walk and no depth cap on a whole keg (C-H3: the python keg is ~82 MB, `lib/python3.14/test` alone has 513 entries, node carries `lib/node_modules/npm`). Exceeding the cap = `MachOError("keg image scan over cap")` (fail closed, refresh-failed), never a silent truncation.
- `_symlink_hops(path) -> (hops: Tuple[str, ...], real: str)` — component walk with `os.readlink` (relative values against the link's dir), max 40 hops; every hop spelling is returned (each becomes its own `--read`, KD-14). The walk covers EVERY component, not only the final link: a symlinked ANCESTOR directory (e.g. `/usr/local/opt/node` in `/usr/local/bin/node -> /usr/local/opt/node/bin/node`) is a hop and becomes a `--read` literal — without it `sandbox-exec` exits 71 (M0:3, `item-6.md` side finding 1). Hops go to `reads` ONLY; `execs` take realpaths (M0:3).
- `_macho_slice(f, size, cputype) -> int`, `parse_macho(header_bytes: bytes, cputype: int) -> MachO` (PURE; validates magic, `cmdsize >= 8` and alignment, cumulative <= `sizeofcmds`, `lc_str` offset inside, NUL-terminated, UTF-8, no `\n`; raises `MachOError(reason)`).
- `_resolve_install_name(name, loader_real, exe_real, rpath_stack) -> List[str]` — `@executable_path/`, `@loader_path/` (of the LOADING image), `@rpath/` over the stack (each rpath resolved against the image that declared it).
- `dylib_closure(exe_spelling) -> Closure(spellings, kegs, images)` — BFS from the realpath; skip `/usr/lib/` and `/System/`; weak missing -> skip; strong missing -> `MachOError`; visited set on realpath; records the SPELLING used (+ its hops) for `--read`, the keg of the realpath, and every image realpath (for the KD-13 signature); every newly added keg is scanned by `_keg_images(keg) -> List[str]`, which visits ONLY these targeted locations (each `os.scandir`, `follow_symlinks=False`, regular files only):
  - `<keg>/lib/*` (top level, files only — `.dylib` and versioned names alike);
  - `<keg>/lib/python*/lib-dynload/*` (top level);
  - `<keg>/Frameworks/*.framework/Versions/*/*` (top level of each version dir: the framework's main image, e.g. `Versions/3.14/Python`);
  - `<keg>/lib/node_modules/**/*.node` (the only recursive one, bounded by the cap).
  - **IMPL:048 deviation** — a fifth location, `<keg>/Frameworks/*.framework/Versions/*/lib/python*/lib-dynload/*` (top level, same `_KEG_SCAN_MAX_FILES` cap per directory): Homebrew python's lib-dynload lives there on this host (`/usr/local/Cellar/python@3.14/3.14.0_1/Frameworks/Python.framework/Versions/3.14/lib/python3.14/lib-dynload`, 76 entries; the keg has no `<keg>/lib/python*/lib-dynload`), so the four locations above never reach `_ssl` and openssl@3 never enters the closure (row B18). To be recorded in ADR 0031.
  A file counts as an image by its first 4 bytes being one of the four Mach-O / fat magics — NEVER by extension (C-H2/C-H3); every image found is a further closure root.
- `_framework_launcher(real) -> Optional[Tuple[str, str]]` (C-H2): when `real` lies inside a `*.framework` directory (`^(.+\.framework)/Versions/([^/]+)/`), returns `(<fw>/Versions/<v>/Resources/Python.app/Contents/MacOS/Python, <fw>/Versions/<v>/Python)` for those that exist and are Mach-O by magic; the first goes to `execs` as its REALPATH (and its `_symlink_hops` to `reads`; M0:3 — measured required, without it python3 exits 1 with `posix_spawn: ... Undefined error: 0`, `item-3h-j.md` 3i), the second becomes an extra closure ROOT. Reason: Homebrew's `bin/python3.14` is a launcher that `posix_spawn(SETEXEC)`s `Python.app/Contents/MacOS/Python` (R-1a; verified paths on this host under `/usr/local/Cellar/python@3.14/3.14.0_1/Frameworks/Python.framework/Versions/3.14/`).
- `_homebrew_keg(real) -> Optional[str]` — `^(/.+)/Cellar/([^/]+)/([^/]+)(/|$)`.
- Interpreter realpath outside a keg and outside `/bin`/`/usr/bin` without `extra_reads` -> refuse.
- Rows B (20): `B1 thin64-dylibs-and-rpaths`, `B2 fat-select-native`, `B3 fat64-magic`, `B4 fat-missing-arch-refused`, `B5 truncated-header-refused`, `B6 ncmds-overflow-refused`, `B7 cmdsize-zero-refused`, `B8 lcstr-offset-out-of-range-refused`, `B9 lcstr-no-nul-refused`, `B10 rpath-resolution-order`, `B11 rpath-loader-path-relative-to-declarer`, `B12 executable-path`, `B13 weak-missing-skipped`, `B14 nonweak-missing-refused`, `B15 cycle-terminates`, `B16 system-paths-skipped`, `B17 image-cap-refused`, `B18 live-python-closure-has-openssl-keg` (macOS live; INFO elsewhere), `B19 framework-launcher-adds-python-app-and-main-image` (synthetic `X.framework/Versions/9.9/{bin/python9.9, Resources/Python.app/Contents/MacOS/Python, Python}` built from Mach-O header bytes under `.claude/tmp/`: `execs` gains the `Python.app` image, the closure roots gain `Versions/9.9/Python`; a same-named non-Mach-O file is NOT added), `B20 keg-scan-targeted-dirs-only-and-capped` (synthetic keg: a Mach-O file under `lib/python9.9/test/` is NOT scanned, one under `lib/python9.9/lib-dynload/` named without extension IS, and a targeted dir over `_KEG_SCAN_MAX_FILES` raises `MachOError`).
**Pattern to follow**: pure parser + bounded I/O split (P1 spirit); refusals name the image and rule, never bytes.
**Verification**: group B green; `B18` lists the `openssl@3` keg and the python keg, and its `execs` contain the python keg's `Python.app/Contents/MacOS/Python`.

### Step 9: mcp-sandbox — `run_script` parameter validation (pure)
**Files**: `Scripts/mcp-sandbox.py` (`validate_run_params(params: dict, cfg: SandboxConfig, healthy: FrozenSet[str]) -> RunRequest`, `_valid_relpath(p: str) -> Tuple[str, ...]`); `tests/test_mcp_sandbox.py` (group C); `tests/run.py` (update the `mcp_sandbox` typed count 38 -> 60 and its ledger comment; hunk-level, edit by content, PC-2; `tests/run.py:811` CASE COUNT DRIFT otherwise; R4:L4) (modify)
**Dependencies**: Step 7
**Description**:
- Accepted members exactly `{files, entry, interpreter, args, stdin, timeoutMs}`; unknown -> `_unknown_params_refusal` (P14) -> `invalid_params`.
- `files`: list, 1..`max_files`; each object exactly `{path, content}` + optional `{encoding, executable}`; `encoding` in {"utf8","base64"}; utf8 strict (lone surrogate refused); base64 `b64decode(validate=True)`; `executable` a real JSON bool; running decoded sum <= `max_files_bytes` checked per file before decoding the next.
- `_valid_relpath`: str, 1..1024 chars, no NUL, no `\\`, not absolute; components non-empty, not `.`/`..`, <= 255 UTF-8 bytes, no C0/C1/DEL, not starting `-`, depth <= 16; first component not `out`/`tmp`/`.sbx*`.
- Collisions: key `unicodedata.normalize("NFD", p).casefold()` duplicates refused; file-vs-dir prefix refused.
- `entry` in paths; `interpreter` configured AND healthy (plan state `ready`); `args` <= `max_args`, each <= `max_arg_chars`, no NUL; `stdin` UTF-8 <= `max_stdin_bytes`; `timeoutMs` int not bool, >= 1, clamped to `max_timeout_ms`.
- Rows C (22): `C1 dotdot-refused`, `C2 absolute-refused`, `C3 nested-dotdot-refused`, `C4 empty-component-refused`, `C5 dot-component-refused`, `C6 nul-refused`, `C7 backslash-refused`, `C8 control-char-refused`, `C9 reserved-out-refused`, `C10 entry-leading-dash-refused`, `C11 case-collision-refused`, `C12 nfd-nfc-collision-refused`, `C13 file-dir-prefix-collision-refused`, `C14 too-many-files-refused`, `C15 decoded-total-refused-before-next-decode`, `C16 bad-base64-refused`, `C17 timeout-bool-refused`, `C18 timeout-clamped`, `C19 entry-not-in-files-refused`, `C20 args-nul-refused`, `C21 stdin-too-large-refused`, `C22 unknown-member-did-you-mean`.
