### Step 10: mcp-sandbox — workdir lifecycle, materialisation, `out/` collection, cleanup
**Files**: `Scripts/mcp-sandbox.py` (`prepare_work_root(path) -> Tuple[int, int, int]` (root fd, lock fd, quarantine fd), `sweep_work_root(root_fd, quarantine_fd, deadline) -> int` (returns the quarantine count), `create_workdir(root_fd) -> Tuple[str, int]`, `materialize(wd_fd, files)`, `collect_out(wd_fd, limits) -> Tuple[list, bool]`, `remove_tree(root_fd: int, name: str, abs_root: str, deadline: float) -> bool` (KD-18), `_strip_acl_fd(fd: int) -> bool` / `_strip_acl_spelled(path: str) -> bool` (darwin, `ctypes`, KD-18 step 5), `class Quarantine` (`add(name)`, `count()`, `retry(deadline)`), `remove_workdir(root_fd, name, deadline) -> bool`; constants `_RM_MAX_OPS = 1_000_000`, `_QUARANTINE_MAX = 16`, `_FLATTEN_DEPTH = 50` (M0:9), `ACL_TYPE_EXTENDED = 0x00000100`, `FILESEC_ACL = 5`, `_FILESEC_REMOVE_ACL = 1` (M0:8); the KD-17 timing constants it uses — `_CLEANUP_DEADLINE_S`, `_STARTUP_SWEEP_DEADLINE_S`, `_DEGRADED_RETRY_S` — are defined in Step 7's timing block, R3:M4); `tests/test_mcp_sandbox.py` (group H); `tests/run.py` (update the `mcp_sandbox` typed count 60 -> 79 (H1-H19; M0:9 added H19) and its ledger comment; hunk-level, edit by content, PC-2; `tests/run.py:811` CASE COUNT DRIFT otherwise; R4:L4) (modify)
**Dependencies**: Step 9; Step 1 item 4 (remover prototype, `item-4.md`), item 4b (flatten, `results.md`), item 3l (ACL strip, `item-3l.md`)
**Description**:
- Startup: `prepare_work_root`: create 0o700 if absent; `lstat` not symlink; `realpath == path`; owner euid; `mode & 0o077 == 0`; `root_fd = os.open(root, O_RDONLY|O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW)`; then the single-owner lock (C-M1): `lock_fd = os.open(".lock", O_RDWR|O_CREAT|O_NOFOLLOW|O_CLOEXEC, 0o600, dir_fd=root_fd)`, `fstat` regular + owner euid, `fcntl.flock(lock_fd, fcntl.LOCK_EX | fcntl.LOCK_NB)`; `BlockingIOError`/`OSError` -> stderr `mcp-sandbox: refusing to start: work root <path> is in use by another runner (lock held)`, exit 1. The lock fd is held for the process lifetime and never passed to a child (`close_fds=True`, CLOEXEC). Then the quarantine directory (R2:S-H1a): `os.mkdir("quarantine", 0o700, dir_fd=root_fd)` if absent; `quarantine_fd = os.open("quarantine", O_RDONLY|O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW, dir_fd=root_fd)`; `fstat` owner euid, `mode & 0o077 == 0`, else refuse start. `sweep_work_root(root_fd, quarantine_fd, deadline)` runs only AFTER the lock is held, called once with `deadline = now + _STARTUP_SWEEP_DEADLINE_S` (20.0 s) — ONE deadline shared by every entry, each `remove_tree` call handed the same absolute `deadline`, never a fresh per-entry budget (R3:M4 = S-M2; with per-entry deadlines 17 hostile entries x 10 s already exceeds the runner proxy's `startup_timeout` 120 and the proxy's restart budget then disables the child for good): it first validates every name (below), then removes `^[0-9a-f]{32}$` leftovers with `remove_tree`; a leftover that still resists, or is not reached before the deadline, is quarantined (KD-18 step 6; a rename is cheap and not deadline-gated) rather than refusing start; it then retries `remove_tree` on `^[0-9a-f]{32}$` entries inside `quarantine/` (anything else in there -> refuse start) until the shared deadline expires — the entries not tried stay quarantined and count; `.lock` and `quarantine` are the only other allowed entries in the work root; any other entry -> refuse start; a quarantine count above `_QUARANTINE_MAX` after the sweep starts the runner `degraded` (it still answers `capabilities`), never exits. (The `shutil.rmtree.avoids_symlink_attacks` startup check is dropped with `shutil.rmtree`, KD-18.)
- `create_workdir`: `secrets.token_hex(16)`, `os.mkdir(name, 0o700, dir_fd=root_fd)`, open `O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW`; `tmp/` and `out/` via `dir_fd`.
- `materialize`: per component `os.mkdir(c, 0o700, dir_fd=cur)` + `O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW` open; leaf `os.open(leaf, O_WRONLY|O_CREAT|O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW, 0o700 if executable else 0o600, dir_fd=cur)`.
- `collect_out`: `os.open("out", O_RDONLY|O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW, dir_fd=wd_fd)` (ELOOP/ENOTDIR -> none); iterative `os.scandir(fd)`; `os.stat(..., dir_fd=fd, follow_symlinks=False)`; regular -> `O_RDONLY|O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK` open, `fstat` `S_ISREG` + `st_nlink == 1`; depth <= 8, entries <= 4096, files <= `max_out_files`, bytes <= `max_out_bytes`; any skip sets `outFilesTruncated`; content strict UTF-8 -> `"utf8"`, else base64.
- `remove_workdir(root_fd, name, deadline)` = `remove_tree(root_fd, name, abs_root, deadline)` — the iterative, fd-relative FLATTEN remover of KD-18 (explicit stack, no recursion; segments walked at most `_FLATTEN_DEPTH = 50` levels, every subdirectory at relative depth 50 lifted to a top-level `.sbx-flat-%07d` name with one fd-relative rename — flags cleared with `os.lchflags(<spelled>, 0)` first when the rename fails `EPERM` —, then each segment removed bottom-up; fd window <= 50; per directory: `chmod 0700` on an open refusal, darwin flag clear, then `os.fstat(fd)` and `os.fchmod(fd, 0o700)` before listing ONLY when `(st_mode & 0o700) != 0o700` (R2:M3, M0:9); darwin empty-ACL strip via `acl_set_fd_np`, spelled-path fallback `chmodx_np(_FILESEC_REMOVE_ACL)` or `acl_set_file` under the inode re-check, before giving up on an entry (R2:S-H1c, M0:8); bounded `_RM_MAX_OPS`; deadline checked before EVERY mutating operation (M0:9, replaces "every 256 operations"); loop until the workdir is gone). `shutil.rmtree` is not used anywhere. False -> **quarantine** (KD-18 step 6): `os.rename(name, name, src_dir_fd=root_fd, dst_dir_fd=quarantine_fd)`, or, if that rename fails, the name is kept in the in-memory quarantine set in place; the run's result is returned normally and the runner keeps serving. Only `Quarantine.count() > _QUARANTINE_MAX` sets `degraded = "quarantine full"` (runs refuse `sandbox_unavailable`, `capabilities` still answers); a degraded runner retries the quarantine before each run with `Quarantine.retry(now + _DEGRADED_RETRY_S)` (2.0 s, a KD-17 run term, R3:M4) and clears `degraded` once the count is back at or below the cap, else refuses that run `sandbox_unavailable`. After each run's own cleanup, `Quarantine.retry` runs at most once per 60 s inside that run's remaining `_CLEANUP_DEADLINE_S` (KD-17). Every call site reaches `remove_tree` through the MODULE GLOBAL (never a name bound at import or a default argument), so the in-process rows below can replace it.
- `_strip_acl_fd` / `_strip_acl_spelled` (darwin only; on any other platform they return False without loading anything): `libc = ctypes.CDLL(None, use_errno=True)` loaded lazily once; `libc.acl_init.restype = ctypes.c_void_p`, `argtypes = [ctypes.c_int]`; `libc.acl_set_fd_np.argtypes = [ctypes.c_int, ctypes.c_void_p, ctypes.c_int]`; `libc.acl_free.argtypes = [ctypes.c_void_p]`; for the spelled-path fallback (M0:8, replaces `acl_set_link_np`, which a `readattr`/`readsecurity` deny ACE defeats, `item-3l.md` §4): `libc.filesec_init.restype = ctypes.c_void_p`, `argtypes = []`; `libc.filesec_set_property.argtypes = [ctypes.c_void_p, ctypes.c_int, ctypes.c_void_p]` called as `(fsec, FILESEC_ACL, ctypes.c_void_p(_FILESEC_REMOVE_ACL))`; `libc.chmodx_np.argtypes = [ctypes.c_char_p, ctypes.c_void_p]`; `libc.filesec_free.argtypes = [ctypes.c_void_p]`; and as the second method `libc.acl_set_file.argtypes = [ctypes.c_char_p, ctypes.c_int, ctypes.c_void_p]` with an empty `acl_init(0)` (`sys/fcntl.h:585,615,617,620,623`, `sys/stat.h:413`, `sys/acl.h:199` of `MacOSX14.2.sdk`); `chmodx_np` is tried first, `acl_set_file` if it fails. Both FOLLOW symlinks, so `_strip_acl_spelled` re-runs the KD-18 step-3 prefix check AND `os.lstat(path)` of the entry itself (not a symlink, recorded `(st_dev, st_ino)`) IMMEDIATELY before each call. A NULL `acl_init`/`filesec_init` or a `-1` return -> False with `ctypes.get_errno()` logged as a name (`errno.errorcode`), never a path. The module never imports `ctypes` at top level on Linux paths that do not need it (import inside the darwin branch keeps import-time side effects nil).
- Free-space precheck: `shutil.disk_usage(root).free < min_free_bytes` -> `sandbox_unavailable`.
- **Injection mechanics for H15-H18 (R3:M5)**: the module is loaded IN-PROCESS (`H.load_module_from_path`, import side-effect free, NFR-2 spirit), the test builds a config with `load_config`, calls `prepare_work_root`, and constructs the runner state object directly; it then replaces the module global `remove_tree` (and, for H17/H18, the module constants `_STARTUP_SWEEP_DEADLINE_S` / `_DEGRADED_RETRY_S`) with `setattr` on the loaded module, restored in a `finally`. H15/H16 call `run_one(req)` on a worker thread with a real `/bin/bash` interpreter plan through the real sbx (live: macOS PASS, Linux and nested-sandbox INFO, P23); H17/H18 spawn nothing (pure timing rows, both OSes). No test hook, environment switch or flag exists in production code for any of them. **Where each turns green (R4:M3)**: H17 needs only `sweep_work_root` and is GREEN in this step; H15 and H16 need `run_one` (Step 11) and a ready `/bin/bash` plan (`plan_interpreter`, `measure_vsz`, `self_test`, `PlanCache`, Step 12), and H18 needs `run_one` (Step 11) and the runner state object with its `PlanCache` (Step 12) — all three are WRITTEN here and recorded RED (reason: `run_one` / the plan machinery not implemented yet, never ImportError), and verified GREEN in Step 12.
- Rows H (19; M0:9 added H19): `H1 workdir-0700-unique`, `H2 materialize-excl-nofollow`, `H3 materialize-executable-mode`, `H4 out-symlink-skipped`, `H5 out-hardlink-skipped`, `H6 out-fifo-no-hang`, `H7 out-replaced-by-symlink-empty`, `H8 cleanup-chmod000-and-uchg` (uchg darwin-only), `H9 low-disk-refused` (injected `disk_usage`), `H10 sweep-refuses-foreign-entry`, `H11 deep-tree-removed` (a 10 000-level directory chain inside a workdir is removed by `remove_workdir`, no `RecursionError`, runner not degraded; C-H5 = S-H2. M0:9: the chain is BUILT by rename-chaining 99-deep segments — an fd-relative `mkdir` chain is depth-priced on this host, 28 s to build 2000 levels and ~8.5 min for 10 000, `item-4.md` — and its flatten removal is extrapolated at ~1.4 s from item 4b's linear ~0.14 ms/level, UNMEASURED past 4059 levels; the row records its wall time), `H12 nested-chmod000-removed` (three nested `chmod 000` directories, each holding a file, removed; on darwin the innermost file also `chflags uchg`), `H13 dir-0500-and-0100-removed` (a `0500` directory holding a file — opens, every unlink would fail `EACCES` without the `fchmod` — and a `0100` directory holding a file, both removed, runner not degraded; R2:M3), `H14 acl-deny-delete-file-and-dir-removed` (darwin; INFO elsewhere: a file and a directory inside a workdir each get `chmod +a "everyone deny delete"` (and the directory also `deny list`), the workdir is removed through the empty-ACL strip, nothing quarantined; R2:S-H1c. M0:8: the ACEs are set by the TEST as the owner, outside any profile — inside the profile `file-write-acl` now refuses them; plus a further file and directory, each carrying the ONE "all rights" deny ACE of `item-3l.md` §4 (for the file incl. `read`, `readattr`, `readsecurity`, `writesecurity`, `delete`; for the directory incl. `list`, `search`, `delete_child`, `readattr`, `readsecurity`, `writesecurity`, `delete` — the shape that defeated the old `acl_set_fd_np` + `acl_set_link_np` pair), are removed through the `chmodx_np(_FILESEC_REMOVE_ACL)` / `acl_set_file` fallback), `H15 unremovable-workdir-quarantined-runner-serves` (a `remove_tree` stub injected to return False for one workdir: the run's result is returned normally, the workdir is under `quarantine/`, `capabilities` shows `quarantine.count == 1`, `degraded` null, the next run succeeds; removing the stub, the next sweep empties the quarantine; R2:S-H1a), `H16 quarantine-over-cap-degraded` (17 injected failures -> `degraded = "quarantine full"`, runs refuse `sandbox_unavailable`, `capabilities` still answers; the stub removed, the next run's pre-run retry empties the quarantine and the run succeeds), `H17 startup-sweep-bounded` (R3:M4 = S-M2: 40 planted `^[0-9a-f]{32}$` entries in `quarantine/`, `remove_tree` replaced by a stub that sleeps 0.5 s and returns False, `_STARTUP_SWEEP_DEADLINE_S` set to 2.0: `sweep_work_root` returns within 2.0 s + one stub call + 0.5 s scheduling slack, every planted entry is still present and counted (40, so the runner starts `degraded`), none was skipped by validation; the row also asserts the shipped constant is 20.0 and that `_STARTUP_SWEEP_DEADLINE_S + _STARTUP_PLANS_DEADLINE_S` (95) < 120, the documented runner-proxy `startup_timeout`), `H18 degraded-retry-within-run-bound` (R3:M4: a runner degraded with 17 planted entries and the same slow stub: a `run_one` returns `sandbox_unavailable` within `_DEGRADED_RETRY_S` + 0.5 s, spawned nothing, and the row asserts `_DEGRADED_RETRY_S + _REFRESH_DEADLINE_S + 120 + 10 + _KILL_GRACE_S + 0.5 + _CLEANUP_DEADLINE_S` <= 175, the KD-17 bound), `H19 flatten-removes-rename-chained-tree-within-deadline` (M0:9, item 4b: the test builds in a workdir, unsandboxed and cheaply, the tree a hostile script builds under the depth cap — 20 rename-chaining rounds of a 99-deep carrier renamed under a 99-deep parent and the parent lifted back, ~2079 levels; then, in a CHILD python process with soft `RLIMIT_NOFILE` 256 (so one fd per frame would hit EMFILE at ~252, `item-4.md` finding 4), it calls `remove_workdir(root_fd, name, time.monotonic() + _CLEANUP_DEADLINE_S)`: returns True before the deadline, the workdir is gone, nothing quarantined (no test hook in production code; the child reports only its wall time and the return value); the shipped constants are asserted `_FLATTEN_DEPTH == 50` and `_CLEANUP_DEADLINE_S == 10.0`; pure filesystem, both OSes; measured reference 0.29 s for 2079 levels).

   - > **IMPL:H11 follow-up (2026-10-11)** — `H11 deep-tree-removed` flapped on the clock: it failed 3 of 5 full `all` runs on 2026-10-11, the single `remove_workdir` call on the 10 098-level tree measuring between 1.01 s and 9.43 s against `_CLEANUP_DEADLINE_S = 10.0` (the plan's ~1.4 s extrapolation above was never a bound; the spread is host load). A FAIL that flaps breaks the severity model (`docs/subsystems/tests.md`, Idea 2). Correctness and timing are therefore split for H11 ONLY: the depth stays 10 098; the first call runs under the shipped deadline exactly as before and a clean `True` is a PASS; a `False` there is resumed on the same name with a fresh `_CLEANUP_DEADLINE_S` per call, up to 6 more calls (`H11_EXTRA_CALLS`, justified by the remover's monotone progress — lifted `.sbx-flat-*` segments persist, so each call starts on a strictly smaller tree — and matching production, where a deadline miss is quarantine + `Quarantine.retry`, not a failure). Completing clean on a resume is an INFO row (first-call wall time, number of calls, total time); never returning `True`, a workdir left behind, unclean residue, or any exception (`RecursionError`, ...) stays a FAIL. The "within the 10 s deadline" of row H11 above and of the Step 10 checklist line is now held by `H19` (timing, 2079 levels in a child with `RLIMIT_NOFILE` 256), while `H11` holds correctness at 10k levels. No `mcp-sandbox.py` change, no other row changed; `mcp_sandbox` stays 141.

### Step 11: mcp-sandbox — execution engine (spawn, capped read, status protocol, pgid adoption, slots, cancel)
**Files**: `Scripts/mcp-sandbox.py` (`build_sbx_argv(plan, req, wd, status_fd, cfg) -> List[str]`, `class RunSlots`, `run_one(req) -> dict`, `_read_capped(...)`, `_read_status_line(status_r, deadline) -> Optional[dict]`, `_cut_utf8(b: bytes) -> str`; FIRST CALLERS of the helpers written in Step 7 (R3:M1): `_spawn_sbx(argv: List[str], stdin_pipe: bool, status_w: int, cwd: str) -> subprocess.Popen` (R2:M2), `_Reclaim.adopt_pgid(pgid)`, `_run_child(cmd, timeout, stdin_text, env)` — host-local adaptation of `Scripts/mcp-inspect.py:458-499` with one added `env` argument passed to `Popen`, used ONLY for `/bin/ps` with `env={}`); `tests/test_mcp_sandbox.py` (groups D, I); `tests/files/mcp_sandbox/fake_sbx.py.tmpl` (create, the I11/G12/G13 pass-through fixture, R3:M5); `tests/run.py` (update the `mcp_sandbox` typed count 79 -> 94 (M0:9: +1 carried from Step 10's H19) and its ledger comment; hunk-level, edit by content, PC-2; `tests/run.py:811` CASE COUNT DRIFT otherwise; R4:L4) (modify)
**Dependencies**: Steps 4, 8, 10 (R4:M3: Step 8's closure is what the I rows' runner startup computes on macOS). The I rows' GREEN run also needs Step 12's startup (plans, VSZ, self-test) and is therefore verified in Step 12; Step 12 cannot be a dependency of this step because it calls `run_one` / `_spawn_sbx`, first called here.
**Description**:
- `build_sbx_argv` (pure, golden per platform):
  `[sys.executable, "-I", "-B", cfg.sbx, "--restrict-reads", "--clean-env",
    "--env", "PATH=<interp dirs>:/usr/bin:/bin", "--env", "HOME=<wd>", "--env", "TMPDIR=<wd>/tmp",
    "--env", "LANG=C.UTF-8", "--env", "OUT=<wd>/out",
    "--write", <wd>, *("--read", r) for r in plan.reads, *("--exec", e) for e in plan.execs,
    "--timeout", "%.3f" % (timeout_ms/1000), "--status-fd", str(status_fd),
    "--rlimit-as", str(plan.as_limit), "--rlimit-cpu", str(ceil(timeout)+1), "--rlimit-nproc", "+%d" % headroom,
    "--rlimit-fsize", str(fsize), "--rlimit-nofile", str(nofile), "--rlimit-core", "0",
    *(["--seccomp"] if seccomp_on else []), "--", *plan.argv_prefix, "<wd>/<entry>", *args]`
  `argv_prefix`: python3 `(path, "-I", "-S", "-B")`, bash `(path, "--noprofile", "--norc")`, node `(path,)`. Entry always absolute. Because every run passes `PATH`, sbx emits the exec-dir metadata subpaths for `/bin` and `/usr/bin` (M0:4, Step 3), so a script's bare `sleep`/`cat` resolves; `plan.reads` carries `/private/var/select/sh` on darwin (M0:5) and `plan.execs` carries realpaths only (M0:3). The `cwd` of every spawn is the workdir (`item-6.md` side finding 5: a cwd outside the read scope breaks bash's `shell-init`).
- `RunSlots(n)`: `threading.Lock` + counter; non-blocking `try_acquire() -> bool`; `release()`.
- `_spawn_sbx` (R2:M2; WRITTEN in Step 7 so the `cancel` KILL row is green from the file's first commit, R3:M1; specified here, where it is first called) — the ONE adopting sbx spawn, shared by `run_one`, `measure_vsz`, the `--version` run and `self_test` (Step 12): `reclaim = _REQUEST_RECLAIM.get(None)`; `raise RequestCancelled()` if `reclaim is not None and reclaim.cancelled` (R3:L4: a typed exception, never a generic failure); `proc = subprocess.Popen(argv, stdin=subprocess.PIPE if stdin_pipe else subprocess.DEVNULL, stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.PIPE, pass_fds=(status_w,), close_fds=True, start_new_session=True, cwd=cwd, env={"PATH": "/usr/bin:/bin", "LANG": "C.UTF-8"})`; `if reclaim is not None: reclaim.adopt(proc)` — the Popen is bound to a NAME and passed to a call named `adopt` in the same function, with `start_new_session=True`, which is exactly what `tests/test_cancel.py`'s KILL analyser keys on (`RECORD_CALLS = {"adopt"}` :302, the per-function check :788-803, `KILL_NO_SESSION` :804-809), so it passes with NO `KILL_EXEMPT` row. It returns the live Popen: the caller reads the status pipe and output itself (no `communicate()`), which is why `_run_child` cannot serve here (it has no `pass_fds` and blocks in `communicate()` to exit, `Scripts/mcp-inspect.py:478-489`). The caller closes `status_w` right after the call.
- `run_one` order (C-M6 = S-M2): (1) slot FIRST — `RunSlots.try_acquire()` non-blocking, refused -> `busy`, `retryAfterMs: 1000`, before any plan work; everything below runs inside `try: ... finally: release()`; (2) run deadline armed: `run_deadline = now + _RUN_DEADLINE_S` (175 s, KD-17); (3) precheck degraded — when degraded, `Quarantine.retry(now + _DEGRADED_RETRY_S)` first (R3:M4), still above the cap -> `sandbox_unavailable` — and free space; (4) plan state: `plan_deadline = now + _REFRESH_DEADLINE_S` fixed ONCE here and passed to `PlanCache.get(interpreter, plan_deadline)` (R4:M2; KD-13 signature check, then a wait on a running refresh and/or this run's own refresh, ALL inside that one 30 s term — its VSZ / version / self-test spawns therefore hold this slot and count against `max_concurrent`) -> `sandbox_unavailable`; (5) workdir; materialise; `status_r, status_w = os.pipe()`; `proc = _spawn_sbx(argv, stdin_pipe=stdin is not None, status_w=status_w, cwd=wd)` (adopted into `_Reclaim` inside the helper); close `status_w`; every status line parsed with `_strict_loads` (R2:L10); stdin writer thread; `selectors.DefaultSelector` over stdout/stderr/status, keep <= cap then discard (truncated flag); status buffer <= 4096 bytes (excess -> `internal`); on the `spawned` line -> `adopt_pgid(pgid)`; hard deadline `timeout + _HARD_DEADLINE_SLACK_S`: SIGTERM sbx pid, `_KILL_GRACE_S` (2.0 s, the same grace `_Reclaim.cancel` uses, R3:L5), SIGKILL sbx pid; after `Popen.wait()`: if no terminal `exit` event was seen and a pgid was adopted -> `os.killpg(pgid, SIGKILL)` (ESRCH ignored; KD-1/KD-2, row E33); drain <= 500 ms, close read ends (KD-8); no `spawned` + `refused` or exit 2/3 -> `sandbox_unavailable` with one sanitized sbx stderr line (<= 200 chars); after `spawned`, every outcome is a normal result, including an engine-stage failure (KD-16, declared); finally `collect_out`, `remove_workdir(root_fd, name, now + _CLEANUP_DEADLINE_S)` (False -> quarantine, KD-18 step 6; the result is still returned), the opportunistic `Quarantine.retry` inside the same deadline, release slot.
- `_cut_utf8(b)`: drop a trailing incomplete UTF-8 sequence, then `decode("utf-8", "replace")`.
- Result: `{"exitCode", "signal", "timedOut", "durationMs", "stdout", "stderr", "stdoutTruncated", "stderrTruncated", "outFiles", "outFilesTruncated"}` (`signal` = the NAME string `signal.Signals(n).name`, e.g. `"SIGKILL"`, or null; on a timeout `exitCode` is null, `timedOut` true and `signal` the kill signal's name — Contract item 5), as text JSON + `structuredContent`.
- Cancellation (`notifications/cancelled`): `McpServer._serve`'s `except asyncio.CancelledError: reclaim.cancel(loop); raise` arm (Step 7, copied from `Scripts/mcp-inspect.py:2793-2799`) runs on the loop thread: `_Reclaim.cancel` -> `_signal_group(proc, SIGTERM)` -> `os.killpg(sbx pid)` for every adopted sbx Popen, `loop.call_later(_KILL_GRACE_S, _escalate)` -> SIGKILL — the call path `tests/test_cancel.py:819-831` checks (`KILL_NO_ARM`, `KILL_NO_SIGNAL`; R3:L5). sbx traps the SIGTERM and kills the target group itself (T13); the run thread, unblocked by sbx's exit, sweeps the adopted target pgid only if no terminal `exit` event arrived (KD-1), removes the workdir in its `finally`, and its result is discarded. `tests/test_cancel.py` KILL class.
- Logging: one `log.info` per run, metadata only; content only when `log_content` is true.
- Rows D golden (4): `D1 darwin-sbx-argv-golden`, `D2 linux-sbx-argv-golden-seccomp`, `D3 entry-absolute`, `D4 runner-env-to-sbx-is-two-keys`. Rows I live (11; INFO under a nested sandbox, P23): `I1 hello-python`, `I2 hello-bash`, `I3 hello-node`, `I4 nonzero-exit-normal-result`, `I5 utf8-cut-on-boundary`, `I6 invalid-utf8-replaced`, `I7 stdin-delivered`, `I8 outfiles-utf8-and-base64`, `I9 cancel-kills-run-and-cleans`, `I10 sbx-refusal-is-sandbox-unavailable`, `I11 engine-failure-after-spawned-is-declared-result` (config `sbx` = the pass-through fixture below with the I11 trigger: result is normal with `exitCode: 1`, the message in `stderr`, `capabilities` shows `degraded: null`; pins KD-16). **Where they turn green (R4:M3)**: every I row starts the runner over stdio, and the runner refuses to start until Step 12's startup planning exists, so I1-I11 are WRITTEN here and recorded RED (reason: runner startup planning not implemented, never ImportError) and verified GREEN in Step 12; the D rows are pure (an `InterpreterPlan` built directly) and GREEN here.
- **The pass-through fixture sbx (R3:M5)** — the runner refuses to start unless EVERY startup spawn (VSZ probe, `--version`, self-test of each interpreter) succeeds through `cfg.sbx`, so a fixture that always fails would never reach a run. `tests/files/mcp_sandbox/fake_sbx.py.tmpl` is rendered by the test into `.claude/tmp/test_mcp_sandbox/<run>/fake_sbx` (mode 0700, owned by the euid — `_check_owned_file` accepts it; the real sbx's absolute path and the trigger directory substituted in), and named as `sbx` in that row's config. The runner launches it exactly as it launches sbx (`[sys.executable, "-I", "-B", cfg.sbx, ...]`). It parses only what it needs from its argv — the `--status-fd N` value and the target argv after `--` — and decides by the BASENAME of the target's entry path (the run's `<wd>/<entry>`; the self-test script `_SELF_TEST_NAME = "sbx-self-test"` a constant the runner writes into its self-test workdir; the VSZ probe and `--version` carry no entry file):
  - default (every startup spawn, every other run): `os.execv(sys.executable, [sys.executable, "-I", "-B", <real sbx>, *sys.argv[1:]])` — the status fd is inherited across the exec (it was passed by `pass_fds`, so it is not CLOEXEC), so the real sbx behaves exactly as in production;
  - entry basename `i11-engine-fail.sh`: start `/bin/sleep 0.2` with `start_new_session=True`, write `{"sbx":1,"event":"spawned","pid":P,"pgid":P}` to fd N, write `sandbox-exec: profile error\n` to stderr, wait for the sleep, write the terminal `{"sbx":1,"event":"exit","outcome":"exited","code":1,"signal":null,"durationMs":...}` (what real sbx writes when the engine exits 1 after `spawned`), exit 1;
  - entry basename `sbx-self-test` while the trigger file `<trigger dir>/block-selftest` exists: poll every 50 ms until the file is removed (bounded 30 s), then the default pass-through — this is how G12 and G13 hold a refresh inside its self-test.
**Pattern to follow**: ADR 0008 run loop (P13 §5a), P15, KD-7, KD-8.
**Verification**: group D green (pure, both OSes); group I (11) recorded RED for the stated reason, its GREEN verification is in Step 12 (R4:M3); `cancel` stays green (the first callers of `_spawn_sbx` change no analyser verdict).

### Step 12: mcp-sandbox — startup plans, VSZ baselines, self-test, self-refresh, capabilities
**Files**: `Scripts/mcp-sandbox.py` (`plan_interpreter(spec, cfg) -> InterpreterPlan`, `_chain_signature(paths) -> Tuple[Tuple[str, int, int], ...]`, `measure_vsz(...) -> int`, `self_test(plan, plan_deadline: float)` (R5:L1), `class PlanCache` with `get(name: str, plan_deadline: float) -> PlanState` (R4:M2), `capabilities() -> dict`, `LIVE_VERIFIED = {"darwin": True, "linux": False}`); `tests/test_mcp_sandbox.py` (group G); `tests/run.py` (update the `mcp_sandbox` typed count 94 -> 107 (M0:9) and its ledger comment; hunk-level, edit by content, PC-2; `tests/run.py:811` CASE COUNT DRIFT otherwise; R4:L4) (modify)
**Dependencies**: Steps 8, 10, 11 (Step 10: `Quarantine` for `capabilities()`'s `quarantine` member and the H rows turned green here)
**Description**:
- `plan_interpreter`: `_symlink_hops` + realpath; regular + executable; macOS -> `dylib_closure` -> reads = hops ∪ spellings ∪ kegs ∪ `extra_reads`; Linux -> reads = `extra_reads` plus the realpath dir when outside `/usr`,`/bin`,`/sbin`,`/lib*`; execs = realpath + `/bin` + `/usr/bin` (S-L1, declared in KD-6) + on macOS the framework launcher target (its realpath) from `_framework_launcher(realpath)` when the realpath is inside a `*.framework` (C-H2; its main image `Versions/<v>/Python` is a closure root) — the symlink hops are NOT exec entries, they stay in `reads` (M0:3: the filter matches realpaths, `item-3a-g.md` 3g); on macOS EVERY plan's reads also carry `--read /private/var/select/sh` (M0:5, C-L8 fallback ADOPTED, `item-3h-j.md` 3j: `/bin/sh` runs without it but writes `Error opening /private/var/select/sh: Operation not permitted` to stderr on every invocation; the one symlink literal plus the metadata of its ancestors `/private`, `/private/var`, `/private/var/select` — added by sbx's `metadata_ancestors` — removes it; its target `/bin/bash` needs no read, nothing under `/private/var` wider); signature over every hop, realpath, image, keg root and extra read (`os.lstat`: `st_ino`, `st_mtime_ns`); version via a sandboxed `--version` run (first line, sanitized, <= 120 chars).
- **Every subprocess in this step is adopted, through exactly two helpers (R2:M2)**: every SBX spawn — the VSZ probe (needs the status fd, a mid-run read of the `spawned` line to learn the target pid, and a held-open stdin), the `--version` run and `self_test` — goes through `_spawn_sbx` (written in Step 7, specified in Step 11: `Popen` + `pass_fds=(status_w,)` + `start_new_session=True` + `reclaim.adopt(proc)` in the same function); the caller reads the status pipe and the capped output itself, with a `timeout = remaining refresh deadline` (KD-17). Only the `/bin/ps -o vsz= -p PID` samples go through the host-local `_run_child` (`Scripts/mcp-inspect.py:458-499` shape: no fd to pass, nothing to read mid-run, `communicate()` is fine). At startup there is no request, so both helpers skip the `adopt` (the `if reclaim is not None` branch). No `subprocess.run`/`call`/`check_*` call appears in `Scripts/mcp-sandbox.py`, so `tests/test_cancel.py`'s KILL-class AST check (`BLOCKING_SPAWNS` :303, `KILL_BLOCKING` :308; adoption keyed on `.adopt`, `RECORD_CALLS` :302) passes with NO `KILL_EXEMPT` row (C-M3). Both helpers run inside a request during a refresh (C-M6).
- `measure_vsz(plan, cfg, plan_deadline: float) -> int` (macOS; Linux when `as_mode == "baseline"`; `plan_deadline` is the one passed into `PlanCache.get`, or the per-plan startup deadline): run the interpreter under the full production argv MINUS `--rlimit-as`, with a stdin-blocking snippet (`python3 -c "import sys; sys.stdin.read()"`, `bash -c "read -r _"`, `node -e "process.stdin.resume()"`), spawned by `_spawn_sbx(..., stdin_pipe=True, ...)` so its stdin stays open; target pid from the `spawned` line (read with `_read_status_line`, `_strict_loads`); sample `_run_child(["/bin/ps", "-o", "vsz=", "-p", str(pid)], min(5.0, plan_deadline - now), None, env={})` three times 100 ms apart (R4:L2: each `/bin/ps` sample is bounded by the REMAINING refresh/startup deadline, never a fixed 5 s — six fixed samples could otherwise add 30 s outside every deadline; a remaining time <= 0 before a sample, or a sample that times out, ends the refresh with reason `refresh deadline`), take the max (KiB -> bytes); same for the sbx launcher pid (`proc.pid`); close stdin, wait for the terminal `exit` event within the remaining refresh deadline. `as_limit = max(vsz_interp, vsz_sbx) + as_budget + 256 MiB`; `effectiveBudgetBytes = as_limit - vsz_interp`.
- `self_test(plan, plan_deadline: float)` (R5:L1): full production argv incl. `--rlimit-as`, script printing `ok` (the bash self-test uses builtins only — no external command, so it never depends on the M0:4 PATH rule; `item-6.md` side finding 4); its spawn's `timeout = min(10.0, plan_deadline - now)` — the 10 s is its own cap, never a term outside the deadline; the `--version` spawn follows the same rule (`timeout = min(<its own cap>, plan_deadline - now)`). A remaining time <= 0 before either spawn, or a spawn cut by the deadline (not by its own cap), ends the refresh `refresh-failed` with reason `refresh deadline`, as KD-17(a) (at startup: the refuse-to-start path below; a term partly spent waiting is handled by the `PlanCache.get` rule below, no state change); the KD-17 arithmetic (174.5 s) is unchanged because both spawns already sit inside the one 30 s plan term. Exit 0 + `ok\n` within that timeout, else startup: stderr `mcp-sandbox: refusing to start: interpreter <name> self-test failed (<outcome>)`, exit 1.
- `PlanCache.get(name: str, plan_deadline: float) -> PlanState` (KD-13; R4:M2): `plan_deadline` is fixed ONCE by the caller (`run_one` step 4: plan-phase start + `_REFRESH_DEADLINE_S`; at startup: `min(startup_deadline, now + _REFRESH_DEADLINE_S)` per plan) and is the ONLY deadline inside `get` — `get` never arms a fresh 30 s. Recompute `_chain_signature` of the plan's paths; equal -> current state; differs -> acquire the per-interpreter lock with `timeout = plan_deadline - now` (a lock held by another run's refresh is waited on until `plan_deadline` at most — not until that other refresh's own deadline); after acquiring, re-check the signature (another run may have refreshed meanwhile: equal -> its result); still differs -> recompute plan + VSZ + self-test against the SAME `plan_deadline` (every spawn's timeout is `plan_deadline - now`; R2:L6); success -> `ready`; any exception, or an exhausted deadline on a refresh that had the full term (it started at its run's plan-phase start, no wait before it), -> `refresh-failed` with `reason` = exception type + rule, or `refresh deadline` (no bytes). An exhausted `plan_deadline` that was partly spent WAITING (the lock wait timed out, or the own refresh was cut short by the earlier wait) refuses this run `sandbox_unavailable` ("refresh deadline") but is handled like a cancel: the `PlanState` is not replaced and no failure timestamp is recorded, because the refresh never had its full term. A refresh for the same failing signature is not repeated within 5 s (R-11). Bound: whatever the interleaving (a cancelled refresh, a second signature change mid-refresh), one run spends at most `_REFRESH_DEADLINE_S` in `get`, which is what KD-17 sums. **Cancellation (R3:L4 = S-L1)**: the refresh body is `try: ... except RequestCancelled: raise` placed BEFORE `except Exception` (the refresh-failed arm); a spawn that returns after the request's `_Reclaim` was cancelled (killed by `cancel()`, outcome signaled) is converted to `RequestCancelled` by checking `reclaim.cancelled` right after it, before its outcome is judged. On `RequestCancelled` the `PlanState` is NOT replaced (the prior `ready` plan, or the prior `refresh-failed` state, stays), no failure timestamp is recorded (the 5 s suppression never starts), the per-interpreter lock is released by its `with`, and the exception propagates to `run_one`, whose result the cancelled request discards. Waiters on the lock then run the refresh themselves under the REMAINDER of their own `plan_deadline` (R4:M2), never a fresh 30 s.
- Startup (R3:M4): `main()` computes every configured interpreter's plan against ONE shared `startup_deadline = now + _STARTUP_PLANS_DEADLINE_S` (75 s; each plan's spawns additionally capped by `_REFRESH_DEADLINE_S`), after `sweep_work_root`'s own 20 s; an exhausted startup deadline = stderr `mcp-sandbox: refusing to start: interpreter planning exceeded 75 s`, exit 1 (fail closed). Startup therefore answers `initialize` within ~95 s worst case, under the runner proxy's `startup_timeout` 120 (KD-17 "Startup bound").
- `capabilities()`:
  ```json
  {"interpreters": {"python3": {"path": "...", "realpath": "...", "version": "Python 3.14.0",
      "state": "ready", "reason": null,
      "asLimitBytes": 0, "effectiveBudgetBytes": 0, "reads": ["..."], "execs": ["..."]}},
   "limits": {"timeoutMs": 30000, "maxTimeoutMs": 120000, "...": "..."},
   "backend": {"platform": "darwin", "engine": "/usr/bin/sandbox-exec", "seccomp": false,
               "liveVerified": true, "sbx": "..."},
   "concurrency": {"max": 2, "inFlight": 0}, "quarantine": {"count": 0, "max": 16}, "degraded": null}
  ```
- Rows G (13; the live ones INFO under a nested sandbox, P23; G9, G10, G12, G13 use a fixture interpreter chain under `.claude/tmp/` — a symlink hop to `/bin/bash` that the test "re-points" by atomically replacing it (`os.symlink` to a temp name + `os.replace`: new `st_ino`, same target, so the KD-13 signature changes while the realpath stays a system binary) — and G12/G13 use the pass-through fixture sbx of Step 11 with its `block-selftest` trigger, R3:M5): `G1 missing-sbx-refuses-start`, `G2 interpreter-missing-refuses-start`, `G3 non-macho-interpreter-refuses-start`, `G4 work-root-world-writable-refuses-start`, `G5 work-root-symlink-refuses-start`, `G6 self-test-failure-refuses-start`, `G7 as-limit-above-launcher-vsz`, `G8 capabilities-reports-live-verified-per-platform`, `G9 plan-refreshes-on-signature-change` (fixture interpreter chain under `.claude/tmp/`: re-point a hop symlink; next run uses the recomputed plan), `G10 refresh-failure-refuses-runs-and-capabilities-reports` (planted unparseable image; run -> `sandbox_unavailable`; capabilities `state: "refresh-failed"`), `G11 work-root-lock-held-refuses-start` (a second runner on the same `work_root` exits 1 with the lock message while the first is up, and the first's in-flight workdir is untouched; C-M1), `G12 refresh-runs-inside-a-slot` (`max_concurrent = 2`; the runner started over stdio with the pass-through fixture as `sbx`; the test creates `block-selftest`, re-points the fixture hop, sends run A — its refresh's self-test spawn blocks inside the fixture —, sends run B, which is admitted and waits on A's refresh lock holding its own slot, and sends run C, which gets `busy` immediately; the test then removes `block-selftest` and A and B complete — the refresh held A's slot; C-M6 = S-M2), `G13 cancel-during-refresh-keeps-plan-state` (R3:L4 = S-L1: same setup; run A blocks in its refresh self-test; the test sends `notifications/cancelled` for A's request id: no reply for A, the fixture's self-test process group is gone within `_KILL_GRACE_S` + 1 s, `capabilities` shows the interpreter's `state` still `ready` with `reason` null (never `refresh-failed`); the test removes `block-selftest` and a run sent IMMEDIATELY after (well inside the R-11 5 s window) is not refused — it performs the refresh itself and succeeds with the recomputed plan. **Waiter bound (R4:M2)**, in the same row before `block-selftest` is removed: run B is sent right after A (B waits on A's refresh lock, holding its own slot); about 5 s later A is cancelled while `block-selftest` stays in place, so B's own refresh blocks in the fixture too; B's reply must be `sandbox_unavailable` ("refresh deadline") arriving within `_REFRESH_DEADLINE_S` + 2 s of B's send — one 30 s term, never ~55 s (wait + a fresh term) — and `capabilities` still shows `state: "ready"`, `reason: null` (B's term was partly spent waiting, so no `refresh-failed` and no 5 s suppression); only then is `block-selftest` removed for the immediate run above. The row is the suite's longest (~35 s); the `mcp_sandbox` forge timeout rule of §3 Build System covers it).
**Pattern to follow**: fail-closed startup like `_detect_engine` (P3); `_spawn_sbx` for every sbx spawn and `_run_child` (P15) only for `/bin/ps`, both with an explicit `stdin=` (P19).
**Verification**: group G (13) green; **the rows written red in earlier steps turn GREEN here (R4:M3)** — `F5 capabilities-shape` and `F6 capabilities-answers-during-two-runs` (Step 7), `H15`, `H16`, `H18` (Step 10), `I1`-`I11` (Step 11) — so after this step every `mcp_sandbox` row except group E (Step 13) is green; work root empty after each live row; startup on this host < 20 s for the three-interpreter config (recorded, against the 95 s bounded worst case and the proxy's `startup_timeout` 120, R3:M4); startup for the one-interpreter smoke config recorded against `READ_TIMEOUT` (R-12), which decides Step 14's `read_timeout` question.
  - > **IMPL:062** — `plan_interpreter` also puts every non-system closure IMAGE realpath into `reads` (the interpreter's own realpath included): redundant inside a keg, but a non-keg image reached through a symlink (G9's hop chain to `/bin/bash`) would otherwise have no literal read. The KD-13 signature paths are the spelling's hops, the realpath, every closure image, every keg root, the framework launcher (and its hops) and every `extra_reads` entry. A refresh-failed state's signature watches the last plan's paths plus the spec's hops, realpath and extra reads, so a repair is seen.
  - > **IMPL:063** — the signatures carry what the refresh needs: `measure_vsz(plan, cfg, plan_deadline, wd) -> (vsz_interp, vsz_sbx)` (not one int: `as_limit` needs the max, `effectiveBudgetBytes` the interpreter's) and `self_test(plan, plan_deadline, cfg, wd)`. Every refresh makes ONE planning workdir (`create_workdir` + the `sbx-self-test` file via `materialize`) that is the cwd and write scope of all three spawns, removed after (quarantined when it resists, which can set `degraded`). **Each planning spawn's own cap (10 s) is handed to sbx as `--timeout`; the runner waits for the spawn until `plan_deadline` only.** Read literally as a runner-side `min(10, remaining)` wait, G13's waiter B (its own refresh blocked in the fixture's self-test from ~6 s) would end at ~16 s by the 10 s cap -> `refresh-failed` + the R-11 suppression, contradicting the plan's own G13 ("refresh deadline" within 32 s, state stays `ready`); with the cap inside sbx, "cut by its own cap" is sbx's `timeout` outcome (a self-test failure) and "cut by the deadline" is the runner stopping the spawn at `plan_deadline` (`_RefreshDeadline`). Measured: B answered at 30.01 s (bound 32).
  - > **IMPL:064** — the `healthy` set `h_run_script` hands `validate_run_params` is `PlanCache.admitted()`: ready, OR refresh-failed and due a retry (signature changed, or the R-11 5 s window over). A literal "ready only" set would refuse a refresh-failed interpreter at validation forever, so "the next run retries the refresh" (KD-13, G10's repair) could never happen. A deadline exhausted after a lock wait is raised from `PlanCache.get` as `RunRefusal("sandbox_unavailable", "... refresh deadline ...")`, answered by `handle_sandbox_call`'s RunRefusal arm.
  - > **IMPL:065** — `sweep_work_root` returns only a count, so a startup leftover whose rename into `quarantine/` failed ("stuck in place") was lost to the runner's `Quarantine`. The plan is silent; `RunnerState.__init__` therefore lists the work root's top level for `^[0-9a-f]{32}$` names (no run has happened yet, so each one is a leftover the sweep could not move) and hands each to `Quarantine.add` (which retries the rename and otherwise keeps it stuck), so it counts toward `_QUARANTINE_MAX` and is retried -- fail closed toward `degraded`. Startup also checks the engine (`/usr/bin/sandbox-exec`; `bwrap` on `/usr/bin:/bin`) before the work root and exits 1 when it is missing.
  - > **Startup measured (task-066, this host, macOS 14.2.1, time to the `initialize` reply, n=5)**: one interpreter (`/bin/bash`, the smoke config) median 0.938 s (0.930-0.944) — far under `READ_TIMEOUT` 8.0 and the 6 s threshold, so Step 14 adds NO per-entry `read_timeout` (R-12); three interpreters (Homebrew python3, `/bin/bash`, node with the openssl.cnf read) median 2.699 s (2.679-2.732) — under the 20 s expectation, the 95 s bound and the proxy's `startup_timeout` 120. The suite's own I-runner start (same three) recorded 2.70 s. M0 estimated 78.6 ms / 465.6 ms for the raw spawns; the rest is the runner's own start, the Mach-O walk, the VSZ samples (3 x 100 ms per interpreter) and the sweep.

Canary dir `.claude/tmp/mcp_sandbox-canary-<rand>/secret.txt` stands in for the store root. Every row ends with "work root is empty" (only `.lock` and an empty `quarantine/`). The P23 nested-sandbox control runs first; under a nested Seatbelt every row is INFO (R2:M8). Rows (34; macOS live PASS, Linux-only rows INFO):
`E1 read-ssh-denied`, `E2 read-store-root-denied`, `E3 list-home-denied`, `E4 stat-oracle-denied`, `E5 write-outside-denied`, `E6 tcp-connect-denied`, `E7 unix-socket-denied`, `E8 fork-bomb-contained`, `E9 infinite-loop-timeout-group-killed`, `E10 setsid-orphan-no-hang`, `E11 stdout-flood-10gib-capped` (runner RSS delta < 32 MiB), `E12 huge-alloc-normal-result`, `E13 out-symlink-not-followed`, `E14 out-hardlink-skipped`, `E15 out-fifo-no-hang`, `E16 env-exact-five-keys` (exactly the five `--env` keys plus the M0-3h `ENV_ADDITIONS` named for the run's interpreter — measured, M0:7: python3 none, node none, bash `PWD SHLVL _` as seen by a child; Step 4 `T1` — the set is asserted equal, never "a superset of"; canary and `MCP_PROXY_TOKEN` absent; R2:L5), `E17 workdir-deleted-every-outcome` (ok, nonzero, timeout, sbx refusal, chmod 000 tree, chflags uchg (allowed inside the profile; cleared by the owner's `lchflags(0)`, M0:8), and — darwin — a script that tries `chmod +a "everyone deny delete"` on a file and a subdirectory of its workdir: refused at the source by the `file-write-acl` deny (the script sees EPERM, the run is a normal result, M0:8); the workdir is removed, never quarantined; R2:S-H1), `E18 concurrent-limit-busy-immediately`, `E19 fd-inheritance-only-stdio` (asserted by `os.fstat` over fds 0-255 inside the script, NOT by listing `/dev/fd`, which the profile denies — `item-3a-g.md` 3f, M0:15), `E20 no-tty`, `E21 exec-own-binary-denied` (a planted binary AND a planted `#!/bin/sh` script, both EPERM at exec, `item-3a-g.md` 3b; macOS proof only; INFO on Linux, which has no exec filter — declared, R2:L8), `E22 signal-runner-denied` (the runner's pid and, M0:2, a concurrent sibling run's target pid both EPERM under `(target same-sandbox)`; the script's own background job IS killable), `E23 status-fd-forge-ignored`, `E24 cpu-rlimit-applied`, `E25 fsize-efbig-normal-result`, `E26 nofile-emfile-normal-result`, `E27 rlimit-raise-denied`, `E28 proc-environ-hidden` (Linux INFO), `E29 dot-dot-and-absolute-files-refused`, `E30 dev-null-usable`, `E31 interpreter-keg-not-writable`, `E32 sibling-of-ancestor-stat-denied`, `E33 sbx-killed-target-group-killed-by-runner` (SIGKILL the sbx pid mid-run; the target group dies via the adopted pgid; result returned, slot released), `E34 setuid-exec-stays-confined` (R2:S-L2, assertions fixed by M0:10, `item-3k.md`: a bash script runs `/usr/bin/crontab -l`, `/usr/bin/at -l` and `/usr/bin/sudo -n true` through `/bin/sh -c` with stdin `/dev/null`; each exec is REFUSED by Seatbelt itself — sh exit **126** and `Operation not permitted` in stderr for each, the binary never starts (kernel operation `forbidden-exec-sugid`; the row does not read the kernel log) — and the run returns a normal result; NOT "the binary runs and fails its own reads"; Linux INFO, where bwrap's `NO_NEW_PRIVS` is declared, not live-tested).
**Pattern to follow**: spec "Acceptance tests"; §5 matrix.
**Verification**: group E green on macOS; `mcp_sandbox` typed count set (planned 141 = A10 + B20 + C22 + D4 + E34 + F8 + G13 + H19 + I11, M0:9; final at the red run).
  - > **IMPL:068** — group E landed with 34 rows; `mcp_sandbox` is **141** as planned. Every row pairs the in-run attempt with a positive control (the same probe run unsandboxed by the test, or the same action on a granted target inside the run). `E1`-`E18` passed at the first live run (macOS 14.2.1) with no sbx or mcp-sandbox change. Measured beside them: the fork bomb stopped at `EAGAIN` after 70 forks (nproc `+64` over the uid's count at spawn), the 10 GiB flood took 2.8 s with a runner RSS delta of 0.1 MiB, the setsid orphan survived as T11 declares (recorded, killed by the test), and the existence oracle answered as L5/IMPL:029 declares.
  - > **IMPL:069** — `E19`-`E34` passed live except **`E28`, an OPEN containment finding**. `E22` measured the T10 open question: a CONCURRENT run's target pid is NOT signalable under `(target same-sandbox)` (`EPERM`, like the runner and sbx's parent), so two `sandbox-exec` invocations are separate sandboxes. On macOS there is no `/proc`, so `E28` checks the T6 analogue: `sysctl` `KERN_PROCARGS2` of the runner's pid. **Measured: it returns the runner's argv AND environment from inside the restricted profile (`MCP_PROXY_TOKEN` seen)**. The same holds for any same-uid process. The clean env (KD-5) therefore does not hide the environment of OTHER processes, and T35's "info leak (hostname, hw model)" under-states what the unfiltered `(allow sysctl-read)` (sbx:236) admits. Tried and reverted: `(deny sysctl-read (sysctl-name "kern.procargs") (sysctl-name "kern.procargs2"))` and `(sysctl-name-prefix "kern.procargs")` placed after the allow do NOT close it. A blanket `(deny sysctl-read)` aborts node (M0:6) and also python's `import ctypes` (SIGABRT). `(deny default)` already denies every `process-info*` operation. No measured rule closes it, so no sbx change was made. `E28` stays red until a fix or a decision lands; it is never declared away here.
  - > **IMPL:069 follow-up (decision)** — operator decision (Zoltán, 2026-10-11): `E28` is a **DECLARED LIMIT**, no further measurement. Under restricted Seatbelt a script can read the argv AND environment of any same-uid process via `sysctl` `KERN_PROCARGS2`; the cause is the unfiltered `(allow sysctl-read)` in `_seatbelt_restricted_head`, which node and python need, and name/prefix-filtered denies of `kern.procargs2` had no effect. This supersedes T35's narrower "info leak (hostname, hw model)" description and is to be recorded in ADR 0031's limits. sbx is unchanged. `E28` is now an **INFO** row: it still runs the `KERN_PROCARGS2` probe and still requires its positive control (a failed control or broken probe stays FAIL), and its INFO detail states whether the leak was observed and cites "declared limit, ADR 0031". `mcp_sandbox` stays 141.

### Step 14: mcp-sandbox — smoke-harness registration (smoke, footprint, name_existence)
**Files**: `Scripts/_mcp_smoke_test.py` (`SERVERS` :105, `NEAR_MISS_FUNCTION` :484 (mcp-inspect row :500), `NEAR_MISS_PARAM` :527 (mcp-inspect row :534), `DOCUMENTED_CALL` :561; `ORIGINAL_PARAM_REFUSERS` :579 is NOT changed; new helper `_sandbox_smoke_config_text(work_root: str) -> str`, R4:L1); `tests/test_mcp_footprint.py` (`probe_argv` :345, `measure_server` :373 and its one call site :2444-2446, which already holds `smoke` from :2434); `tests/test_name_existence.py` (`probe_argv` :530-554, `probe_server` :557-583, `build_inventory` :586-592; R3:M6, R4:L1) (modify). The glob-discovered fleet tables (`read_loop`, `cancel`, `wire_log`, `handler_crash`, `protocol_version`) and `py_deps` are NOT here any more: they land in Step 7 with the file (R3:M1). The smoke-harness suites are derived counts (`None`), so this step moves no typed count.

**Description**: One row per table, shaped like the neighbouring mcp-inspect rows (`Scripts/_mcp_smoke_test.py:129`):
- smoke (C-M4; R4:L1): the mcp-sandbox config is GENERATED AT RUNTIME, never checked in with a host path. The helper in `Scripts/_mcp_smoke_test.py` is PURE — it RETURNS the config TEXT and writes nothing: `_sandbox_smoke_config_text(work_root: str) -> str` = `json.dumps({"version": 1, "sbx": <repo>/ClaudeCode/skills/sandbox-run/scripts/sbx, "work_root": work_root, "interpreters": {"bash": {"path": "/bin/bash"}}, "limits": {...defaults...}}, indent=2) + "\n"`. Every launch path WRITES that text with its own write path, so each suite's write record stays complete. Reason: both `tests/test_name_existence.py` and `tests/test_mcp_footprint.py` guarantee "every write lands under .claude/tmp" structurally — their `write_text` is the module's only write path and appends every target to `WRITES` (`tests/test_name_existence.py:425-427`, the rule stated at :319-322 and checked at :2317; `tests/test_mcp_footprint.py:233-235`, checked at :2360) — so a helper that wrote the file itself would put a write outside that record. The smoke harness has no fixture-base constant (R3:L6): its own call site makes `base` with the existing pattern `tmp_root = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(SCRIPT_DIR), ".claude", "tmp")`; `os.makedirs(tmp_root, exist_ok=True)`; `tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="sandbox_smoke_", dir=tmp_root)` (`Scripts/_mcp_smoke_test.py:1029-1031`), writes `_sandbox_smoke_config_text(os.path.join(base, "work"))` to `<base>/sandbox.json` (opened `0o600`), and removes `base` with `shutil.rmtree(..., ignore_errors=True)` after the row (the runner's work root lives inside it, so nothing survives). The runner itself creates `work_root` 0700 (`prepare_work_root`, Step 10); the config file must pass `_check_owned_file` (`mode & 0o022 == 0`), and `write_text` creates with `open(path, "w")` (mode `0o666 & ~umask`, group-writable under a `002` umask), so each suite follows its `write_text` with `os.chmod(path, 0o600)` on the path it just recorded (no new write target); smoke opens it `0o600` directly. The `SERVERS` row is `{"file": "mcp-sandbox.py", "tool": "sandbox_call", "args": ["--config", "{sandbox_config}"], "registered": False, "forced_exc_code": -32602}` (R2:M1: the forced-exception probe sends a non-object `params`, which mcp-sandbox refuses as -32602 before any handler, exactly as mcp-search does, `Scripts/_mcp_smoke_test.py:113-120`, read at :1487-1490) with the placeholder substituted where the row is launched (`run_server(cfg)` :1432). **Threading `smoke` down (R4:L1)**: neither suite's launcher holds the smoke module today — `build_inventory` passes only `smoke.launch_prefix(cfg, ("-B",))` to `probe_server(cfg, sandbox, prefix)`, which calls `probe_argv(path, args, sandbox, prefix)` (`tests/test_name_existence.py:586-592`, :557, :566, :530); footprint's call site passes only the prefix to `measure_server(cfg, sandbox, prefix)`, which calls `probe_argv(path, args, sandbox, prefix)` (`tests/test_mcp_footprint.py:2444-2446`, :373, :387, :345). Both chains gain a trailing `smoke` parameter: name_existence `probe_server(cfg, sandbox, prefix, smoke)` and `probe_argv(path, args, sandbox, prefix, smoke)`, `build_inventory` passing its `smoke` (:588); footprint `measure_server(cfg, sandbox, prefix, smoke)` and `probe_argv(path, args, sandbox, prefix, smoke)`, the call site passing the `smoke` it loads at :2434. In each `probe_argv`, beside the existing `--project-root` rewrite, a `{sandbox_config}` token is replaced by `cfg_path = os.path.join(sandbox, "sandbox-config", "sandbox.json")` after `write_text(cfg_path, smoke._sandbox_smoke_config_text(os.path.join(sandbox, "sandbox-config", "work")))` + `os.chmod(cfg_path, 0o600)` — the suite's OWN recorded `write_text`, so `WRITES` names it and the end-of-run check passes: name_existence's `sandbox` is `FIXTURE_ROOT/server-root` (:589; `FIXTURE_ROOT` :370 replaced per run under `FIXTURE_BASE` :369, checked at :2317), footprint's is `<mkdtemp under FIXTURE_BASE>/server-root` (:2438-2441, `FIXTURE_BASE` :183, checked at :2360); the runner's `work_root` lives in the same directory. The recorded `CHILD_ARGV` then carries a config path inside the sandbox, so the "no probed child is given a root outside the sandbox" records stay true. Without the substitution name_existence would hand a literal `{sandbox_config}` to `--config`, the runner would refuse to start and the row would be SKIP (:2395) forever (R3:M6). All three launch paths call the ONE text helper from the smoke module, so they cannot drift; only the WRITE is per suite. Unstartable host (no engine) -> SKIP as for other rows. Startup must fit `READ_TIMEOUT = 8.0` (:139): one `/bin/bash` interpreter only (R-12; M0 estimate 78.6 ms median / 118.0 ms cold for the raw spawns, `item-6.md`, M0:11 — no per-entry `read_timeout` planned); if the Step 12 measurement exceeds 6 s, add a per-entry `read_timeout` key to `SERVERS`, honoured by `run_server` (:1432) for the `initialize` read (`read(self, timeout=READ_TIMEOUT)` :188 already takes a timeout) and by the footprint suite's launch, and document it in the table's header comment — never raise the global value.
- smoke near-miss rows: `NEAR_MISS_FUNCTION["mcp-sandbox.py"] = ("run_scrip", "run_script")`; `NEAR_MISS_PARAM["mcp-sandbox.py"] = ("run_script", {"entri": "x"}, "entry")` (refused before any run); and — because mcp-sandbox is a LATER param refuser, NOT one of the six `ORIGINAL_PARAM_REFUSERS` (:579, a closed historical set) — a `DOCUMENTED_CALL["mcp-sandbox.py"] = ("capabilities", {})` row, which satisfies the "documented-call row iff a later param refuser" check (:670-677).
**Pattern to follow**: the `SERVERS` header comment (:105-120) and the mcp-inspect near-miss rows.

### Step 15: M3 — mcp-proxy per-child `relist`
**Files**: `Scripts/mcp-proxy.py:469-475` (ChildSpec), `:486` (`_CHILD_KEYS`), `:589-627` (`_cfg_child`), `:1267-1295` (ChildSlot init), new `ChildSlot.refresh_tools()` after `_list_all_tools` `:1354-1400`, `:1523-1547` (`ChildSlot.stop`), `:1657-1667` (aggregate), `:1902-1904` (`tools/list`) (modify); `tests/files/mcp_proxy/tf_stub_child.py` (knobs); `tests/test_mcp_proxy.py` (new constant `GM = "M. relist"` after `GL` :126, `def group_m(suite, fixture_root)` defined beside `group_l` :5015, and CALLED BEFORE `group_l` in the run order :5088-5099 — `group_k(suite)` / `group_m(suite, fixture_root)` / `group_l(suite, fixture_root, pyc_before, tree_before)` — because group L is the hygiene snapshot that must see every fixture write, R3:L1); `tests/run.py` `mcp_proxy` row :726-738 (count 112 -> 123 at :738, ledger comment :713-725 extended "... = 112; the relist work added group M, M1-M11 = 123"; description names relist; hunk-level, edit by content, P16); `project-forge.yaml:352` (`mcp_proxy` description: "112 typed cases" -> "123 typed cases" and a group M clause, R2:M7), `project-forge.yaml:354` (`timeout: 420`, re-measured, R3:L7)
**Dependencies**: none (independent of M1/M2)
**Description**:
- Config: `relist = entry.get("relist", False)`; `if not isinstance(relist, bool): raise ConfigError(f"{where}: relist must be a boolean")`; `ChildSpec(..., relist=relist)`.
- Constants: `_RELIST_TIMEOUT_S = 10.0`, `_RELIST_MIN_INTERVAL_S = 1.0`.
- `ChildSlot`: `self.relist_task: Optional[asyncio.Task] = None`, `self.relisted_at = 0.0`.
  ```python
  async def refresh_tools(self) -> None:
      """Re-list this child's tools (opt-in "relist"); the NAME SET is frozen.
      Coalesced: one relist in flight per slot. A failure keeps the last good
      descriptors (stale is safe); a changed name set disables the slot, exactly
      as a restart that changed it does."""
  ```
  Body: return unless `self.spec.relist and self.state == "ready" and self.client is not None`; return if younger than `_RELIST_MIN_INTERVAL_S`; if a relist task is running -> `await asyncio.wait_for(asyncio.shield(task), _RELIST_TIMEOUT_S)` (TimeoutError swallowed); else start `_relist_once(self.client)`, whose FIRST statement stamps the attempt — `self.relisted_at = loop.time()` — before any await (R4:L3 = security LOW, CWE-400: the min-interval must bound FAILING relists too; stamping only on success would let a child whose `tools/list` keeps failing or timing out be re-listed on every upstream `tools/list`, one 10 s child call per client call): `tools = await asyncio.wait_for(self._list_all_tools(client), _RELIST_TIMEOUT_S)`; `(ChildStartError, ChildUnavailable, asyncio.TimeoutError, OSError)` -> `log.warning("child=%s relist failed: %s; serving the last listing", ...)` (the attempt stamp stays, so the next relist waits `_RELIST_MIN_INTERVAL_S`); if `client is not self.client or self.state != "ready"` -> return (restart raced); name set differs -> `state = "disabled"`, `disabled_reason = "tool set changed on relist"`, `log.error(... added=%s removed=%s ...)` as P9, stop the client via `asyncio.ensure_future(client.stop())` with the existing failure-logging callback; else `self.tools = tools` (`relisted_at` was already stamped at the attempt's start, R4:L3).
- `ChildSlot.stop()` (mcp-proxy.py:1523-1547, C-L7): right after `self.stopping = True` and the restart-task cancel, the same for the relist task — `rt = self.relist_task; if rt is not None and not rt.done(): rt.cancel(); await asyncio.wait({rt}, timeout=_STOP_WAIT_S)` — so no relist outlives its slot or races the final `"disabled"`; `refresh_tools` returns immediately when `self.stopping`.
- `McpServer`: `self._relist_slots` = slots with `spec.relist`; in `tools/list` (:1902): when non-empty, `await asyncio.gather(*(s.refresh_tools() for s in self._relist_slots), return_exceptions=True)`, then rebuild the aggregate in config order (routing unchanged: names are unchanged).
- Stub knobs, added to `tf_parse_args` (`tests/files/mcp_proxy/tf_stub_child.py:369-379`, whose only delay knob today is `--init-delay` :378) and documented in the module docstring's flag list (ends with `--init-delay` at :40): `--desc-counter` (description `"tf stub tool (list #N)"`), `--toolset-reread` (re-read `--toolset-file` at every tools/list), `--list-error-after N`, and `--list-delay S` (R2:M9; float, default 0.0: `time.sleep(S)` before answering every `tools/list` AFTER this process's first, in `tf_list_tools` (:288), so the proxy's startup listing stays fast). It does NOT mirror `--init-delay`'s mechanism (R3:L6): `--init-delay` (:325-326) keys on `state.prior`, the number of PRIOR PROCESS STARTS read from the `.pids` file (:109-119), so it delays a RESTARTED child's initialize. A relist happens inside ONE process, so `--list-delay` needs its own per-process counter: `self.list_count = 0` in the state's `__init__` (:100-119), incremented under `tf_state_lock` at the top of `tf_list_tools`, sleep when `list_count >= 2`. The counter counts `tools/list` REQUESTS (a paged listing's follow-up pages count too; group M uses no `--page-size`) — needed by `M7 relist-coalesced` (two concurrent upstream `tools/list` while one relist is in flight must cause exactly one child `tools/list`, counted from `events.jsonl`) and `M11 relist-task-cancelled-on-stop` (shutdown while the child is still inside its delayed `tools/list`).
- Rows M (11): `M1 relist-non-bool-refused`, `M2 relist-default-off-descriptions-frozen`, `M3 relist-description-refreshes`, `M4 relist-off-byte-identical-listing`, `M5 relist-name-set-change-disables` (call isError quotes "tool set changed on relist"; sibling unaffected), `M6 relist-failure-serves-stale` (stub `--list-error-after 1`: the failing relist serves the last good descriptors; then — R4:L3 — a second upstream `tools/list` sent within 1 s of the failed one causes NO second child `tools/list`, counted from `events.jsonl`, so the min-interval bounds failing relists too), `M7 relist-coalesced`, `M8 relist-min-interval`, `M9 relist-over-http-front`, `M10 relist-during-restart-serves-stale`, `M11 relist-task-cancelled-on-stop` (stub with a slow `tools/list`; proxy shutdown during a relist completes within `_STOP_WAIT_S` with no "Task was destroyed but it is pending" warning and no child left; C-L7).
- No code change for the runner's body cap: deployment passes `--max-body-bytes 8388608` (mcp-proxy.py:2919).
**Pattern to follow**: P8, P9; restart-loop race comments (mcp-proxy.py:1434-1503).
- `project-forge.yaml` `mcp_proxy` timeout (R3:L7): group M adds real waits (the `--list-delay` rows M7/M11, the 1 s min interval M8, the relist timeout path); after group M is green, run `forge_call test` target `mcp_proxy` once, record the wall time, and set `timeout` to `max(420, ceil(1.5 * measured / 60) * 60)` — the same measured + 50 %, rounded-up-to-60 rule as the `all` target (`project-forge.yaml:42-45`) — with a one-line comment above it naming the measurement. Group M keeps every delay <= 2 s so the suite's growth stays small.
**Verification**: `mcp_proxy` = 123 green (planned; final at the red run) inside its re-measured timeout; the existing 112 unchanged; group L's hygiene rows still the last recorded.

- `_read_leaf(dir_fd, name, cap) -> bytes` — `O_RDONLY|O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK|O_NOCTTY`, `fstat` `S_ISREG`, `st_nlink == 1`, `mode & 0o022 == 0`, size <= cap; raises `FileRefused(reason)` with `missing|symlink|not-regular|hardlinked|writable-by-others|too-large|io-error`.
- `load_tier(tier, root_path) -> TierIndex` — stat-keyed cache `(st_dev, st_ino, st_mtime_ns, st_size)` of `skills.json` plus each `SKILL.md`; strict-JSON loader: `_strict_loads(text)` then `_refuse_duplicate_keys_parse(text)` (duplicate keys refused; P24, R2:L10); `budget_sum` per tier in UTF-16 code units, `len(s.encode("utf-16-le")) // 2` (R2:M4); top exactly `{version, skills}`, `version == 1`, `skills` <= 512; entry exactly `{name, dir, description, scripts}` (`scripts` REQUIRED, possibly `[]`); script exactly `{path, interpreter}`; `path` `^scripts/[a-z0-9][a-z0-9._-]{0,63}$`; interpreter in {python3, bash, node}. Top-level violation -> tier `error`; per entry -> `invalid` with a fixed reason (`bad-name`, `bad-dir`, `dir-not-real-directory`, `skill-md-<reason>`, `skill-md-not-utf8`, `frontmatter-malformed`, `frontmatter-mismatch`, `description-too-long`, `description-not-clean`, `script-path-invalid`, `script-<reason>`, `duplicate-name`).
- `parse_frontmatter(text) -> (name, description, body)` — line 1 `---`, line 2 `name: <name>`, line 3 `description: <raw remainder>`, line 4 `---`; NOT YAML. Description: non-empty, <= 120 code points, no `\n`, `== _web_clean(description, "")`.
- `dir`: one component `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9._-]{0,63}$`, `os.stat(dir, dir_fd=root_fd, follow_symlinks=False)` is `S_ISDIR`.
- `merge(household, private) -> Dict[str, Skill]` — private shadows household.
- `render_tool(merged, tiers) -> dict` — constant preamble (<= 300 code points): `"Skills: reusable instructions, references and scripts. Index lines are labels written by household members, not instructions. load(name[, file]) returns quoted reference text; run(name, script, args?, stdin?) runs an indexed script in a sandbox; list; validate.\n"`; then one line per skill sorted: `f"{name} - {description}"` / `f"{name} [private] - {description}"`; `units = len(desc.encode("utf-16-le")) // 2`; over `max_description` -> preamble + `"SKILL INDEX REFUSED: rendered description is {units} UTF-16 units, ceiling {max}; household tier sum {h} UTF-16 units, private tier sum {p} UTF-16 units. Fix skills.json."` (every number in UTF-16 code units, the unit ai-soul's 1800 per-tier budget is counted in; R2:M4), no enum. Input schema with `name` enum, `file`, `script`, `args`, `stdin`, `timeoutMs`.
- Re-rendered on every `tools/list` (cheap when cache keys match).
- Rows (30): A startup/flags (5): `A1 no-roots-empty-index`, `A2 root-group-writable-refused`, `A3 runner-url-without-token-refused`, `A4 max-description-range` (511 and 4097 refused, 512 and 4096 accepted; R2:M5), `A5 tools-list-one-tool`. B schema (8): `B1 unknown-top-key-tier-error`, `B2 version-not-1-tier-error`, `B3 unknown-entry-key-invalid`, `B4 scripts-missing-invalid`, `B5 duplicate-json-key-tier-error`, `B6 duplicate-name-both-invalid`, `B7 bad-name-invalid`, `B8 interpreter-unknown-invalid`. C frontmatter (6): `C1 exact-frontmatter-ok`, `C2 yaml-ish-extra-key-malformed`, `C3 name-mismatch`, `C4 description-mismatch`, `C5 description-too-long`, `C6 description-with-bidi-not-clean`. D rendering (8): `D1 preamble-under-300`, `D2 private-shadows-and-marked`, `D3 sorted-lines`, `D4 utf16-units-measured` (an astral-plane character counts 2 in the description AND in `budget_sum`), `D5 over-ceiling-refusal-names-both-sums` (both sums named in UTF-16 units), `D6 over-ceiling-tools-list-still-succeeds`, `D7 enum-matches-valid-names`, `D8 cache-invalidates-on-atomic-rename`. E dirs (3): `E1 dir-symlink-invalid`, `E2 dir-not-directory-invalid`, `E3 root-never-listed`.
**Pattern to follow**: P10, P13, P14; KD-11.
**Verification**: groups A-E red then green; `amalgamate.py --check` clean; `generated_region` 140 green (strict blocks called outside the regions, the one declared bare parse consumed, `window-region-has-a-caller` reached through `_ensure_dict(`, R3:M2); `forge_call describe` lists `mcp_skill`; **fleet green in the same commit (R3:M1)** at `read_loop` 55, `cancel` 92, `wire_log` 68, `handler_crash` 68, `protocol_version` 83, `py_deps` 63 (`DECLARED_POOL` 11, `DECLARED_KILL` 6, `DECLARED_REPLY_ONLY` 5, `DECLARED_W` 19, `DECLARED_D` 11, `DECLARED_FULL` 16); the second ADR 0008 `addendum.py` call exits 0.

### Step 18: mcp-skill — runner HTTP client and `run`
**Files**: `Scripts/mcp-skill.py` (`class RunnerClient`, `_read_token_file(path) -> str`, `fn_run`, `_collect_bundle(skill_fd) -> List[dict]`, `_sanitize_run_result(res: dict) -> dict`); `tests/test_mcp_skill.py` (groups I, J); `tests/files/mcp_skill/stub_runner.py` (create: stdio child behind a test `mcp-proxy --http`, canned results incl. hostile output); `tests/run.py` (update the `mcp_skill` typed count 54 -> 75 = + I11 + J10 and its ledger comment to the final group list; hunk-level, edit by content, PC-2; `tests/run.py:811` CASE COUNT DRIFT otherwise; R5:M1) (modify)
**Dependencies**: Steps 11, 15, 17
**Description**:
- `_read_token_file`: P10 logic (O_NOFOLLOW, fstat regular, owner euid, `mode & 0o077 == 0`, <= 4 KiB, one trailing newline stripped, printable ASCII, >= 32 chars), re-read at EVERY run; refusals name the rule only.
- `RunnerClient(url: str, token_file: str, timeout_s: float)`: URL check at startup (`urlsplit`; scheme `http`; `hostname` exactly `127.0.0.1` or `::1` — the bracketed `[::1]` spelling — and NEVER `localhost` or any other name, since a name's answer depends on the resolver (`/etc/hosts`, a search domain) and can point off-host (R2:S-L3, CWE-918); explicit port; non-empty path; no userinfo/query/fragment; else exit 2). Every response body (`application/json`, and each SSE `data:` payload) is parsed with `_strict_loads` (R2:L10). `_post(obj, sid)` with `http.client.HTTPConnection(host, port, timeout=30.0)`; headers in SDK order (P17); 3xx refused; `application/json` <= 32 MiB; `text/event-stream` line-wise with bounded `readline`, `:` comments reset idle, the event for our id returned; overall deadline `timeout_s`. `initialize()` -> `mcp-session-id` (`^[\x21-\x7e]{16,256}$`) then `notifications/initialized`. `call(arguments)`: one retry with a fresh session ONLY on HTTP 404. Session id under a lock; new connection per request. `close()` (C-M2 = S-M3): when a session id is held, one `DELETE` to the runner URL with `mcp-session-id` + bearer, timeout 2 s, every error swallowed (best effort, logged at debug as metadata only); called from mcp-skill's shutdown path (stdin EOF / SIGTERM handler) before exit. Sessions of a skill server that dies without it are reaped by the runner proxy's `--session-idle 900`.
- Request size (C-M2b = S-M4): `_encode_file(data: bytes) -> dict` picks, per file, the encoding whose JSON-SERIALIZED form is smaller: `utf8` only when `data` decodes strictly AND `len(json.dumps(text, ensure_ascii=False).encode("utf-8"))` is <= `len(json.dumps(base64_text))`; otherwise `base64` (control-heavy or `\u`-escaped text can serialize up to 6x its size; base64 is a fixed 4/3). `fn_run` then serializes the complete JSON-RPC request body exactly as `RunnerClient._post` will send it (`json.dumps(obj, separators=(",", ":"), ensure_ascii=False, allow_nan=False).encode("utf-8")` — the §5 "Strict JSON" spelling, one spelling everywhere, R4:L4) and, when `len(body) > --runner-max-body`, refuses with `invalid_params` "run request is N bytes, runner body cap M" BEFORE opening a connection; `_post` sends exactly those bytes.
- `fn_run(name, script, args?, stdin?, timeoutMs?)`: index refused -> isError; tier `private` without `--allow-private-scripts` -> `invalid_params` "private scripts are disabled"; `script` equals one `scripts[].path`; interpreter from the entry; `_collect_bundle`: fd-walk only `scripts/` and `refs/` of THIS skill dir, leaves matching `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9._-]{0,63}$`; anything else in those dirs (subdir, symlink, bad name) refuses the run naming the rule; <= 64 files, <= 4 MiB; per-file encoding by `_encode_file` (smaller serialized form); body-size check against `--runner-max-body`; call `sandbox_call` with `{"function": "run_script", "params": {...}}`.
- `_sanitize_run_result`: `structuredContent` (else parse the text; else isError `internal`); runner isError -> pass `error.code`/`message` sanitized; else stdout/stderr `_web_clean(.., "\t\n")`; out paths `_web_clean(.., "")`; utf8 contents sanitized; base64 files listed as metadata only; render a header line (exit/signal/timedOut/durationMs) + framed blocks (`kind=stdout|stderr|outfile`).
- Logging: function, skill, tier, interpreter, exit, duration; never token, bodies, args, stdin, output.
- Rows I client (11): `I1 url-non-loopback-refused` (`http://10.0.0.1:1/mcp`, `http://localhost:3010/mcp` and `http://127.0.0.2:3010/mcp` each exit 2; `http://127.0.0.1:3010/mcp` and `http://[::1]:3010/mcp` accepted; R2:S-L3), `I2 url-https-refused-v1`, `I3 token-file-symlink-refused`, `I4 token-file-mode-refused`, `I5 token-never-logged`, `I6 token-reread-each-run`, `I7 redirect-refused`, `I8 response-size-cap`, `I9 sse-keepalive-tolerated` (stub sleeps 20 s), `I10 session-404-retried-once`, `I11 session-deleted-on-shutdown` (after one `run`, closing mcp-skill's stdin makes the test proxy log/observe a `DELETE` for that session id and its session count drop to 0; C-M2 = S-M3). Rows J run (10): `J1 run-household-ok-end-to-end` (real proxy + real mcp-sandbox, macOS; INFO elsewhere and under a nested sandbox, P23 control first, R2:M8), `J2 unlisted-script-refused`, `J3 private-script-refused-without-flag`, `J4 shebang-and-mode-ignored`, `J5 refs-sent-with-scripts`, `J6 symlink-in-scripts-refuses-run`, `J7 hostile-output-sanitized`, `J8 busy-passed-through-iserror`, `J9 request-body-over-cap-refused-before-send` (`--runner-max-body 65536` and a 60 KiB file of `\x01` bytes: its utf8 JSON form would be ~360 KiB, its chosen base64 form ~80 KiB still exceeds the cap -> refused `invalid_params`, and the stub runner saw NO request), `J10 encoding-picks-smaller-serialized-form` (a control-heavy text file is sent as `base64`, a plain ASCII file as `utf8`; C-M2b = S-M4).
**Pattern to follow**: P10, P17 (rewritten with bounds), KD-10.
**Verification**: groups I/J green; `mcp_skill` typed count moved 54 -> 75 in this step's commit (planned 75 = A5 + B8 + C6 + D8 + E3 + F14 + G4 + H6 + I11 + J10; final at the red run; R5:M1).

R3:S-M1), Linux has no depth cap (M0:9), the bounded startup sweep and degraded retry with their arithmetic against the proxy `startup_timeout` and KD-17 (R3:M4), the single 2 s kill grace (R3:L5), a cancel never marking a refresh failed (R3:L4), T35 sysctl, T36 `/` listing, R-3 bash budget, NPROC starvation, Linux unverified), red counts.
- ADR 0032: four layers; the "Contract with ai-soul" items; runner behind a dedicated proxy; MCP HTTP client inside a server (hand-written, lift on a second host); the sanitizer as a declared hand copy with the WHOLE-source evidence and why S2 and the renamed copy were rejected; plan self-refresh (KD-13); same-uid trust (decision 4), household threat model = accidents, semantic injection declared; under the same-uid declaration (S-INFO): the runner token IS the boundary — any same-uid holder of the token file can call `run_script` directly and so bypasses mcp-skill's `--allow-private-scripts`; the timeout chain (KD-17) and the startup bound (95 s vs the runner proxy's `startup_timeout` 120, R3:M4), `params` accepted as a JSON-encoded object string through `_ensure_dict` like the rest of the fleet (R3:M2), the request-size rule (C-M2b) and the session `DELETE` + `--session-idle 900` (C-M2); the work-root lock (C-M1); Linux `liveVerified: false`; the loopback-literal-only runner URL (R2:S-L3); declared (R2:S-INFO): `capabilities` exposes the computed read set — absolute paths of interpreter kegs, framework images and `extra_reads` — to its caller; the caller is the trusted runner-proxy client (mcp-skill, or a same-uid holder of the runner token, which is already the boundary), so this is accepted, and mcp-skill never forwards `capabilities` to the model; the settled text-cleanliness contract (R4:CONTRACT: ai-soul refuses `UNSAFE_TEXT_RANGES` ∪ `/[\p{Cc}\p{Cf}\p{Cs}]/u` in every skill text at approve time, a superset of `_web_clean`'s drop set, so approve-time ⊆ load-time-clean) and its declared residual: Node ICU and Python `unicodedata` may sit on different Unicode versions, so a newly assigned code point can be classified differently on the two sides — if mcp-skill reports `description-not-clean` on an APPROVED skill, check that version skew first; the timeout chain's one per-run plan term (a refresh wait plus any own refresh share one `plan_deadline`, R4:M2); the omitted-`function` status reply of both dispatchers (R4:M1); Contract item 4 confirmed by ai-soul (2026-10-11) with ai-soul's narrower own bounds (<= 16 files, <= 262144 bytes per skill version, base64 only under `refs/`) making mcp-skill's 64 / 4 MiB a runner-side backstop (M0:12); **declared limit — hostile depth (M0:14)**: a deliberately hostile script can build roughly 1M directory levels in a 120 s run by rename-chaining (~11 ms per 99-level round; extrapolated, unmeasured past 4059), removal is linear at ~0.14 ms/level so its cleanup spills into quarantine over several runs and the 16-entry ceiling makes it visible as `degraded`; no background reaper (decided: the trigger is deliberate hostility of an approved skill only); untested: a cut through a `uchg` directory (the remover clears flags with `lchflags(0)` before the rename); red counts.
- Addenda (P25; each staged as `.claude/tmp/addenda/<adr>.json` `{"title", "body"}` with `purity_call create_text_file`, then `python3 ~/.claude/skills/p/skills/wiki/scripts/addendum.py --page 'docs/adr/<file>.md' --item-file '<staged .json>'`; a one-line title without backticks, a body with no level 1-2 heading):
  - ADR 0027 — title "relist: a child may opt into a fresh tools/list, its name set frozen": relist semantics, frozen name set, coalescing/min-interval (stamped per attempt, so failing relists are bounded too, R4:L3), stale-on-failure, `ChildSlot.stop()` cancels an in-flight relist (C-L7), no `list_changed` relay (Contract item 8).
  - ADR 0005 — title "restricted mode: reads, exec filter, clean env, supervised timeout, strict rlimits (ADR 0031)": the amended-by pointer to ADR 0031 and the one-paragraph summary of what the gate now auto-allows and what still prompts (`--net`, `--status-fd`, above-default rlimits).
- `project-forge.yaml` `all` target (R2:M7): after Steps 1-19 land, run the full fleet once through `forge_call test` (target `all`) and record the wall time; set `timeout` to `max(900, ceil(1.5 * measured / 60) * 60)` and rewrite the comment at :42-44 to the new measurement (suite counts and seconds). A new suite target whose own measured time exceeds 400 s gets its own `timeout` the same way.
- Roadmap: file the items of §5 "Deferred to roadmap" — 14 (M0:8: item 12 landed in v1, so it is not filed; items 14 and 15 were added at close-out on 2026-10-11), lane `next` for 1, 2 and 15; `later` for the rest, each naming its ADR.
**Pattern to follow**: `docs/adr/0030-point-at-the-near-miss-never-resolve-it.md` (decision, alternatives, gate and red run, declared limits); component pages `docs/components/mcp-proxy.md`, `docs/components/llm-router.md`; ADR 0022 single-writer roadmap.
**Verification**: each `addendum.py` call (0027 and 0005 here; the two 0008 calls were verified in Steps 7 and 16) exits 0 and prints the page path; against each ADR's working-tree copy taken just before the call (ADR 0005 already carries a user hunk, PC-2), the only change is an appended `## Addendum (<date>): ...` block (no existing byte changed), and the staged diff (`git diff --cached`) holds only that block; wiki reindex clean; `forge_call test` full fleet green inside the re-measured `all` timeout; `python3 Scripts/amalgamate.py --check` clean; `roadmap.py` exit 0.
